Before his feet could fully leave the roof, Spiderman’s arm had already flown out and grabbed onto Lando’s own, the tight grip keeping him hanging on the edge of the roof by the heels of his feet. He could briefly hear the sound of a web being shot and before Lando could register it, he was looking down to the side of him where he could see his camera wrapped up in spider webs and being pulled back up to the roof. 

“Now I’m wasting my webs on you.” Spiderman said, referring back to the comment he made earlier and Lando could practically feel the smirk radiating off him. 

“Well, your reaction times have gotten better, bravo. Really, well done.” Lando huffed and turned his head to look at Spiderman once again, he’d probably be a little more embarrassed about nearly falling again if he wasn’t still leaning backwards off the roof. If Spiderman let go now then he would certainly fall to a certain death. With this in mind, Lando managed to lift the arm held by Spiderman and grabbed onto the hero’s arm for extra caution. Staring at him now, he was covered by Lando’s own shadow, but the way the orange sky circled around them was somewhat fascinating. 

“You’re such a dickhead, your brain hasn’t gotten any better either, you’re still falling off roofs two years later!” The camera had now made its way back up, resting in the free hand of Spiderman.

“It’s not like I intentionally go to roofs just to fall off them, Spidey!” Lando furrowed his eyebrows and suddenly, he was pulled forward off the edge of the roof. He tumbled forward into Spiderman’s arms, instinct grabbed onto the suit now that he had no grip to keep him up. The hero shuffled them away from the edge, now standing safely away. He moved them back far enough so they were a safe distance away, acting as though Lando would make a break for the roof and launch himself off it. Lando still hadn’t let go and Spiderman hadn’t made a move to break the contact either. Suddenly, the embarrassment hit him like a train and he groaned loudly. “I can’t believe I just nearly fell off the fucking roof again.” 

“I mean I did tell you to stay away from them, pity you didn’t listen to me, mate.” Spiderman hummed. “Not the worst save I’ve had though, at least I don’t have to replace another thing of yours. I’d rate this one a 6/10, minus points for being boring and easy, bonus points since I don’t have to buy you another thing and you’re cute.” 

Lando will be going home later and screaming into his pillow for that. Cheers, Spidey. “And what would you rate the first one?” 

“The first one? Oh definitely a 10/10, you were very close to becoming a pancake, very entertaining, first time I met you and you were profusely thanking me whereas now I get nothing but attitude and sass from you.” 

“You deserve the sass, asshole.”Lando muttered and pursed his lips. “Well, thank you for saving me. Doesn’t change the fact you implied I couldn’t hunt for Doctor Octopus.” 

“I didn’t imply it, I said it.” 

“That’s even worse! ” Lando screeched. 

“No, it’s not.” Spiderman said sternly, his joking demeanor changing slightly. “He’s a really dangerous supervillain, he’s hurt a lot of people and he’s going to continue until I can stop him. He’s… his mind isn’t right. Anything could happen to you if I let you go near him, and I won’t let you near him. Of course, you could always not listen to me and go, but I’d rather you actually follow my advice on this one, I don’t want to have to save you a third time.” 

Lando ran his tongue over his teeth. He did have a point. He’d seen on the news the chaos the villain had caused across the city, maybe it was best that he didn't get himself involved with that after all. Doctor Octopus was a man gone mad. Lando had actually met him a few times since he was Oscar’s profession for the first two years of university, known as Dr Octavius. Oscar had commented a few times about how the professor was seeming weird, talking to himself during the lectures and overall just having a bad vibe around him. When he didn’t return for the third year on the first day, everyone had assumed he’d gotten a job elsewhere.

That was until Doctor Octopus actually ripped a hole open in the ceiling and screaming about needing to find Spiderman. He’d taken a few students with him seemingly to lure the hero, he eventually saved the day but Doctor Octopus had escaped. He’d caused a few more incidents since then but was now lying low. “Okay, fine, whatever! If I can’t go after him, what else can I do then?” 

He seemed to ponder for a second and his white eyes widened, it was like watching a lightbulb turn on above Spiderman’s head. “There’s another villain I’ve got for you, a lot less dangerous but I still need some research before I can go into it fully. He’s been…uh- causing a lot of trouble for the neighbourhoods! Think you can look into it for me?” 

The Brit nodded frantically, eager to help. “Sure, gimme all the details and I’ll get started as soon as I can. And once I do that, you’ll pose for some pictures for me, please?” Lando drawled out the last word annoyingly, now finally letting go of the hero and putting his hands in a prayer motion. 

“Yes, you’ll get your pictures. Don’t worry about the details for now, I’ll bring them to yours for you. If anything goes wrong or you need me at all, go on the Spidey crime watch app and just put in your name and location, I’ll be there as soon as I can, got it?” 

“Got it, thank you so much, Spidey. Although… I was actually going to send this to your email…”

“I don’t have an email, but go on.” Spiderman interrupted. 

“I’ve got a few suggestions on how you can make the app better, maybe I could help out with that too?” Lando twiddled with his thumbs. He knew it was a little much to offer, of course he wouldn’t want someone messing with his app, one of the most prominent ways he could receive alerts about the ongoing crimes. 

Spiderman shrugged. “I don’t see why not, just don’t break the app. If there was one person who could break it, I know for a fact it would be you.” He handed over the camera, in perfect condition (maybe it had a few sticky bits where the webs had been, but better this than broken) and Lando practically hugged it to his chest. 

“You won’t regret this, honestly! Fuck yes, I’m Spiderman’s assistant!” 

“Are you fuck? You’re a pain in my ass.” 

“You wish I was a pain in your ass.” Lando let out a wild cackle, having to stop himself from gasping out loud at his own comment. 

“Oh my fucking god, you’re a nightmare, mate.” Spiderman groaned and facepalmed. He made his way back to the edge of the roof, this time sitting before it. He lightly tapped the ground next to him. “Now, why don’t we watch the rest of the sunset together? That is why I came here, after all.” 

It was getting darker, which meant it wouldn’t be long until Spiderman left to go continue with his duties. With that in mind and wanting to make the most of the time with him, Lando hastily made his way to Spiderman, slotting himself down right next to the hero. The orange skyline was fading rather quickly, it was a shame that they had missed so much of it already. Maybe Lando would need to come back soon, so he could actually sit with him peacefully and take in the scenery. 

A silence fell over them as they watched the bustling city, the sound of horns and chatter of people below becoming distant in his ears. It was relaxing, to sit here with someone and just enjoy what was around them. The orange in the sky had long faded away, the moon slowly starting to make its appearance and a blue was creeping in. Lando really didn’t want this to end so soon. 

He spoke up softly, almost worried that speaking up would remind Spiderman that the sunset was fading away. “I noticed something, Spidey.”

“Very observant of you, Lando.” Spiderman retorted.

Lando went to whack him before realising that this wasn’t Oscar next to him and he couldn’t just randomly hit people when they gave him some sass back. He cleared his throat and lowered his arm back to his lap. “Your suit. You had a different one when we met, this one’s a lot more… expensive.” 

“I’d have probably said better, but whatever adjective works for you, mate.” Spiderman said. “Nice hoodie, by the way. Where’d you get it?” Lando could’ve sworn he heard a slight glint of recognition, as if he already knew. 

“It’s not mine… I’d never buy such a plain hoodie! No logo, no design, nothing! It’s my best friend's hoodie. Anyway, don’t change the subject! Tell me more about the suit, I’m quite interested now.” 

“Well, tell your roommate it’s a really nice hoodie.” Spiderman hummed before extending his arms, allowing Lando to have an up close view of the details in the suit. “Got some nice upgrades over the years, much better than the homemade one I used to wear. Got a built in digital analysis tool and it just looks better. Look, even my web-shooters were upgraded.” He revealed the palm of his hand where the gadget rested, pressing his two middle fingers down to the button and watching as a stray web shot out. The web flew over the edge of the roof, disappearing below them. Lando takes back the tech-savvy comment, he had no fucking clue what kind of gadget he was looking at. 

“How could you even afford that? I mean, do you have a day job? Aren’t you too busy saving everyone for that? I mean, I’ve just assumed you’re a similar age to me but for all I know you’re a rich middle aged man!” Lando reeled himself in once he saw himself going on a little bit of a tangent, a sour face being made once he realised he could be obsessing and blushing over a 50 year old man. 

“I can’t reveal too much about myself, kinda defeats the point of the mask then.” Lando rolled his eyes and huffed to himself. “But to answer your questions, yes I’m a similar age to you and yes I’ve got a day job. Sort of. But I got all this tech from Iron Man and his company Webber Industries. Heard of him?” Spiderman asked smugly, like he knew that it was clearly someone Lando had heard of. 

“Mark Webber?” He gasped. He didn’t bother voicing his relief at knowing they’re at least a similar age, he probably had already sensed the panic radiating off him beforehand.

Spiderman nodded. “Mark Webber.” His accent seemed thick saying the last name, Lando really wished he could work out what it was. 

“Holy shit, dude. You’re so lucky, when I was a kid I always wanted to meet Iron Man. He’s always off in America now with the Avengers.” He grumbled. 

Mark Webber, genius, billionaire, playboy, philanthropist. Lando was very young when Iron Man first made his superhero appearance and graced the world, not long after he revealed his identity as the one and only Mark Webber. He was previously dubbed as the United Kingdom’s main superhero, until the Avengers, earth's mightiest heroes, were formed and he swiftly packed up and left for the United States. He still came back from time to time, could sometimes be seen back in the city or flying around in his suit, but Spiderman was the city’s hero now. 

Spiderman crossed his legs and rested his elbows on them, his hands holding up his head. “Tell me about it. He’s been kinda my mentor since I started this whole thing, now I barely get to see him. I mean, good for him that he’s in the Avengers and all, but it would be nice to see him in person rather than a digital projection of him.” 

“That must suck, I’m sorry to hear that.” 

“You’re so good with words, has anyone ever told you to become a therapist?”

“Shut the fuck up, I’ll leave now. I know for a fact you’re enjoying my company.” Lando crossed his arms over his chest and raised an eyebrow at the hero. “Come on, Spidey. Admit it!”

“Maybe I’ve enjoyed it a little bit, not a lot. But a little bit.” He teased lightly. “It’s fine though, he’ll be home soon. Besides, it means I get to help protect the streets without him watching over me like a protective dad.” 

“My best friend, the one whose hoodie I’m wearing, he actually works at Webber Industries. He said he’d get me an autograph from Mark next time he sees him. He’s doing an internship there, you may have seen him around! His name is Oscar Piastri, have you heard of him?” Lando was fully aware of the dreamy, smitten tone he had whenever he said Oscar’s name and he was begging that it hadn’t come off as a teenager with a crush. 

Spiderman tensed, although Lando hadn’t noticed. “Ah- haha, no I’m sure I haven’t. You know, there’s so many people who work there, we probably just haven’t crossed paths! Haha… yeah.” Spiderman looked to his side away from Lando and pulled out his phone. “Oh, would you look at that? It’s time for me to go!” Lando could be a little stupid but he wasn’t as oblivious as everyone thought, he could hear the panic in his voice. 

“Wait!” Lando interrupted as he watched Spiderman scrambling off the floor and dusting his suit off. He quickly followed suit, the sunset now gone, no longer did the orange cast glimmer on them. Instead, darkness began to settle and the moon reflected the pale light. Lando frowned to himself, the sunset disappearing just reminded him of the sudden change of mood in the conversation. “Why are you leaving so suddenly? Have I done something wrong? I’m really sorry if I’ve done anything.” 

“No- no, you haven’t done anything, I promise. I’ve just had an alert on my phone, duty calls.” Spiderman reassured and showed Lando his phone, proving that a message had popped up with a location for him to attend. “I’m sorry to leave so suddenly.” 

Lando felt his nerves ease, tension he didn’t know was building had left his body. “Like you said, duty calls. Just make sure you bring those details for me soon, alright Spidey?” 

“You’ve got it, mate! Hey, don’t be a stranger to joining me at sunset. I guess I enjoyed your company more than I let on!” Spiderman said before jumping on the edge of the roof and promptly front flipping off it (show off), Lando watched as his webs stuck to the tall buildings and he weaved his way through them like he knew the back of his hand.

He quickly lifted his camera up, capturing a mid air shot of Spiderman and a web flying towards a building. It wasn’t his best shot, but it’ll do. He’ll save posting until he gets the other shots to go alongside it. Until then, his job is to now investigate this criminal and fix that buggy thing that Spiderman somehow calls an app. 

God, he can’t wait to go home and tell Oscar all about this.

Chapter End Notes

Hope you enjoyed this chapter!

Chapter 3: Chapter 3

Chapter Summary

Oscar buys a nice outfit and Lando finally gets to be Spiderman’s assistant.

Chapter Notes

Hope you guys enjoy this one… I’m finally catching up to a point where I’ve written (I’ve got another 15k left and more to go lol I’m being dramatic)

See the end of the chapter for more notes

“-and then he grabbed me before I could fall off the roof, he let me hold him after he saved me! And he was telling me about Iron Man. I probably have a better chance meeting Mark Webber through him than I do you! Mate, I’m even gonna be his assistant!” 

“Assistant?” Oscar asked, not looking up from the hoodie he was eying in the shop. His hands shuffled through the rack of hoodies as he tried searching for his size. He was looking at a plain navy one, Lando really wished he’d go for something a bit more colourful and lively. “Are you sure it was him agreeing for you to be his assistant or just him trying to please you?” 

Lando rolled his eyes and lightly pushed Oscar’s shoulder, the boy not budging and still focused on finding his size. “Okay, so neither! He said I could take some pictures of him if I did him a favour.” 

“And so now you’re stuck stalking a villain and fixing his app for him?” Oscar deadpanned, finally finding his size in the hoodie and adding it to the rest of the clothes he’d gathered. 

“Oscar.” He whined, drawling out the word.

“Lando.” The Aussie responded in a similar tone mockingly.

“Quit being so negative! Just be happy for me, I get to spend time with Spiderman!” Lando practically squealed. 

Lando had woken up the morning after his meeting with Spiderman, a flutter in his stomach and a skip in his step. As he had walked into their living room, he could see a piece of paper stuck to the window from the outside with paragraphs of writing on it. It contained the information regarding the villain he had to investigate and a guide on how to access the Spiderman crime watches app settings to fix it. He had already spent the first half of the morning working out some of the features he could add and making a to-do list for his research, after that he had gone to his only lecture of the day. 

As soon as Oscar had walked through the door after they both returned from their lectures, Lando had bombarded him with loud noises about Spiderman being this- Spiderman being that. Spiderman, Spiderman, Spiderman. While Lando kept on, the Aussie walked around doing a few chores and patiently listened to his friend. They were the definition of always talking, always listening. 

In between his continuous ramblings, Oscar had broken through the conversation and suggested going to the shopping centre to grab some more clothes, with Lando agreeing before hurriedly returning to his previous tangent.

Oscar made his way towards the till, weaving wistfully through the array of people rushing to do their Christmas shopping. Lando eagerly followed after him, watching the way Oscar’s body moved lightly and with ease as he had to squeeze and fight for his life pushing through everyone. By the time they reached the queue, the Australian had been standing there for around 30 seconds while Lando came out with messy hair and breathing heavily like he had been caught in a fight.

The younger boy smiled in amusement, eying Lando up, taking in his appearance. Lando felt naked under his eye and felt like he needed to cover up his bits. He pushed Oscar’s shoulder playfully. “Damn you and your fucking fast pace today, since when were you Sonic? You know I get lost in crowds easily, you can’t leave me behind!” 

“We both know you would usually strut through this place like you own it.” Which was true, Lando did have a little bit of a complex about him where he walked into every place like he could tear it down, a little sass in his walk. Usually Oscar followed after him politely waving at everyone, trying to keep up like a lost puppy. “Not my fault you fell behind. Wanted to join in with the panicked mums, did you?” 

“Yeah, you know, I had to help make sure they get their playdough and crayons that’ll be either eaten or demolished within a week.” Lando’s grin pulled to the side and he used his Oscar’s shoulder as an armrest, having to stretch a little bit as the Aussie was a little taller than him. He crossed his legs over and leaned in towards Oscar. “I could be Santa’s little helper, you know.” Or Spiderman’s little helper, he briefly thought. 

Oscar stepped forward as the line moved, chasing Lando to stumble a little before the Aussie grabbed his arm gently and pulled him along too. Once his hand dropped off his wrist, Lando could feel the heat left behind and goosebumps trailed along his arm. “First off, playdough looks very edible so I wouldn’t blame them and secondly, we’re in a H&M, not the North Pole.” 

“So no playdough and crayons?” Lando pouted. 

“No playdough and crayons.” Oscar repeated, heading towards the queue as a worker shouted ‘next’. The British boy scurried after him. Oscar paid for his items and they walked out of the shop together, into the wilderness of the shopping centre that seemed even busier than it did 20 minutes ago. 

He checked his phone briefly for the time before looking at the shop ahead of them. Charles had finally booked the venue and sorted the dates out. It had worked out that most people could attend on the 22nd December, since a lot of people were heading home for the holidays and while the first term of university ends on the 15th, they still had their jobs that didn’t end until then. Charles had gone above and beyond this year, booking out a gorgeous venue with a proper DJ, lights, buffet, the lot. He even sent out card invitations (hand delivered by Max) saying something along the lines of ‘you are cordially invited to the Leclerc annual Christmas Party’ (which Lando had snorted at, since George actually had started the tradition). 

The Monegasque had already told him not to allow Oscar to show up in his joggers and a hoodie, meaning the two had to actually dress up quite nicely for this. For Lando, that was no issue. For Oscar? Yes, there was an issue. Lando was determined to make sure everyone could view his full beauty the way he could. 

“Have you thought about what you’re gonna wear to the party?” Lando subtly dropped, slowly walking towards the entrance of the other shop, they were bound to have some nice clothes in there.

Oscar raised his eyebrow as his eyes followed Lando, who was walking backwards towards the shop ever so slightly as if he wouldn’t notice. “What are you hinting at?”

“I’m not hinting at anything, Oscar!”

“Then why are you creeping towards that expensive shop?” 

“I’m not!” Oscar stared at him blankly, zeroing in on him and not budging from where he was standing. “Okay maybe I am?” He sheepishly admitted.

A sigh heaved out, like he was going to regret asking. “What do you want, Lando?” 

“Right well, I thought maybe we could dress you up this year? You know, instead of wearing a hoodie and your joggers?” 

Oscar frowned and looked down at himself, wearing exactly what Lando had just said. “What’s wrong with my outfit?” 

“Nothing! Absolutely nothing! It’s lovely, mate. In fact, I love seeing you in them everyday, you look fit in them!” He truly could have smacked himself for that, especially when he saw a glimmer of entertainment in Oscar’s eyes. “I- I mean, it really suits you and I’m glad you’re comfortable in them but I think you’d look great in something a little more out there! ” 

“Dude, calm yourself.” 

He couldn’t calm down after he just admitted that Oscar looked great in such a mediocre outfit. Lando had used the word fit to describe him, if anyone else were around to hear it, he’d never live it down. He took a deep breath and got himself in the zone. “Oscar Piastri, will you do me the honour of letting me choose your outfit for the Christmas party?” Lando asked, immediately realising it kind of sounded like a proposal. 

The Aussie rolled his eyes and walked towards Lando. “Lead the way then.” He gestured for the boy to move along. 

“Yes!” Lando exclaimed and made a beeline for the shop, grabbing what he thought was Oscar’s arm but ended up being his hand. The younger boy took it with no complaints, thank god he couldn’t see Lando’s face right now, otherwise all he would see is beetroot red cheeks. 

They made their way into the dimly lit shop with a strong cologne sifting in the air. The two browsed (read: Lando browsed and Oscar followed) and Lando kept pointing out all these clothing bits, to which Oscar would just shrug his shoulders and say ‘you choose, mate’ . The weight of the Aussie’s hand had grown heavy once he remembered their hands were still intertwined, but Oscar hadn’t made a move to detach them so he wasn’t about to either. 

Once Lando had chosen a few good shirts and some trousers to go with it, he tugged Oscar along to the changing rooms. He peered into one of the changing rooms like a meerkat before shoving the Aussie in the room and handing the first outfit. “Now, try them on please. We’ll look at this outfit first!” 

Oscar whined and threw his head back and my god did it do things to Lando. He blinked rapidly as he tried to burrow the sound away and sight away. Out of sight, out of mind, he thought as he quickly drew the changing room curtain shut between them before Oscar could see the alarmed look on his face. 

He tapped his foot impatiently as he waited for Oscar to change. It felt like forever before the curtain opened again and the Australian stood there with dead eyes at his friend. “This is abysmal, Lando.” He deadpanned.

“It’s chic! Look in the mirror, you look great!” Lando grabbed his arms and pulled him out of the changing room. He turned Oscar towards the mirror and stifled a laugh. Okay, this one might have just been to laugh at. Lando had given up a button up Hawaiian shirt a tourist would wear along with red khaki pants. “Just… just absolutely fabulous, Oscar.” His laugh had slipped through a little while saying his name, having to pull a face to start the giggles from coming through anymore. 

“I can’t believe I agreed to try on clothes and you’ve given me a Leclerc outfit.” Oscar looked at himself in the mirror and made a fake gag, retching. Lando couldn’t contain his laughter anymore and let out a booming laugh. He quickly whipped out his phone and snatched a picture of it before Oscar’s insane reflexes could stop him. “Oh my god, delete that!” 

Lando grinned widely. “It’s already posted on Instagram now. Right, go try on the second outfit now!” Oscar didn’t need to know right this second that he had quoted him directly, meaning Charles was bound to message either of them soon claiming he was a fashion connoisseur. Honestly? Lando has never seen such a badly dressed couple with him and Max. How people can ever be that tasteless with fashion, Lando will never understand. 

Oscar begrudgingly made his way back into the changing room, snatching the second outfit that Lando held out for him. The curtain closed behind him, now it was another waiting game. Lando suddenly remembered something he had also meant to tell Oscar. “Oscar, you’ll never guess what!”

“If this is about Spiderman again, I’m gonna come out of this changing room and strangle you with those red pants.” 

“What if it’s about Spiderman and Mark Webber?” Lando asked in a sing-songy voice. 

There was a beat of silence before Oscar said, “Go on.” 

“Knew that would grab your attention! Well anyway, when I spoke to Spiderman, I asked him about his suit, right? It looked amazing! It had all these cool gadgets and his eyes moved! He was telling me how he can get all his suits and gadgets from Mark Webber, fucking Iron Man!”

“Lando,” Oscar started. “Do you remember I have an internship there?” 

He rolled his eyes, despite Oscar still being behind the curtain and not being able to see it. “Of course I do, you muppet. But you don’t actually get to see him now, do you? God, Spiderman gets to work with Mark and gets to use all his cool shit to make even more cool shit. Imagine if I met Iron Man through Spiderman, Oscar? I could die peacefully then. Maybe he’d let me try a suit on! Maybe-“ As Lando continued to ramble on, he hadn’t noticed that the Aussie had drawn the curtain back, dressed in the new outfit. 

While he was off on his usual tangent, Oscar watched him as he rambled on about his fantasies of even being near the two heroes. He didn’t notice that when Oscar had drawn the curtain that he had an annoyed expression plastered on his face, that his gaze softened when the adoration shined on Lando’s face. He missed the way that affection and confliction intertwined in his irises, dancing a battle between each other. The way a soft smile played on Oscar’s lips. With Lando lost in his narrative, of course he’d never notice. 

“-right, Oscar?” 

Oscar blinked before snapping out his trance, Lando now looking at him in excitement. “Uh- yes?”

“You weren’t listening, were you?”

“No mate, not even a little bit.” Lando exhaled dramatically in feigned irritation before taking a proper look at the second outfit. 

Woah. 

Just… woah.

Lando was envious of how effortlessly attractive he looked, but also so so so infatuated with him. Oscar always looked great even in just his joggers and hoodies he always dawned, but to see him in a pair of loose cargo trousers and a white T-shirt with what looked like sticker designs plastered around the shirt? It was doing things to him. It was unfair, he was barely dressed up, in fact it was just casual wear! He really wished Oscar dressed more like this all so he could sit there and oogle at him. 

“Wipe the drool of your chin and close your mouth, anyone would think you’d just seen a stroopwafel.”

And now he was just embarrassed. He wiped at his chin quickly, hearing the laugh of Oscar quietly while his hand came away dry. “Shut the fuck up. This is the one.” 

Oscar took a look at himself in the mirror before pulling a face of approval. “I gotta say, even I quite like this.” 

“Does this mean you’ll start ditching the joggers and hoodies when we go out?” Lando asked hopefully. 

Oscar looked at Lando over his shoulder. “Not a chance.” 

“Why not? You could even wear my own clothes!” 

“We both know I couldn’t get your cargos on. Joggers? Yes. Tight trousers or cargos? Probably not.” 

Don’t think about his thighs. Don’t think about his thighs. Don’t think about his thighs. 

“Guess I know what you’re getting for Christmas then! Come on, my treat today!” Lando twirled Oscar by the shoulders before he could see the look of fluster on his face, forcing him back into the changing room, yanking the curtain shut. “I honestly can’t wait for this party, we’re gonna look so good. We definitely need to take some pictures with Max and Charles, we’ll look like goddamn models next to them.”

“Not like it’s difficult really.” 

“I mean, it is when you got the Leclerc face card. We’re lucky his double denim outfits bring him down a notch.” Oscar let out a quiet laugh at that. Lando let some time pass before he let his thoughts run out his mouth. “You’ll definitely be there for the party, right?” His heart seemed to hang on, waiting for a response. 

A beat of silence fell and for a moment, he thought he’d hear every excuse Oscar could find to not go. The curtain opened and Oscar walked out in his normal outfit, stopping just a foot in front of the British boy. Their eyes locked in a gentle gaze Oscar spoke with a steady voice. “Lando, I promise you I’ll be at the party.” 

Lando, still harboring a hint of doubt towards the promise, questioned it further. “Do you mean it? No disappearing acts? You can’t just up and leave like usual, I want you there by my side. We arrive together, we leave together.” 

And Oscar, he was always floating between easy and difficult to read. Sometimes Lando could read him like an open book and other times he felt like a safe that he couldn’t unlock. With that in mind, he could see the hesitation in Oscar’s face, a fleeting shadow in his eyes and almost gave up even hoping. “I promise you, no disappearing. We arrive together, we leave together.” He confirmed. 

That was enough for Lando. To even have him promise was a good enough sign, as he’d always say that he can’t guarantee it, that if he had to leave he would. “Okay. Okay, great.” He nodded, belief casing his voice. “Now, let’s get out of here, we gotta watch Tyrion and Daenerys meet!”

*

Lando looked out his window, sunset had long passed and there had been no sign of Spiderman. No matter, he had his own task to get up to today. 

The whole day he’d been working on changing some features to the Spiderman crime watch app. The app was an easy way to report crimes directly to Spiderman, allowing you to share details on the crime and who was reporting it. It was very mediocre and Lando hadn’t been happy with it. The first thing he did was allow people to share their locations from their device, not everyone knows the streets they’re on! Someone could also be kidnapped and currently in a moving vehicle, sharing a live location will allow you to keep track of them no matter what. 

The next change he added was priority, allowing people to mark their report as a low, medium, or emergency. Lando imagined emergency would be used the most, but he imagined something that was medium could be someone’s cat getting stuck in a tree that perhaps firefighters could help handle instead. By adding the feature, it made sure that Spiderman was attending to the utmost urgent of crime scenes and utilise his time better. 

Thirdly, he made it so it could be accessible online rather than only being an app. By the time someone had downloaded it and made the reports it could already be too late. He had a few more ideas in mind, but they were the most important ones to complete. 

By completing this, he was now onto his next task. To investigate a villain called Professor Diabolical. Lando thought it was quite a lame name, but he wasn’t about to go insulting this villain to his face should they come eye to eye. He received a few bits of the information he needed on the letter received from Spiderman, which read as: 

‘Dear Lando,

Here’s the information on the villain Professor Diabolical. He’s been at large now for the past 3 weeks, apparently he has a team of people sourcing/ creating illegal weapons and selling them on to criminals, causing chaos in London. 

If my intel is correct, Professor Diabolical will be near Junction Street alleyway by the old abandoned factory this Thursday at 9pm, he’s supposedly meeting who’s interested in buying the weapons. I’d go myself, but my focus is on finding Doc Ock right now. 

Don’t be a hero, I just need you to investigate and let me know about anything you find. If anything goes wrong, remember to report it on the app and I’ll be there as soon as I can. 

From your friendly-neighbourhood Spiderman

P.S to make changes to the app, go to http://spideycrimewatch.co.uk/spideyhrtc, work your magic.

Spiderman had done the hard part for him, now all he needed to do was go there himself and investigate it. He tried looking this Professor Diabolical up online, searching for any additional information he may need, but there was nothing on him. Not a single thing. No news articles, no tweets, no posts. Nothing. The closest thing he could find to it was Professor Chaos from South Park, Lando wondered if they took inspiration from that. 

Oh well, he had his game plan, he didn’t need much information to do his job. He was going to check out the abandoned factory, take some pictures of the production lines and weapons if they were still in the building, meaning he needed to get there earlier than the meeting time. He also needed to work out a good vantage point, somewhere he wouldn’t be caught but somewhere he could still see and hear the exchange. 

As the impending thought of the 30 minute walk and the how long the rest of the tasks would take settled in his mind, his sense of urgency heightened and realised he should probably get a move on. He swiftly dressed himself, overly eager to start his journey for the evening and made a beeline for the door. However, as his hand touched the doorknob, he quickly remembered that Oscar was home and his gaze traveled to the living room. 

There Oscar was sprawled out on the sofa, his usually wavy styled hair was a mess across his forehead and framed his soft features. His one leg was dangling over the edge of the sofa, the other one up closer by him. His eyes were screwed shut but Lando could tell he wasn’t asleep, probably very close to though. Lando’s hand slipped from the doorknob, allowing himself to take the careful and quiet steps towards Oscar. 

“Oscar?” He called out as he lowered himself to the floor onto his knees, his head close to Oscar’s own. “I’m going now, okay? I’m not sure what time I’ll be back yet, I’ll give you a text once I’m on my way though.” 

The Aussie’s eye pried open and he slightly shifted to look at Lando. “Hi.” He said groggily. 

Lando left out a soft giggle and moved a few strands out of Oscar’s face. He quickly retracted his hand as he noticed what he was doing, but Oscar seemed to have not minded. Either that or he was just too tired to care. “Hi. Did you hear what I said?” 

“Just about… you’re going to see that villain now?” 

Lando nodded with excitement. “Yeah, Professor Diabolical.” 

“What a fucking atrocious name, borderline illegal to have that name.” 

“Tell me about it, mate.” Lando rolled his eyes and rested his hand on Oscar’s arm. “It’s like a thirty minute walk.” 

“Are you sure you really wanna go do this? I’m sure Spiderman wouldn’t mind if you wanna back out, he’d probably let you take the pictures anyway.” 

Lando shook his head. He made a deal with Spiderman, he wasn’t about to back down now. Plus, he was actually really excited to go investigate, he was excited to feel a sense of purpose and be able to help the hero. “Listen, I agreed to be his assistant, I’m fulfilling that duty.” 

“You’re not his assistant.” 

“His partner in crime then.” 

“Crime?” Oscar was fully looking at him now, his arm that wasn’t being held by Lando’s hand was resting behind his head, his shirt riding up a little and exposing his torso. God, how can he just sit there looking like that at Lando and expect him to just be fine. 

Suddenly feeling nervous under the sharp gaze of Oscar and feeling flustered looking at his arms and waist, he started to sputter. “I- uh- we- we’re thick as thieves?” It came out as a question and Lando pulled a face at his own answer. 

“I’m begging you don’t say that to a superhero.” He said groggily. 

“Wouldn't dream of it.” Lando tore his eyes away from Oscar before he could say anything else even more stupid. “See you later then, if you’re home.” 

“Lando, be careful, will you? I don’t want Spiderman at our door bringing you home half dead.”  

“I’ll be fine!” He brushed off the concern and pulled himself up, making his way towards the door again. Lando looked over his shoulder to Oscar, who had now sat up straight and was stretching out his limbs. “And by the way, go to your actual bed and sleep. I know for a fact you’ll only complain about your back tomorrow.” 

And with that, Lando walked out the door before Oscar could protest anymore about his little investigation. But as he walked out, he missed how Oscar had sprung up from the sofa with all the energy in the world. 

As he embarked on the thirty minute journey, the cool breeze of the winter night brushed harshly against his cheeks. The dark of the night enveloped the city and left an odd atmosphere on the streets. It was surprisingly very quiet which unnerved Lando slightly. He buried his hands in the pocket of his coat, silently thanking himself for throwing a hoodie underneath it too. He pulled the wooly hood of the jumper up and started to walk faster, bumping the blood in his body. 

Once he was at the alleyway, he took his headphones off and left them to rest around his neck, turning the music off. He peered into the space before walking towards it, careful to not step on the icy patches. 

Here it was: the abandoned factory that apparently wasn’t so abandoned. Lando scanned the area for any signs of anyone being there, the last thing he wanted right now was to be caught instantly. Once he was sure no one was outside, he took a look at the factory. Some of the windows were smashed, the walls had moss growing on them and overall it looked like it hadn’t been touched in twenty years. 

Lando crept towards the rusty entrance of the building, the door opening with ease and creaking as he pushed it further. His eyes drifted around the place, looking for any signs of people inside or anything worth noting. There were puddles of water scattered throughout the place, probably from the broken windows and the droplets falling from the caving-in roof, creating an echoing effect as they fell. The moonlight trickled through the broken ceiling, casting eerie shadows on the factory machinery. He pulled out his phone and turned the flash on, cursing himself for not thinking far enough ahead that he would need a flashlight. The light emitted barely did anything for him, but it was better than nothing. 

The walls were coated in spray painted symbols, some saying curse words, one of them listing someone’s Instagram handle and another was drawing of a beautiful flower. Empty spray paint cans were on the floor, the colours of the cans eroding and sinking into the wet floor around it. 

He carefully moved through the factory, his footsteps in the puddles echoing against the walls, a reminder of the lack of life in the building. Once he reached the old broken machinery, he searched for any sign of the weapons or any sign at all that someone had been there. God, it really did seem like no one had been there in years, surely no criminal mastermind was actually working from this place? It was falling apart from both the inside and outside and overall the place was unsafe. Although, he did doubt that the villain was doing any risk assessments on the place. 

Lando’s pulse quickened as he advanced down a dimly lit corridor, the moonlight’s gaze barely peeking through now. The thrill of the investigation amplified the pounding beats of his chest, adrenaline coursing through his blood as he was determined to undercover what was really going on here. Amidst the thrill however, a sense of dread had entered his body the minute he had noticed there were no signs of life within the building. The fear held a tight grip on him, reminding him that anyone or anything could be lurking within the darkness, within the corners of the building, waiting for him. 

Bang!

His body instinctively turned itself towards the noise, his breath hitching as his light shined into the distance. Upon closer inspection, he could see a rat scurrying away from a blank of wood that had fallen on the floor. He urged himself to move forward and briefly thought that perhaps he had bitten off more than he could chew, that maybe he wasn’t actually cut out for this investigating crap. 

After ten more minutes of roaming around the place, there were no signs of anyone even entering the building or anything at all being stored there. With a huff of disappointment (but a silent thank you from his body for finally calming down), he retreated back on himself and made his way out of the building. There was no way in hell he was staying in there any longer than he needed to. 

Once he was safely out of the building and could see the dim glow of the street lamps, he turned his flashlight off and checked the time. It was currently 8:10pm, meaning he had about forty minutes until Professor Diabolical was supposed to show. 

Which is why Lando was so shocked when he saw someone walking down the alleyway with a white mask and a bat dragging along the floor behind him. 

Lando had always thought that while he might be a little stupid at times and clumsy, he at least had quick reactions (maybe not as quick as Oscar, sure) and had a little bit of survival instinct within him. That was all thrown out the window when he saw the masked criminal walking towards him menacingly. 

The man stalking towards him had a swagger to his walk with arrogance oozing off him, his one hand gripping the wooden bat loosely as it tumbled against the floor, creating an irking noise and his other tucked into his trouser pockets. He was wearing a white button-up shirt with the sleeves meticulously rolled up, revealing his forearms and a black waistcoat over the top, hugging his lean frame. The ghostly white mask looked like it was picked up from a Halloween shop, which was probably on sale considering they were near enough in December now. He could see tufts of blonde hair peeking over the top, but nothing else gave away the appearance of the man behind the mask. 

Lando’s breaths grew shallow, the realisation dawning on him that the approaching figure must be the man he was investigating. The man who had been causing chaos , the man he needed to help put a stop to. 

Professor Diabolical.  

“Well, well, well, what do we have here?” The voice called out menacingly, drawing closer to Lando’s frozen body. God, he really wished he hadn’t wasted so much time in the factory, he would’ve had time to hide. Or maybe he could’ve shown up earlier, he didn’t exactly expect the villain to show up so early. The villain had a little skip to his step, the bat now lifted from the floor and being swayed left to right. “It seems I’ve got myself a little lurker. Care to tell me why you’re here before I bash your brains out?” The taunting challenge hung in the air like a sinister omen. 

Lando nearly whined, a villain was getting closer and closer to him and all he could do was stand there like a blubbering fool, unable to do anything. His muscles tensed and his breath was stuck in his throat as it dawned on him how vulnerable he truly was right now. He didn’t have time to contact Spiderman with the villain getting closer, he didn’t have an ounce of fight in him and he was frozen to the spot. His mind was racing with desperate thoughts as he tried to think of some solution, for some escape route. 

“W-who are you?” Lando managed to choke out. 

The villain tilted his head to the side and laughed. “Oh don’t you know? I’m Professor Diabolical. You should know that, you were sent here to spy on me.” 

“Honestly you’re such a dumbass, did you come up with it on your own?” Lando snorted, feigning fake confidence. Maybe if he could throw him off a little, he could stall for some time. “Did you really sit down and think Professor Diabolical sounded cool? Like at all?” 

Professor Diabolical stopped with his foot in the air as he went to take his stop, his head snapping up to stare at Lando. “Are you really insulting me when I’m here with a bat in my hand?” 

“Depends, do you even know how to swing that bat?” He sassed. Lando briefly noted that he could hear a very strong American accent coming through. How he hadn’t noticed that before beats him, but he was confused by the familiarity of it. 

“Course I do, asshole.” To prove his point, the villain swung the bat around before it ended up flying from his grip and smashing into the wall of the abandoned factory. Lando’s eyes followed the bat as it soared through the air and slowly turned to look back at Professor Diabolical, who in turn was standing there looking quite sheepish and his hand rubbing the back of his neck. “I meant to do that.” 

“Who the fuck are you really?” 

“I told you, I’m the great Professor Diabolical! Here to wreck havoc on your city and uh- sell weapons to the low life criminals here!” His voice sounded unsure, like he was reading it from a script and couldn’t believe what he was saying. 

“Oh my god…” Lando muttered out loud with his voice wavering with astonishment, almost smacking himself in the face once he realised who it actually was. The accent should’ve given it away, but his fear-stricken daze had prevented him from clocking onto it. He felt like he was in an episode of Scooby-Doo where it was so painfully obvious who it was but he had to dance around it a little to find out. “Logan?” He blurted out, exasperation seeping through.

“Uh- who? Who’s that?” He squeaked out. Gone was the cold and menacing exterior of the 

“You’re un-fucking-believable mate. You’ve turned to a life of villainy?” Lando couldn’t believe what he was seeing. Here was Logan, Oscar’s best friend, his enemy, turning to a life of crime. If he didn’t  hate him before, he certainly hates him now. “How do you think Oscar would feel if he found out about this? One of his closest friends is a villain selling illegal weapons?” He asked, disbelief coating his tone. 

The man tore the white mask off his face, throwing it to the side and low and behold, it was Logan! His eyes were wide like a deer caught in headlights and he was frantically shaking his hands. “This isn’t what it looks like!” 

“Oh yeah? Then what is it? Because I was told by Spiderman himself that I needed to investigate Professor Diabolical and here you come strutting in, saying that’s who you are. What’s this for? Money? God, I beg that you just get a normal job.” 

“What the fuck, Lando?”

“Please get a job, Logan. I’ll even put in a good word at the Bugle for you or something, you don’t have to be a villain!” 

“Lando!” He screeched. “I’m not a villain for crying out loud! I’m here to investigate too! I- uh- I made up this persona to get in with this gang to find out more about them!”

“Oh give off, you’re such a bullshitter! Just you wait till I tell Oscar about this. You know, I was right about you! I knew you were bad news but Oscar insisted you were a really good friend to him. I’m over here offering to help you out with a job and you’re still lying to me.” Lando spat out and made a move to grab his phone, Logan pounced forward to try and stop him before he could message Oscar. Lando managed to swivel out of the way, Logan falling slightly forward before catching himself.

“Dude, you’ve got no idea what’s really going on, do you?” Logan laughed and shook his head to himself. 

Lando furrowed his eyebrows. “What are you talking about?” 

“All this, you don’t see it do you?”

“Stop speaking in riddles!” Lando almost stomped his foot on the ground in frustration.

A sigh left the American’s mouth and he crossed his arms over his chest, contemplating something before speaking his mind. “Okay, look. I’ve been helping Spiderman with an investigation too! I’ve been disguised as someone called Professor Diabolical and I’ve infiltrated their gang! Spiderman sent you to watch the exchange to make sure nothing went wrong, he didn’t tell you about the whole plan since he thought you’d be safer not knowing the truth for now. He thought you’d hide and stay out of the way!”

Lando really didn’t know what to think. He didn’t like Logan, he didn’t know enough about him to know if he was telling the truth or not. Lando placed a hand on his hip before addressing him. “So then what? What was the actual point of me investigating this then?” 

“He wanted you to check the factory to make sure it hadn’t been touched since he ransacked it and took the last weapons away. Then, you could watch our exchange and you could report it if it started going sideways since I can’t exactly whip out my phone and call for Spiderman! He’s too busy looking for Doc Ock.” Logan said. 

“So there is actually a gang involved?”

“Yes.” 

“And Spiderman approached you for help? God help his standards.”

“Well, I offered my help and fuck you, just because you don’t like me doesn’t mean other people agree with you.” 

“I’m sorry, I can’t wrap my head around this.” Lando could see Spiderman’s point of Lando being there for if anything went wrong, but why involve Logan of all people? What did he have to do with Spiderman? “How do you even know Spiderman?” 

“How do you know Spiderman?”

“Touché, not relevant.” Lando said. “I don’t understand why he just didn’t tell me the truth…” He was perplexed, it was really odd to him that Spiderman just didn’t tell him the truth. This whole situation just screamed weird to him. It was really questioning the hero’s judgment considering he paced his trust in Logan. 

“To keep you safe, my god! Look, would you believe me if I told you these people are actually working for Doc Ock?” 

Lando perked up at that, now that he was interested in helping with. “Doc Ock?”

“Yeah, big supervillain with eight mechanical tentacles coming from him, do you know him?” 

Lando rolled his eyes and almost scoffed, he couldn’t believe this guy. “Don’t sass me, of course I know who it is.” 

“Well anyway, word on the street is these people are buying these weapons for Doc Ock. God knows why, but apparently he’s been collecting them. While Spiderman searches for the supervillain, he wants us to track down these criminals and find out whatever we can.” 

The British boy’s jaw clenched and he let out an irritated breath. “So…” Lando exaggerated a gulp. “He wants us to work together?” God, he could’ve retched at the thought of working with the American. 

“Precisely. Although, you weren’t meant to know it was me. You were meant to be hiding and I was trying to scare you. You know, you’ve got no survival instinct.”

Lando’s eyebrows shot up. “What was I supposed to do?” Lando shouted and pointed to the bat on the floor dramatically. “You were going to hit me with that fucking bat!” 

“I wasn’t actually going to hit you! But like, I thought you’d at least run and hide?” Logan’s voice rose, matching Lando’s fervor. 

“You’re an imbecile-” Lando’s voice trailed off as he heard a very loud screeching, then cut off as something came to a halt. The two looked at each other, their argument forgotten and together they looked to where the screeching was coming from. 

There at the entrance to the alleyway was a van, compiled with what looked to be at least 12 men inside. Logan’s eyes widened in alarm once he realised these were the men he was supposed to meet, watching them emerge from the van. He quickly noted that his mask was long discarded on the floor along with the bat and he wouldn’t have enough time to hide Lando and get his outfit back together. He had to make a choice quickly. Help Lando or help himself. 

He didn’t really need to think twice about it. 

Logan instinctively grabbed onto Lando’s hand, his grip firm and urgent. Lando, while shocked by the physical contact, recognised the oncoming danger as his eyes locked onto the group of criminals. They hadn’t been noticed yet, but they would if they didn’t make a move now. “Fucking move, quick!” Logan whispered in his ear. 

Together, they dashed towards the entrance of the abandoned factory, the rusty door screeching in protest as the hinges moved. Logan forced it shut behind them and Lando prayed to himself that the criminals hadn’t been too close to hear it. The fear inside him compelled him to move as they crouched underneath one of the broken windows.

As Lando scurried under the window and knelt down, he whined at the sensation of the shattered glass digging into the fabric of his joggers. He wasn’t sure if they had cut his knees, the adrenaline in his body was quick to replace the pain. The place was silent, the only thing they could hear was the rigid breaths they released and the distant sound of the criminals getting out of the van.

His eyes scanned the room for any potential exit but with it being so dark and the moonlight’s gaze hardly lighting the place, he couldn’t make out a good plan. The weight of the silence bore down on them until Lando whispered. “Logan?”

“What could you possibly say right now that is important enough to risk our lives?” Logan hastily whispered.

“I don’t know what’s going to happen here, but I’m sorry. I’m sorry for being an asshole, I just… Oscar’s my best friend, then suddenly you came into the picture and at one point it was all about how great his new friend is. I just couldn’t- I don’t know, it wasn’t fair but I-”

“You like Oscar.” Logan interrupted, his face illuminated by the moonlight showed his knowing gaze. Lando nodded guiltily, confirming his suspicion. “I get it, I really do. He’s yours and you’re his. You’re fine dude, I forgive you since we could die any second.” 

“Please don’t jinx us.” Lando begged. “Oh my god, I need to message Oscar in case I die.” 

“We have more important things to think about, Lando!” Logan uttered and moved so he could peek out of the broken window. Lando pulled out his phone quickly to message Oscar.

Lando Norris

Oscar

My darling 

It’s kinda gone tits up here I won’t lie

I could die, I’d write something sentimental if I had the time

Clear my browser history and don’t give Charles any of my stuff 

Love you

Lando’s fingers moved swiftly over the phone, sending the array of urgent messages as his fingers trembled slightly. He hastily stuffed his phone back in his pocket, ignoring the scrap of the glass on his knees as he shifted himself and joined Logan in peering like a meerkat over the windows edge. 

The men had moved fully into the space where they were previously standing, one of them looking at the now dirty white mask on the floor and another examining the discarded bat. Lando had forgotten about the pieces of Logan’s cheap costume, the criminals having a growing suspicion about them. Lando put his hand over his mouth as he surveyed the men, noticing that a few of them had knives on them, and then nearly gasped out loud once he saw a few of the bigger guys had guns in their hands. Oh my god, what type of movie had he walked into? 

Just as Lando began to voice his concerns, a firm tug on his back by the American yanked him down, bringing him to the shelter of the window again. Lando parted his lips to speak until Logan silenced him with a finger to his own lips in a ‘shush’ motion, Lando immediately shut his mouth. Suddenly, he heard the low grumble of someone moving past the window and stopping right outside of it. Fear pumped through his body like a drug with an intoxicating surge, his heart hammering out of his chest. 

The tense moment stretched into what felt like hours to Lando, the two boys still huddled together underneath the window. Any sudden movement with the shattered close beneath them would draw attention to them. The low grumbling gradually moved away from the window, heavy footsteps following in its path. Lando breathed in and out, calming himself before he could freak out any further. The breathing was his only anchor to the world right now. 

“Bro, I need you to go on the app and get Spiderman here now. Holy shit, it was meant to be one guy.” Logan said hastily. “My phone isn’t here, I couldn’t bring it just in case.” 

“Yeah, yeah okay.” Lando nodded frantically and scrambled quickly to get his phone out of his pocket. As he unlocked the phone, he noticed that it was left open on his messages with Oscar. 

Oscar Piastri

Lando??

Lando mate what’s happened?

You can’t just drop a bombshell like that

Have you reported it on the Spiderman app?

I’ve reported it, he should be coming soon

Please hold on Lando 

Love you too

As his heart swelled reading the last message, his phone turned black. He blinked in confusion. What? He tried to turn his phone back on, but the black screen remained. 

“No, no, no.” He muttered to himself, whacking the phone on the side a few times for good measure. It was useful, his phone died. How stupid could he be to use it so much for his music and using it as the flashlight, no wonder it had already died. Lando turned to Logan sheepishly, who was already staring him down with a death glare. “Look- at least Oscar’s already alerted him, he should be here soon.” 

“Wait- listen, you can hear them talking.” 

Lando quickly shut up and moved to look over the windows edge again, Logan quickly following suit. They could see two people gathered in the centre of the alleyway talking. 

“Sir, we found that white mask and bat over there. We’re not too sure what’s happened here, but it looks as though the guy we were meeting got into a little… altercation.” One of the brutes said.

“Ha, they think I beat your ass.” Lando whispered triumphantly.

Logan lightly punched his side. “Pay attention, dude.” 

The smaller looking of the two took the mask out of the brutes hand, examining it carefully. “I think we’ve been played here boys, look at this cheap thing. We’re supposed to be meeting a criminal mastermind yet he’s picked this up from Wilkinsons.” The smaller guy held the mask between his fingers, like touching it would give him a disease. 

Lando nearly laughed at the prospect of being right, turning to Logan who was gritting his teeth together. “Don’t say a fucking thing.” 

“Sir, what about the weapons? Doesn't the Boss need them?” 

“Check the factory, I’ve had a sneaky feeling Spiderman may have been involved with this right from the beginning. The boss was insistent that we needed this though. Make sure the weapons are still there, god forbid we have to tell him they’re gone.” 

“Sir, what does the Boss want with them?”

“They’ve got some powerful things in them that he needs, you don’t need to do anything more than that. Now, do your job and go check the factory.” The smaller man ordered, turning on his heel and walking away. 

Lando and Logan snapped to look at each other, they were right next to the door of the factory and no matter what, would be caught. Before the brute could make a move for the factory, a red ball of fury came flying down from the sky and swung-kicked him into the air, a flurry of punches being thrown at him until flew backwards. 

“It’s Spiderman, get him!” 

The hero had impeccable timing as always. He was dressed in his usual suit with a black hoodie thrown over it. Lando and Logan watched from the window in awe as Spiderman moved with grace, moving one by one along the criminals. While he was busy punching a brute, another was approaching behind him getting ready to pounce on him. Lando was about to yell, until the hero slid in between the brutes legs and appeared behind him, using all force in his legs to kick the brute into the other guy, sending them tumbling. Another guy approached him, causing Spiderman to shoot a flurry of webs and sticking him to the wall behind. 

He watched on as he single-handedly took on 12 criminals with ease, using his web-shooters and gadgets to quickly defeat them. One of the gadgets shot tendrils out at the criminals, yanking them all forward to crash together in one big heap. It seemed like seconds passed before the hero was standing there, his chest rising up and down as his eyes surveyed the area. 

One guy weakly stood up, his gun shaking in his hands as he took tentative steps towards Spiderman. “Hands up where I can see them!” He commanded and Spiderman slowly raised his hands in the air. 

The hero fell to his knees. “Oh dear god, please, not a gun! I’m really scared of guns!” He cried out and crouched to the floor. The criminal saw this as the perfect opportunity to hobble closer, his gun still pointing at the hero. 

Once he was right next to him with his finger on the trigger, he said “Any last words?” While his back was turned to Lando, he could hear the smirk in his voice. 

Lando could hear his heart in his ears, the pounding in his chest growing as the urge to intervene clawed at him. He took a deep breath, he trusted that Spiderman had a calculated plan as he could see an unwavering confidence in his stature.  

“Yes actually, didn’t you know guns were so last year? It’s all about the webs now.” Spiderman taunted. The criminal caught off guard by the comment loosened his grip around the weapon. Before he could regain his composure, Spiderman grabbed the gun, his reflexes honed to perfection as he dodge the bullets that were frantically shot. With one swift movement, the gun was ripped out of his hands and rattled loudly as it hit the ground. 

The criminal backed up slowly then proceeded to turn around and quickly make a sprint out of the alleyway. Spiderman’s eyes zeroed in on him, with a web flying out and gripping onto his back. The masked vigilante swayed the web side to side, causing the criminal to hit the van and wall each time he swayed. He continued this before throwing him in the air, pulling on the web’s end to meet him in the sky and proceeded to punch him. The criminal launched back down the ground, out like a light. 

Spiderman landed on the ground in a typical superhero stance before pulling himself up from the ground. Logan had already made a move for the rusty door, Lando following after him as they moved out of the building. The young Brit manurerved around the bodies and prayed that he didn’t accidentally step on one. Before he could properly think about what he was doing, he sprung himself on Spiderman and sent them both flying to the ground. 

Before they could hit the ground, a web had flown out towards the wall in front of Spiderman, making it so they were actually leaning towards the ground rather than on the wet floor. “Is that your phone in your pocket or are you just happy to see me?” Spiderman teased, his one hand wrapping around Lando’s waist while the young Brit wrapped his arms around the hero’s torso. Realising the compromising position, he made a move to scramble away, dropping his arms. Spiderman kept his firm grip however and pulled on the web slightly to plant their feet firmly on the ground. 

“No jokes right now please, I just almost died. You’d have a lot of fun peeling my body from the ground and bringing it to Oscar. But god, thank you again for saving me.” Lando stated, accepting his fate with Spiderman still holding him and leaning into the touch. He looked down towards the direction of his arm and noticed there was a big hole in the black jumper. His hand wandered to the hole, playing with it a little and examining it. 

“You’re so lucky your friend reported it, I told you to go on the app if anything happened!” Spiderman said sternly. 

“My phone died!” Lando pouted. 

“And I didn’t even bring mine.” 

Spiderman looked up at Logan, the waistcoat buttons had now been undone and his hair looked like a hand attacked it. “And you, what happened to not revealing yourself? What if they’d seen your face?”

Logan shrugged like he couldn’t care any less. “He’s not as stupid as he looks, he knew it was me straight away.” 

“Oh- uh, you know each other?” Spiderman asked with an uncertain tone in his voice, his hand dropping from Lando’s waist. 

The young Brit frowned and allowed himself to step away. “Yeah, he’s Oscar’s friend. We… we know each other.” Lando wasn’t actually too sure what to call him and Logan now. If you asked him a few hours ago, he’d have gone on and on about how they were enemies and he couldn’t stand to be around him. After experiencing whatever this was together, he thought he could live with them being civil. “He’s my ride or die now.” 

“I don’t think we’re quite there, Lando.” 

“Brothers in death.” 

“We didn’t die.”

“We-”

“-okay!” Spiderman interrupted and stood between the two. “Looks like the mission was a fail anyway. I’ll round these up and contact the police, they’ll handle it from here.” 

“I’m sorry we failed, but they showed up earlier than expected.” Logan apologised. 

“It’s fine, they probably came to scope it out earlier like I sent Lando to do.” Spiderman reassured, his hand clasping Logan on the shoulder. He looked over his shoulder at Lando. “I’m guessing Logan explained what I really wanted from you then?” 

“Yep, bit offended I was just meant to be the reporter, but I can forgive you if I can still take the pictures?” Lando blinked with puppy eyes and looked up through his lashes, asking with a hopeful tone. “Even though I didn’t actually report it like you wanted me to?” 

“Even if you didn’t actually report it.” 

“Amazing!” Lando whooped. “Listen, as much as I’d like to stay, I do have a roommate that probably thinks I’m dead right now, so I should probably head home and see him!” 

Spiderman froze in his space, Logan sending him a pointed look. Lando eyed them both carefully… weird. “Will you be okay getting back by yourself?” The hero asked. 

Lando shrugged. “I mean, I should be fine. You handled them all anyway.” 

“And it’ll take you about thirty minutes to get home right?”

“Y-yeah? How do you know?” Lando furrowed his eyebrows as the conversation took a weird turn. 

Spiderman responded, “I walk these streets when I’m not busy being Spiderman, you know. I’m not always out beating up bad guys and saving damsels in distress like you.” 

“Right…” His body relaxed a little, he wasn’t sure why he had felt an odd feeling at that. “I best get going then?” 

“I’ll see you on the rooftop soon?” Spiderman asked. “We can take pictures and watch the sunset.” 

A soft smile spread across his face and he nodded eagerly. “I’ll see you there.” 

And with that, he started to make his journey back to the flat, back to Oscar, back to the boy he loves and back to the safety of his arms. 

While Lando made his way back to his flat, Spiderman and Logan stayed behind. Spiderman forwarded the details of the crime scene onto the police quickly while Logan picked up his discarded cheap mask and the bat. He swung it playfully with his one hand, pretending to hit a ball and watching it fly off after a great hit. 

“How fast did he figure it out?” Spiderman asked as he carefully moved the unconscious bodies in a pile on the floor, webbing them together to prevent them from escaping should they wake up anytime soon. 

Logan leaned on the bat as the bottom of it touched the floor. “Faster than I thought, yet you were so insistent he wouldn’t realise.”

Spiderman rubbed his face in frustration. “He’s oblivious usually, Logan. How was I meant to know he would catch on?”

“Give him some credit where it’s due. He can be smart when he wants to be and he really wants to help.” Logan defended the young Brit, feeling a sort of protectiveness over him after their experience together and the confession from him in the factory. 

“I know, you don’t need to remind me.” The hero muttered, the bodies now piled and all weapons discarded on the floor, also covered in webs. His one web-shooter had run out. He pulled out another vial of the solution and began to set it into the gadget. 

A tense silence hung in the air, the weight of the unspoken incident laying between them. Logan could tell the other was mad at him, the eyes of the high-tech mask simmering with anger and frustration. He was unsure if it was purely the fact he was found out or the fact that he and Lando had ended up in trouble. He stood there awkwardly, debating if he should speak his mind, whether it was worth the reaction and the conversation to be had. With a deep breath, he punctured the strained silence. “You should tell him.” Logan urged. 

He stopped in his tracks, the vial of solution halfway in the gadge. Spiderman’s eyes mixed with conflict before he breathed as sigh. “You know I can’t.” He reminded the other. 

“He doesn’t deserve to be kept in the dark.” Logan persisted, his voice tinged with concern as he attempted to persuade the hero. 

A bitter scoff left the hero. “What he doesn’t know won’t hurt him, Logan.” He shot back. Realising the harshness of his words, he pulled back on his anger. “I’m sorry, I know it wasn’t your fault. I just need to make sure you’re both safe, that everyone is safe.”

“And I am safe, I’ve proven I’m safe! Give him a chance!”

“The answer is no, I’m sorry. I can’t risk it anymore than I already have. Go home, Logan, I’ll speak to you soon.”

The conversation was done, Logan knew there was no point in fighting it anymore, he’d have to bring it up another day when he was perhaps a lot more calm. “Bye, Spiderman.” He muttered, his voice inked with a faint trace of resignation as he turned to leave. 

Spiderman stood there looking at him, a sadness eroding from his stance and the eyes softening. He looked like he wanted to say something, but Logan had already started walking away. Great.

Wonderful. 

The cold winter air bit at his cheeks, the fabric of the suit keeping himself safe from the harsh effects. As Logan’s figure faded into the winter night, Spiderman remained rooted to the spot. A wave of unresolved emotions washed over him with a tinge of regret, regret for the way he acted towards Logan, for brushing him off so quickly. With a sudden resolve, he shot a web into the darkness, swinging himself away into the frigid winter night, his heart heavy with the weight of unspoken truths. 

Lando had finally made it home, rushing himself slightly with adrenaline from the earlier event still cruising through his body, that and his phone was dead, couldn’t exactly contact anyone if something happened. His legs pulled him begrudgingly up the stairs, his body protesting at the continuous movement. You’d think after living there so long, he’d be used to it now. 

He reached the front door, using his key to let himself in. The moment he entered the flat, immediately met with the sight of a distressed Oscar. His hair was a mess, flat against his forehead and his clothes were creased, a look of relief was coating his face however. 

“God, Lando.” He moved forward to take the boy into his arms, his arms snaking around Lando’s waist, the young Brit instinctively wrapped his arms around Oscar’s neck and buried his face in the crook of his neck. His hot breath caused shivers to run down the Aussie’s spine. Oscar’s hand reached up to the back of Lando’s head, wrapping itself in the little curls. “Please don’t ever do anything like that again. You don’t realise how worried I was. You text me saying that and I was stuck here, I couldn’t do anything to help you.”

“Osc, you couldn’t have done anything even if you were there. You literally saved us reporting it, my phone died! Thank you so much, I don’t know what I’d do without you in my life.” He mumbled into his neck, his grip tightening like he was trying to mould them together.

“I had no clue what was happening, you didn’t answer my texts and I got myself so worked up. I-I though it was already too late. Guns and knives, Lando, I can’t have you near it. What if anything happened to you? I couldn’t live with myself knowing that you were hurt or worse , Lando. Please, no more investigating. Don’t ever offer to do it again.” Oscar pleaded, pulling back from the embrace and putting his finger under Lando’s chin, lifting his head. Their eyes met, worry and love clashing like they were caught in a trance. He could stand there and study his eyes for days, to be caught in the chocolate rivers of his eyes is all Lando could ask for. 

He could say it. He could say it now and get it out of his system, who cares if Oscar didn’t feel the same? The boy deserved to know how loved he was, to know how cherished and admired he was. He deserved to know he was the light of Lando’s world and the thing that kept him moving in life. He owed everything to the boy holding him so tightly. 

But, he couldn’t say it. 

No matter how hard he tried, every attempt over the years, he couldn’t let the words escape his mouth. He couldn’t reveal the vulnerability to him. While Oscar was everything to Lando, he knew that it wasn’t the same for him. Why ruin something so perfect for feelings he had harbored and hidden so well already?

Lando nodded in agreement, never breaking eye contact, his breath hitching and hoping to god that Oscar didn’t notice. “I promise, no more investigations. I don’t think Spiderman would let me know anyway.” He giggled lightly. 

“Logan messaged me, assuming he’s the ‘us’ you’re referring to?” 

Lando nodded in response. “Yeah he is, it’s a very long story. How I Met Your Mother, pizza and gossip?” Lando offered, pulling his best puppy dog eyes. He knew Oscar could never deny his eyes, even though he probably wouldn’t anyway. 

“Of course, go sit down and I’ll make you a hot chocolate too. You can tell me all about it.” Oscar led the boy towards the living room, grabbing the orange blanket and draping it over him. 

As Oscar hurried after the boy, ordering them a pizza and making a stunning cup of hot chocolate, Lando couldn’t help but wonder something. He thought about the ‘guns and knives’ comment from Oscar, he hadn’t mentioned anything about it. He didn’t tell Oscar anything about the situation so far, so for Oscar to know there were guns and knives there… it struck him as odd. That was until he remembered him saying that Logan had messaged him, probably updating him on some quick details. Lando brushed it off, it was definitely because of Logan. 

Oscar came over with the mug of hot chocolate, setting it down on the coffee table in front of him. The steam coming off it filled in with delight, he’d have it in a few minutes. “Thanks, mate. I really appreciate it.”

“It’s no problem, you know that. You sure you don’t wanna watch Game of Thrones tonight though?” The Aussie offered. 

Lando shook his head. “No, I’ll end up talking and we’ll only have to rewind it. It’s comfort show time, I wanna see Ted get rejected for the umpteenth time.”

“Whatever you say, your majesty.” Oscar joined him on the sofa, settling himself under the blanket and lifting an arm for Lando to snuggle into the side of him. It was a lovely routine that he wouldn’t change for the world. Oscar put their show on, letting Lando sink into the sweet peace of his embrace. “Pizza’s ordered, shows on and hot chocolate in front of you. Do you need anything else before I get too comfy?”

“Just stay with me, please. Will you stay in my room tonight?” 

Oscar seemed to hesitate slightly, sniffling before nodding. “Sure, try not to catch this little infectious disease though.”

“You’re sick?” Lando exclaimed, recoiling himself away from Oscar’s touch dramatically, his hands flailing in fake horror. “Get away from me!” He laughed.

The Aussie pulled Lando back under his arm, whining softly. “Not sick, just a little sniffle.” He insisted with a sweet tone. 

“A little sniffle. Are you sure?” Lando asked with a whimsical tone, his eyebrows raising in mock suspicion. He playfully poked Oscar’s side, laughing as the Aussie tried to keep his hands away to prevent any tickling Lando may do. 

“Keep your hands to yourself before I bite them off, you minx. I’m sure I’m not sick! Now, tell me what happened already! I’ll tell you the gossip I heard about Max and Charles~” He sang and ruffled Lando’s hair.

God, he did love him. 

Chapter End Notes

I’d love to know what you guys thought, please let me know if you enjoyed! Any feedback is good feedback :)

Chapter 4: Chapter 4

Chapter Summary

Oscar falls sick and Lando finally gets to take his pictures of Spiderman.

Chapter Notes

Hi guys! Little bit of a shorter one here today just to get the story moving along to where it needs to be! Thank you al the kudos and support you guys have given me over the course of this so far!

I created a Playlist for this story in case anyone wants to check it out! I’ll be adding to it as I go along.

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Oscar was lying, he was in fact sick after all. 

Lando had noticed the last few weeks that Oscar wasn’t feeling the best, his pale skin looking worse for wear, lack of sleep and exhaustion catching up to him leaving purple eye bags in their wake, the constant runs in the freezing cold with nothing but a loose shirt and a pair of shorts (although he wasn’t one to complain about seeing the shorts), the sniffling. But also… Oscar was clingy. He was only ever clingy when he had an illness coming on, but it also made him a stubborn bastard too.

Which is why they were currently in the kitchen arguing with each other. 

He also had a tendency to ignore his illness and carry on with his day like he wasn’t struggling with every movement. It always ended in him finally crashing and Lando having to look after him. He was just trying to prevent it before it could happen!

“Tell your boss you’re not going in! Fuck right off if you think you’re going to the library like this!” Lando ordered, looking over his shoulder at Oscar as the Aussie leaned against the kitchen cabinets staring at the ceiling. He was washing the dishes from his breakfast when he could see Oscar trying to sneak out of the room from the corner of his eye. He angrily threw the sponge across the room expecting Oscar to dodge it, but he let the wet sponge hit his face without any movement. Lando was definitely not letting him go to work after his usually fast reflexes failed to stop the sponge from colliding with him. 

“I have to go in, Lando. I need this job and I’m not even sick! I’m just a little tired, mate.” Oscar replied, sniffling disgustingly. A little was an understatement. His hair was a greasy mess on his head and his eyes were droopy and red, as much as Lando found him attractive, even he could admit he didn’t look his best right now. 

Lando scoffed and placed the last dish on the drying rack, grabbing a dry cloth to wipe his hands on. “You look like shit, Oscar, you’ve barely slept, you keep sneezing and last night I had to rub your back after you nearly threw up after smelling the chicken.”
