The younger boy looked down from the ceiling and at Lando now, his head flopping around a little like he had no strength to hold it up. “The chicken was expired, that’s why!” 

“Okay, whatever! What about my other points, hm? And we both know you don’t need the money, your parents send you money every month.” Since Lando knew Oscar, his parents had sent him a good chunk of money every week for his rent, food, clothes etc, even though he had two jobs. Sure, his internship was helping towards his future career, but Lando never understood why he needed to keep the library job. 

“I’m not sick. Also, it doesn't hurt to have extra money around!”

“You’re sick, don’t lie!” 

“M’not.” Oscar mumbled and tried to stand from where he was leaning against the cabinet, but in doing so he nearly tumbled over. Lando was quick to rush forward and grab him around the waist, managing to pull him up with a grunt and move in the direction of Oscar’s bedroom. “Fine, I might have a bit of a cold.”

“Awh, you think? I’d have never guessed after you let a dirty sponge hit your face and nearly face-planted the kitchen floor.” Lando sassed him, trudging them along to the Aussie’s bedroom. When there was no quip from Oscar, Lando bit his lip in worry. 

The younger boy was standing up straight now, although he had a little wave to his step. “Are you sure I can’t go in today? What if… what if they really need me in?”

“I’m sure they can manage to stack some books and scan them without you. Bedroom, now.” Lando said with a stern voice and pointed look on his face. Oscar huffed and rolled his eyes, sluggishly pushing the slightly open door to his room. However, the door slammed open from the force and hit the wall inside the room with a loud bang.

The two stood there looking at the door together, Lando could see the hinges of the top of the door had come slightly loose. He walked into the room with Oscar following like a sloth after him, he examined the door and saw a mark on the wall where the door handle had hit it. Upon further inspection, he could see it left an indent in the wall. Lando knew Oscar was strong, he always lifted the Brit with ease and was the jar opener in the flat, he must’ve overestimated how much force the door needed with his co-ordination off. “When our landlord keeps our deposit, you better give me half of what you owe me.” 

“Don’t be dramatic, we’ll fix it. No way am I letting that sleazebag keep our deposit.” Oscar grumbled and walked past the sight of the door, ignoring that he had thrown it open and hurt the poor wall. He threw himself on the bed and under the covers he went, snuggling under them like a koala. 

Lando could’ve cursed whoever gave Oscar this cold. “Come on, in your fluffy pajamas! You’re not going to sleep in your outdoor clothes.”

“Don’t wanna move.” He mumbled, the words slightly muffled as Oscar nuzzled his head in the pillow comfortably. 

“You don’t even wanna wear your Garfield set?” 

“… My Garfield set?” Oscar's voice perked up at the mention of the pajamas. 

“Uh huh, but I guess we can always give them to someone else who wants to wear them.” Lando playfully suggested, a grin spreading across his face. He knew exactly what he was doing. 

“If you give them away you’ll have nothing for Christmas from me.” Oscar said with a playful but yet serious tone.

“You wouldn’t!” Lando said accusingly. 

“Oh, I would.”

He wouldn’t. Probably. 

Lando walked to his chest of drawers and went to the middle drawer, pulling out the fluffy Garfield pajamas. He bought them for Oscar as a joke before, calling him a grumpy cat who just wanted lasagna (Oscar didn’t even really like lasagna all that much, but it didn’t matter). The younger of the two had despised it at first, but Lando caught him more than enough times wearing them to know he actually loved them now. He walked over to the bed and gently laid them next to the sick boy. “Right you put them on, I’ll be back now, okay?” 

Oscar weakly nodded, followed by three sneezes and each time Lando uttered ‘bless you’ . He walked out of the room and pulled the door slowly behind him, careful with it after the hinges became loose. Once he was satisfied the door wasn’t about to fall down, he made his way to the living room and pulled out his phone. He googled the campus library’s number and gave them a quick call. After a few rings, someone answered. 

“Good morning! This is the library of the University of London, how can we help?”

Lando recognised the voice from when he would go to visit Oscar during his lunch breaks during their first year in university. “Katie! It’s Lando, Oscar’s friend. How are you?”

“Hey Lando, I haven’t seen you in ages! I’m doing great, just busy as always in the library, what about you?”

“Just busy looking after a sick Oscar, after he refuses to acknowledge he’s sick!”

“Some people never change, especially Oscar.” Her light laugh came through the phone. “ What’s up? What can I do for you?”

“I’m actually calling about Oscar, I know he was scheduled in for today but he’s really sick-“

“Lando.” She interrupted quickly. “Oscar doesn’t work here anymore, he hasn’t for at least a year now.”

“What? Yes he does, he’s been going to work the past week. He was coming in today, have you confused him with someone else?” Lando asked and his face contorted in confusion. His grip tightened on the phone as he processed the revelation. 

He heard her sigh deeply. “Lando… I’m not sure why he didn’t tell you, but he handed in his notice last year, he said he didn’t have enough time in his schedule for it anymore. Maybe you should talk to him about it?” She offered, probably since there was nothing else she could do. 

“Right, sorry for bothering you Katie! Have a nice day, bye!” He quickly hung up before she could respond and he threw his phone down on the sofa in front of him, bouncing off it and falling to the floor. He ran a hand through his hair and tugged at the curls, frustration wavering through him. Why on earth did Oscar lie to him for a full year about working in the library? Why wouldn’t he just be honest and tell him that he left? He felt a hint of betrayal bloom in his chest, a hurt spreading through him at the thought of not being told the truth by his best friend. Disbelief and anger washed over him, his mind clouded with so many questions. 

He was sure there was a reason why Oscar didn’t say anything, maybe he was dealing with a project at the time and didn’t have the capacity for the job then and got in too deep to tell Lando the truth. Yeah, that was it. His emotions wavered and he felt a somewhat feeling of understanding enveloping him. It was fine, if Oscar wanted to tell him and talk about it, he would’ve done it by now. 

There’s no point in him bringing it up, especially now after a whole year. 

He did a little walk of shame to pick up his phone, grateful no one had been around to see him launch his phone at the sofa. Lando grabbed their orange blanket and made his way back to Oscar’s room, he’ll just act normal, it’s fine. 

He was careful with the door again, terrified it was going to fall down any moment. Lando peered into the room and almost tripped over himself seeing Oscar sat up in bed shirtless, pulling the pajama top over his head. He almost drooled at the side before quickly prying his eyes away once the Aussie’s head popped through the shirt. “Hi.” Oscar said weakly. 

“Hi.” Lando entered fully now and sat on the bed in the empty space next to Oscar. They both looked at each other and he gave the younger one a soft smile. His hand came up to touch Oscar’s forehead, the boy melting under the touch. Lando frowned at the warmth on his forehead. “How are you feeling?”

“Cold.” His body shivered along with it. “Who were you on the phone to?”

“Oh… just Charles, asked if there was any particular food we wanted at the party.” Lando had to hand it to himself, he did pretty well for lying on the spot. 

Oscar hummed in response, his eyes landing on the orange blanket in Lando’s hand. “Gimme, please, mate. I’m so cold.”

“You can’t wear too many layers, you have a fever.” Lando warned him. Oscar already pried the blanket out of his hands and wrapped himself up like a burrito, shuffling under the normal blanket as well. Lando smiled in amusement, he’d soon get too hot and just take it off anyway. 

“Are you staying here?” Oscar asked. 

“Of course I am, you don’t even need to ask.” Lando said, lying down next to Oscar and turning on his side to look at him. He almost cooed at the sweet sight of the pale and exhausted boy. He wished he could protect Oscar from all the bad in the world, keep him wrapped up in their orange blanket and make sure he was always healthy and safe. 

While he was busy staring, Oscar reached a hand out and placed it on Lando’s shoulder, trying to drag him in a little closer. Once he succeeded and the Brit had closed the gap between them, he tried to retract his hand. However, his hand wouldn’t move. Lando’s shirt was lifting away from his chest with Oscar’s hand still attached to it. A look of panic washed over the Aussie and he frantically waved his hand, trying to detach the two. Lando watched it curiously and muttered ‘ what the fuck’ to himself before grabbing onto Oscar’s wrist. 

Oscar froze, his nerves subsiding and the t-shirt dropped from his hand. His head had raised from the pillow in the quick panic, allowing it to plop back down on the pillow with a huff. “What was that, mate?” Lando wasn’t sure what had just happened, but it was very odd.

“Nothing, just your shirt getting stuck to my hand. You should probably- um- take it off? Right?” Oscar weakly suggested. 

Lando shrugged his shoulders, if it would help calm Oscar down and prevent him from having another quick panic then sure. He sat up on the bed slightly and pulled his shirt off in one swift movement, his curls bouncing as it slid off the top of his head. He felt an intense stare on him, it wasn’t anything new. Lando was no stranger to knowing Oscar stares at him, that he stared with soft eyes and a caring smile. That he looked when he thought no one could notice, but Lando could always feel it. It was like a watchful eye, always protecting, always caring, always listening. 

Their friends often teased him when Oscar wasn’t around, they dubbed him ‘heart-eyes Piastri’ , Lando was sure the other would have a fit if he found out. It made Lando feel content. Maybe that was why he was so okay with what they had, without needing to push it any further. Without needing to confess his love for him. Without needing to know every secret Oscar harbored. They had their life and it worked for them. 

With the shirt discarded to the floor and his tanned torso on display, he laid next to Oscar again and was delighted to see some colour in the boy’s face. “Little red there, aren’t you?”

“I’m just warm!” Oscar said, his voice stuffy and whiny from being blocked up. He tore the big blanket off his body for dramatic effect. 

“Whatever you say, Piastri.”

“Fuck off, Norris,” 

Lando smiled contently and wrapped his arm around Oscar’s pale frame, watching as Oscar very pointedly tried not to look at his chest and was instead looking him in the eyes. Now was as good a time as any, he wasn’t going to ask it outright, he just wanted to see what was said. “Hey, Osc?” 

“Yeah?”

“Did you call in sick after?”

“Uh- yeah I text my boss to let her know I wouldn’t be in. She said it was fine.” Oscar said. While Lando was upset that he was lying to him so easily, looking him in the eyes with ease, he told himself he had his reasons for it. 

“Okay.” He nodded, disbelief and hurt subsiding within him. “Let’s get you to sleep.”

*

Lando threw his backpack to the ground with an exaggerated huff, feeling personally victimised by the unnecessary amount of stairs to the rooftop. He was actually going to take the lift this time, but it had a big out of order sign on it. The receptionist (who didn’t even bother checking if he should be in the building) had merely drawn out that he would need to take the stairs, because of course he did. 

After the long and treacherous journey up the stairs, he finally made it to the rooftop just as the sunset began to set and Spiderman was nowhere to be seen. He knew this was a possibility, he was a busy hero and they hadn’t exactly set a date on when they’d meet. He sighed to himself and sat down on the cold rooftop cross-legged, pulling out his phone. 

Lando Norris

Oscarrrrrr

He’s not here :(

Oscar Piastri 

I mean the sunset has only just really started?

How long have you been there?

Lando Norris

2 minutes

BUT he’s usually here, before you go to judge me >:(

Think I’ll have to come back another day 

 

Oscar Piastri

You’re busy the next few days, give it a few more minutes

I’m sure he’ll turn up

You know he can’t resist the sunset 

He turned his phone off with a click with his boredom already catching up to him. Lando decided to give it five more minutes, otherwise he’d head back to the warm flat and venture to the rooftop another day. A cold gust of wind overcame him and he pulled the bobble hat over his ears a little more, grateful he’d thrown on his (read: Oscar’s) hoodie before leaving. He also would’ve left without a hat if Oscar hadn’t forced it on his head. Lando thought he looked stupid with it on, but the Aussie had taken the Brit’s camera and snapped a photo of it, calling him cute. 

He was totally normal about it. 

While he waited for Spiderman, he decided he’d take some pictures of the landscape for a little bit of practice. If Spiderman showed up, they wouldn’t have a lot of time to be messing around with shots, it tends to get darker a lot quicker now that they’re in December. 

He pulled the camera out the bag, turning it on and messing with a few of the features. Lando aimed it towards the city skyline and zoomed in and out on a pigeon sitting on the lamppost by the opposite street. The camera made its little noises and he messed around with the focus before snapping a picture. 

As he took the picture, the red and blue hero had swooped into frame from a web, scaring the poor pigeon away but pulling a pose with his hand pointing towards the camera and his legs in a crouched position. It looked like a cool fighting stance, he may have ruined the pigeon photo, but he did already get one good photo. 

Spiderman landed next time, rolling forward on the rooftop until he was planted on his feet, he had a backpack on him that he also soon threw to the ground. The hero stretched his body and turned to face Lando. “Sorry- am I too late for my appointment, Mr Photographer?”

“Hm, I don’t know…” Lando pretended to look at a watch on his wrist and tapped his foot impatiently. “I got an appointment with Green Goblin after this, I don’t think he’d appreciate me being late, he’s got a lot of bombs.”

“Eh, nothing I can’t handle. I’m your priority, right?” Spiderman walked closer to Lando and it took every fiber in his body to not ogle at the slight sway of the hero’s hips or how nicely the suit fit around his pecs and biceps. “Didn’t think I’d see you so soon, can’t get enough of me, can you?”

“What can I say? I’ve got an Instagram page that is just dying to see you”

“And it’s not you dying to see me?”

Lando gulped. “Nope, I just want my pictures.”

“Right.” Lando could swear he heard a smirk in his tone. “How do you want me anyway? Swinging, fighting, like one of your French girls?”

“Shut up, oh my god.” A furious blush coated his face as he imagined Spiderman undressing himself, laying on the luxurious sofa in the typical Titanic style. He shook his head to himself. “Right- how about we start with some swinging? We can see the sunset in the background.”

Spiderman chuckled, satisfied with the reaction from the Brit. “Alright, just swing around?”

“Try to look a little cool at least, I know that’s difficult for you.” Lando said and positioned the camera properly as Spiderman scoffed at his comment and launched himself in the air. 

With an acrobat’s grace, he twirled in the air using the high buildings nearby to catapult him higher and do some tricks as he free-fell down towards the rooftop again. He released his webbing at the right moment every time, swinging until his body was high in the air again and repeating. Lando watched in awe as his body twisted majestically and moved like it was blessed with a silk touch. 

The golden hues of the sunset painted the sky. In the distance, the bustling life of the city moved around them, but it was as though the two were stuck in their own world. Spiderman doing what he was made for, Lando capturing it. He adjusted the lens as the blur of red and blue spurred past him, the sight was truly inspiring and he was determined to catch the true essence of the hero in his photos. He continued to take all his shots, changing the angles and the scenarios multiple times before Lando was pleased. 

As the sunset began to fade and the last rays of orange disappeared beyond the city skyline, Lando took one last photo of Spiderman perched on the rooftops edge. The photo was from the side, showing the protective stance the hero held as he watched over the street below. He couldn’t help but feel privileged to witness it in person, to have been saved by him three times already and to be able to converse with him like he’s known him his whole life. His photos may not do him the justice he deserves, but Lando was selfish enough to be grateful he didn’t need to share this side of him with the world. 

He looked through the photos briefly, satisfied with what he had taken and put his camera area with a content sigh. Spiderman was now laying down on the floor, sprawled out like a starfish. Lando crept over to him and towered over him, a long shadow being cast by the last rays of sunshine. 

“You must’ve taken hundreds of photos then, please tell me you got something out of it?” He pleaded, his hands meeting each other in a prayer motion. 

“I’m not sure… we may need to go again.” He teased him.

“Kiss my ass, I’m not doing anymore than I already have.” He protested. “Now, lay down with me.”

“Say please! Just because you’ve saved my life a handful of times doesn’t mean you can’t use manners!”

“I’m not asking, muppet.” Lando felt a brief familiarity wash over him, which quickly disappeared when Spiderman had reached out to grab him and yanked him down to the floor. He yelped as his hoodie was tugged, a hand flying up to keep the bobble hat on his head. His body was leaning over Spiderman, hovering in a push up position as Spiderman’s hand was still twisted in his shirt. He thought he could feel the heat from his body and all he wanted to do was hide his face, because god knows how he was looking at the hero right now. “Hi.”

“Hi.” He said, a shyness overtaking him. 

“You have very pretty eyes.” What the fuck was he supposed to say to that? “It’s like the Atlantic and Pacific met and collided.” 

“Thanks, Shakespeare. I’d love to hear the poems you’ve written about me. Maybe on our fifth date?” Lando cheekily grinned, letting an overconfident demeanor take over. It was funny to him calling them dates because if these really were dates and this was the fourth, then he’d been saved ¾ times. 

“It’ll be sixth knowing you, there’ll probably be another point I have to swoop in and save you.” He said, finally shifting and moving the hovering boy so that Lando was laying next to him instead. Somewhat relieved at no longer being under the scrutiny of his gaze, Lando turned to watch the sky. 

It was dark now, the sunset gone and the darkness of the evening had settled in nicely. Lando frowned to himself while looking up at the night sky, the light pollution preventing the stars and planets from being seen. “It’s a shame, isn’t it?”

“What is?”

“That we’re here in the city where we can’t see the sky in all its glory.” His eyes harbored a twinkle as he spoke. “I’d love to go to a mountaintop or something just to see them.”

“Yeah… it is a shame.” He agreed. 

Together they watched the sky, his eyes could make out what must have been particularly bright stars and he focused on them. He watched planes fly over their heads and wondered to himself why Spiderman hadn’t left him yet. The sunset was gone, the photos were done, he had nothing else to stay for. 

If he was going to stay, then Lando was going to ask the burning question on his mind. “Can we talk about what happened, with my so-called investigation?”

Spiderman seemed to wince and Lando turned his head to look at him, seeing the hero slowly nod. “Yeah.. yeah okay, what do you wanna know?” He saw the hero shoot a web at his discarded backpack, pulling out a jumper. Spiderman put it on, his body convulsing suddenly from the cold. 

“I understood why you did it, what the whole point of it was, but I don’t understand why you couldn’t tell me the truth about it?” Lando had only been a little hurt by it, but with it mixed in with the lies from Oscar, he’d been beating himself up over it a little. “Do you not trust me? Because I understand you don’t really know me but I’d never jeopardise the mission on purpose-”

“I’m sorry, I really am. I thought I’d be keeping you safe by not telling you the truth about Logan and what it was for, I thought you’d stay well out of the way if anything were to happen.” He sounded truly apologetic and even though Lando had been upset, he’d already forgiven the vigilante the moment he was saved. “It wasn’t fair of me though to let you go into that blind with barely any information, you deserved to know.”

“Can you at least let me know what is happening with Doctor Octopus? Please? I understand he’s gone a bit crazy-”

“A bit is an understatement.” Spiderman interrupted suddenly and Lando shut his mouth. He heard the light huff from the hero and grinned to himself knowingly. “Did you hear about how earlier this year, Oscorp Tower caught on fire after an explosion?”

Lando nodded slowly, he remembered seeing it online as it was happening. A loud explosion had reverberated throughout the city and caused the well-known Oscorp Tower to set on fire, falling apart from the top. Oscar had been at home with him when it happened and they later found out that his lecturer, Doctor Octavius and his wife, had been involved with the accident. They were working on a compound called GR-27, created to cure a rare illness of which Norman Osborn’s wife had suffered from. At least, that was according to the leaked documents they received at the Daily Bugle. 

Oscar had explained the idea of it all in great detail using his smart words and Lando at the time had just nodded along like he understood most of it. From what he gathered however, the cure that was created was actually deadly and while working on the substance, it had caused an explosion in the Oscorp tower and subsequently killed his wife. 

He knew later on that alongside his teaching, Doctor Octavius stopped working with Norman and started his own company that worked towards creating prosthetic limbs in an effort to help people such as war veterans who were unfortunate enough to lose their limbs. After that, all Lando knows is the man showed up with four mechanical arms during one of Oscar’s lectures and from there, basically lost the plot. 

“I remember, his wife passed away, he left Oscorp, made his own company and went crazy?” Lando summarised in one. An oversimplification of the turn of the events, sure, but it wrapped it up quite nicely. 

“Well… not sure how much I’m supposed to tell you, but you promise not to tell anyone?” The hero’s hand came out with the pinky finger sticking out, Lando taking the finger into his own, the silent confirmation. “Considering you work for the Bugle, you already know about the GR-27 cure? I mean, your team did publish it.” 

“I’ll have you know, I actually had nothing to do with that article.” Lando responded smugly. 

“Shame, it was probably one of the only interesting ones they’ve ever released.” Spiderman quipped, the other boy couldn’t even deny it. “But anyway- Otto, his wife and Norman had been working on creating the cure since Norman’s wife died, obviously it backfired and ended up killing Mrs Octavius. Otto left the company and he and Norman had a very public falling out, with Otto accusing him of his cure being a death trap.” 

“And was it?”

Spiderman hummed in response. “GR-27 was also called Devil’s Breath, Norman had been experimenting with it and the side effects were… let’s just say that the obvious result is that it could cause death.” Lando grimaced upon hearing the alternative name to the so-called cure. “Otto went on to create his own company to help people have prosthetic limbs, but more importantly, to help himself.”

Lando’s face scrunched up in confusion. “But- he had all his limbs? What would he need it for?”

“Not every disability is visible, Lando.” He tutted at the British boy. “Otto was suffering with a neuro-degenerative disease that would eventually leave him paralyzed. His disease was quite fast moving… he started to rush his work, he made mistakes and he overlooked the flaws in favour of just having the work completed.” Spiderman sounded regretful, Lando felt a small shiver travel up his spine. 

“And that’s where the metal arms came from?” Lando asked, curiously. 

Spiderman nodded quickly. “And that’s where his erratic behaviour came from, the work was incomplete and the arms that he was supposed to control, had taken over his life. His own creation is controlling him and he doesn’t even fully realise it. All he could think about was Norman, everything was about Norman and what he did to Otto, to his wife. All that he wanted in life was revenge.”

“He lost his mind from his own work…” Lando concluded quietly, a sadness washing over him at the thought of someone feeling trapped in their own mind, unable to escape and drowning in their own distasteful thoughts. To be changed by your own invention, to have no control. Perhaps it wasn’t the worst of fates he heard of, but it was certainly bad enough that Lando would never wish it upon anyone. He felt sorry for him in some way, even if he had risked the lives of many.

“Yes- and while I’ve been trying to stop him from killing Norman and destroying the city, he’s decided to make me a target for getting involved.” Spiderman said lightly, like his life wasn’t being threatened by a madman. “But do you see now why I didn’t want you going after him? Anyone or anything that gets in his way of his revenge, he will do his best to destroy them.”

“I understand- I’m sorry for bringing all this up, I just wanted to know what I’ve gotten myself involved with.”

“Don’t apologise to me for that, I’m just glad nothing bad happened to you. If your friend Oscar didn’t report it, I’m not sure what would’ve happened.” Spiderman shook his head lightly, frustration seeping through him. “You don’t ever need to apologise for stuff like that, okay?”

“It’s just… on top of you not telling me the truth, I think someone close to me has also been lying about something.” Lando bit his lip, he was unsure why he was even bringing it up, but he felt like he couldn’t talk to anyone else around him about it. Oscar was well known for his mysterious behaviour and his hidden secrets, Lando thought no one would take him seriously. 

“Oh? Do… Do you wanna talk about it?” Spiderman asked politely. While Lando was positive that the hero had no actual care about his personal life, he just needed to get the feelings out. He nodded at him, before turning back to face the near star-less sky. If he was going to pour his feelings out, he wasn’t about to do it while looking Spiderman dead in the eyes. “Go on, get it out.”

He took a deep breath, and off he went. “I never thought much of it, you know? He was always quite secretive and he kept his personal stuff to himself, but he always told me more than he would tell anyone else. We’re like this-” Lando crossed his two fingers. “-and I’d give my life for him a thousand times over. He’s had two jobs and his own personal hobby since we’ve known each other and I found out recently he was actually lying to me about still having one of his jobs. It’s just- I know it sounds dramatic, but why wouldn’t he tell me that? It’s something so little and it’s not like he needed the job anyway, it wouldn't change anything and yet he still didn’t tell me, he lied to my face again when I asked him about the job. It’s such a little lie that’s turned into something massive and I can’t help but think he’s lied about a lot else to me, what else is he hiding? Just- he means so much to me and the fact he’s hidden this from me just makes me feel like I’m insignificant in his life. I tried to let it go, I tried to tell myself I didn’t care but my mind can’t stop going back to it.”

“And do you think there’s any reason he’s hiding things from you? Maybe he has his reasons?” Spiderman didn’t miss the way that Lando tensed where he laid. “Not to defend him or anything! But if he’s hiding it from everyone and not just you, it may not be a personal thing?” He suggested. 

“But it feels personal, I’m his best friend! I had to look after him the other day, he was so sick that he could barely move or stand up straight. He’s barely at home anymore and I know he doesn’t get anymore than 3 hours sleep at a time… I don’t know what to do. I care about him so much and I want to help him and be there for him, but how can I do it when he just won’t talk to me?” Lando ranted exasperatingly, his hands flailing around as he let his emotions free. 

“I understand, he’s been maybe letting his… other commitments got to his head and he maybe didn’t consider how you had been feeling too. Maybe he doesn’t realise that these secrets are hurting you. Have you- uh, tried talking to him? I think to resolve this, you need to bring it up.” Spiderman was sitting up straight now and looking down at Lando, disrupting the view of the polluted sky. 

Lando sadly laughed to himself, a bitter feeling rising in his chest and a sad exhale coming from his nose. “We kinda got this unspoken rule where I just don’t question what he does. He does his thing and I don’t question.”

“Maybe you should question it.”

Lando pulled a face. “What?” 

“Question it, just ask what you want to know. You’re never going to get anywhere without asking.” Spiderman suggested. 

“It’s not that simple, I told you, it’s been going on for years.” Lando retorted.

“What is stopping you? Realistically, what do you have to lose? Just ask, maybe that’s all you really need to do.” Spiderman said exasperatedly, his hands moving as he talked. His voice wavered at points and his gaze now stared at anything that wasn’t Lando. 

Lando supposed he was right, what did he have to lose? Sure, it could damage their friendship if they end up arguing and Oscar refuses to tell the truth, but whatever they have between them is already becoming damaged by the lies themselves. The fear of losing their bond chewed at his insides, churning his stomach relentlessly. If he didn’t bring it up, he was sure that they were doomed to fail soon. A surge of determination but hesitation surged through him, the weight of the confrontation looming over the balance of their relationship. 

“But what if it’s not that easy? To ask him something that we’ve both just danced around for years… I don’t even know how to approach it.” A frown tugged at the corners of his mouth.

Spiderman sighed, shifting uncomfortably where he sat and a shiver floating through his body. “But you deserve to know the truth. Just ask, Lando. High risk, high reward- right? You don’t get anything you want in life without a little difficulty.”

“I guess.” Lando murmured, his voice barely above a whisper. He allowed himself to shirt up right, adjusting the beanie on his head and trying to catch Spiderman’s eye. “I just don’t want things to get worse. What if he doesn’t want to tell me? What if it’s something I can’t handle?” 

“You should have more faith in m- him .” Spiderman spoke with an urgency. “He-he wants you to know if he’s not even trying to hide it anymore. If you won’t do it for yourself, then do it for me so I can have my sunset-watching buddy back, won’t you?” He gently nudged Lando’s shoulder with his own, easing the tense atmosphere with the light hearted humour. 

Lando bit his lip, the uncertainty feeling leaving him as he felt a weight being lifted from his shoulders. “I should just face it straight on…”

“Yes! It may be difficult, but all you need to do is ask.” Spiderman’s eyes softened and he looked at Lando, the white eyes of the mask finally meeting his own. “You can handle whatever this world throws at you.”

“Okay… okay! You’re right, I gotta go home now!” Lando rushed to his feet, stumbling a little as he did so, Spiderman was quick to follow after him and hold him by his arm to stop any injuries.

“You can handle everything except for gravity apparently.” One of the white eyes widened like the raising of an eyebrow.

“That doesn’t count as you saving me, by the way.” He narrowed his eyes. 

“Sure it doesn’t.” He tutted. Lando ran to his backpack across the roof, careful to make sure his camera was safe and secure in the backpack before tugging it over his shoulder. “Wait- you’re going to ask him right now?” The beady white eyes grew.

“Of course! Carpe diem, seize the day, all that shit!” Lando laughed energetically. “Thank you so much, Spidey, for the photos and the little emotional rant. I’m gonna go talk to Oscar-” it was only then that he realised it was the first time he admitted out loud just who he had been talking about. “-and maybe confess something of my own! See you soon!” He added cheekily. 

“Lando, remember to just ask-” Lando had already dashed for the rooftops exit, the door slamming tightly against him and Spiderman was left to the brash breeze of the night. “-me. Goddamn it.” The hero huffed, snatching the backpack and swinging away from the rooftop. 

Lando burst out of the hotel building, the receptionist lazily spouting something about not running in the reception. He didn’t care enough to stop and listen, he was a man on a mission right now. His eyes trailed to the sky sending a silent thank you to Spiderman before rushing across the road and heading into the flat. As he was entering, he looked up and saw the living room window was open, Oscar must’ve forgotten to close it when he headed out. 

Typical, now their flat would be freezing. 

He flew up the stairs of the building, ignoring the dull ache in his muscles as he took two steps at a time. Lando wanted to get in, attempt to cook something for them both and get their show ready to go, Oscar would surely be on his way home now and it was the perfect time to ask him. His heart was going erratic at the thought of finally knowing the truth, of finally knowing the mysteries that the Piastri boy held. Even if it took some buttering him up and convincing, he would finally know the truth.

Violent (and exaggerated) breaths escaped his body as he mounted the last step, trudging along to the front door of their flat. He took the key out of his pocket and tried to open the door, except it locked itself. Lando furrowed his eyebrows, and it seemed like Oscar was already home before him. No matter, he could make them something after their discussion. He unlocked the door and let himself into the flat, flicking on the light switch just in time to see Oscar fall face first as he ran out from the hallway to their bedrooms. 

Lando muttered a curse to himself as he closed the door behind him and quickly made his way over to help Oscar off the floor, the backpack sliding off his shoulder and landing on the floor with a thud. By the time he made his way across the room, the fallen boy had already scrambled his way back up from the floor and flashed Lando a bright smile, his cute bunny teeth making an appearance. Others around them always said that the toothy smile from Oscar was rare, but Lando’s was blessed enough to see it even in the smallest of moments, like right now.

Oscar’s usual naturally-wavy hair that always laid in a perfect style (that Lando definitely wasn’t jealous of) was sticking up in multiple different directions, he was wearing a creased t-shirt and a pair of goddamn shorts while their flat was as cold as ice. Lando could see the goosebumps trailing up his arms and raised an eyebrow. “Any reason why you decided to kiss the floor, just now?”

“Um- I tripped?” Oscar answered quickly. “How’d your meeting with Spiderman go? Did you get all the pictures you wanted?” The grin never left his face and he was bouncing lightly on the balls of his feet. He was acting strange, stranger than usual. 

Lando placed his hand on Oscar’s arm and used the other to remove the beanie from his head, tossing it behind him to the kitchen counter and ran the hand through the thick curls on his head, brushing them out. Oscar was breathing a little heavier than usual, his cheeks slightly flushed pink and his chest rising up and down a little rapidly. “Yeah it was fine, I managed to get some good pictures I’m going to edit later, are you okay? You seem a little…” His voice trailed off as a violent cold shiver ran through his body and his eyes darted towards the open window. 

The Aussie followed his gaze and let out a little ‘oh’ before going over to close the window, cutting off the trail of the cold air. He made his way over to the sofa and sat on the arm of it, his thighs on perfect display as the fabric of the shorts tightened around them. His arms were on either side of himself, resting on the arm and pushing his chest out a little. If Lando was a better man, he’d probably look away. 

“I’m all good, just got back from my run. If I knew you were coming back so soon I’d have picked something up for us. Listen- I’m all yours tonight, it’s about time we get a move on with Game of Thrones!”

Well, that would explain the harsh breathing, the flushed face and the fact he’s wearing shorts when their flat felt like winter had finally come (pun with Game of Thrones totally intended). Lando was delighted to hear he had Oscar all to himself tonight, he had the chance to ask the burning question that seared in his mind. He could faintly hear the accented words of Spiderman entering his mind, ‘just ask him’. 

“I thought I could try and cook for you later-”

“The only thing either of us can cook is pancakes and quite frankly, I’m sick of eating them.” Oscar interrupted with an eye roll. 

“-Anyway, I’ll try and cook something nice for us. If it fails, pizza it is. But I wanted to ask you something first, if that’s alright?” Lando said and made his way closer to Oscar. 

He was standing knee to knee with the Aussie now, looking down at him still sitting on the sofa edge. They were close, never close enough for Lando’s liking but enough to admire his features up close. If it was possible, the smile on Oscar’s face had grown wider before speaking. “Of course, we can cook something together so we’re both to blame at least. What was it you wanted to ask?”

“What…” What are you hiding from me? Why do you leave late at night and come home during the morning? What are you really doing during your ‘runs’, Oscar? Why did you lie for the last year about still working at the library? Why do you always randomly disappear? Why didn’t you just tell me the truth? Do you really even have an internship?

So many questions flew through his head, so many ones he could ask and finally get the answer to. But they wouldn’t come out his mouth, failing to escape him as a looming feeling of anxiety washed over him.

He couldn’t bring himself to ask. 

It seemed so simple, to just ask him what was going on, but the response is what he was dreading. Spiderman said he could handle it, could handle whatever the world threw at him, but he was wrong. He couldn’t handle the truth, because the truth would change whatever they have now. The truth could be scary, the truth could hurt. The truth could ruin whatever was left between them. 

Lando was scared, but he was also a coward. 

He could live like this, he’s done it for years already. 

“Lando, you there?” He snapped out of his thoughts and his eyes wandered to Oscar’s own. A look of worry coated his eyes and his smile had dimmed a little. “Don’t lose yourself in your head there. Just ask, Lando.”

‘Just ask.’ 

He couldn’t. 

He was sorry to Spiderman, for having the poor hero listen to his problems only to ignore any shred of advice he shared. ‘I’m sorry Oscar,’ for not being able to fix whatever it is between them right now, he wasn’t ready for it. 

“What do you think of maybe visiting my family with me after the New Year? My mum said she misses you, she prefers you over me.” While it was absolutely true that his mum loved Oscar, it was complete and utter bullshit to what he truly wanted to ask him. 

Oscar’s face visibly flattened, the perfect smile was gone and his eyes glossed over like a brick wall had come up between them. He hesitantly placed his hand out, Lando took it into his own and felt the nerves creeping onto him more and more as the seconds passed. “Are you sure that’s all you wanted to ask?” Oscar questioned, a tinge of disappointment prevalent in his voice. “There’s… there’s nothing else?”

Lando shook his head frantically, probably a little too quickly if he was honest. “That’s all, I just wanted to see if you’ll come with me.” He did his best attempt at a smile, although it probably came across more as a grimace than anything. 

The Aussie’s shoulders sank and he looked deflated, like life had been sucked out from him and he was left as a shell of a person. “Okay.” He said unsurely. “You’ll tell me if there’s anything else, right?”

“Of course.” Liar. 

Oscar slowly nodded and his hand squeezed Lando’s three times. “Yeah, I’ll come with you. Need to make sure your mum sees her favourite son, after all.” He said, a playful tone coating it but a smile didn’t quite reach his face. 

There was something unspoken there, a line neither of them felt they could cross, a wall that couldn’t be broken. 

Did he make the wrong choice?

“I’ll let her know then.” Three squeezes to Oscar’s hand. He was about to let go, but the hand tightened around his own. “Oscar?”

“While we’re on the topic of asking each other to do things… Would you want to go to Winter Wonderland together this year?”

Lando shrugged his shoulders lightly. “I mean, we always go with our friends, I thought we were still doing that?”

Oscar bit his lip and lightly pushed himself off the sofa edge, standing so close to Lando that chests were close to touching. His instincts told him to take a step back and give him room, but the tight grip on his hand told him otherwise. Lando’s eyes locked with Oscar’s from the moment he moved til the moment the younger was looking down at him. “I mean, we could maybe go by ourselves, you know? Enjoy some time together. I feel like we haven’t been able to actually do something nice together for a while.”

His stomach did twists and turns as he took it in, a sweet feeling washing over him and the sick nerves were nearly forgotten. The last two years, they had all gone as a group to Winter Wonderland, Charles always saying that they were a group and must have a group tradition. For Oscar to be willing to face the wrath of Charles must really mean he wanted it to just be them two.

Lando nodded eagerly. “Y-yeah, sure! I’d love to, you know I love doing anything with you.” Calm down, Lando. 

“I know you do.” He smirked, although the effect didn’t reach his eyes. “Now, are we going to attempt to cook?”

“Bake off?” Lando suggested with a bright grin. “I got a Victoria sponge cake recipe that’ll obliterate your muffins.”

“Oh, you are so on, Norris.” 

Oscar dropped his hand and moved away, the space in front of Lando an empty barren landscape now. His heart clenched and he pushed his feelings away, following after him as they both ran to the kitchen to steal the ingredients first. 

He couldn’t lose this over something as silly as a secret, he refuses to lose it.

Chapter End Notes

I hope you guys have enjoyed this so far! I have officially caught up to the prewritten work I’ve done and I’m now currently writing the next scenes. Next time we’re back, we’ll be in Winter Wonderland watching a cute little date!

Chapter 5: Chapter 5

Chapter Summary

Lando and Oscar enjoy some time at Winter Wonderland.

Chapter Notes

15k words… this one was a big one!

Unfortunately, updates will be slowing down now as I will be playing catch up with my qualification and i have six assessments due on the 23rd November, I prioritised this lol

I created a Playlist for this story in case anyone wants to check it out! I’ll be adding to it as I go along.

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Nestled within the heart of the bustling city London laid The Hyde Park Winter Wonderland. As Twilight descended over the city, the park became a shimmering haven of enchantment, enticing anyone who comes near its embrace. Its pathways were adorned with a mesmerizing tapestry of twinkling lights and props of Santa, reindeer and elves. 

Lando and Oscar walked in sync through the labyrinth of pathways, the bright lights illuminating the way forward and a soft sound of Christmas music played. It was their third time there, but it never ceased to amaze Lando how breathtakingly beautiful it truly was. The sound of children with their parents shouting and trying to rush past them became a distant sound as Lando turned to look at Oscar to the side of him. He stared in awe as the lights brightened his pale complexion. His hands were tucked into his coat pockets and his teeth were gritted tight as the cold wind blew past them. 

Lando rubbed his hands together to create some warmth, complaining to himself internally for not bringing any gloves and only wearing a hoodie (which may or may not be Oscar’s, he wasn’t sure until he noticed the lack of design on it), the big pocket of it doing next to nothing to keep his hands warm. They were already an hour behind their schedule since Oscar was back late from a shift at Webber Industries and Lando had assumed they weren’t going anymore. That was until Oscar had burst into his room and threw one of his beloved jumpers at the boy, demanding that they still go out. He had been in such a rush to get ready, he’d completely forgotten about wrapping up appropriately.

Oscar turned to look at Lando, meeting his gaze and he rolled his eyes with a soft smile. “I told you to grab the gloves from the side!” He said.

The older boy pouted, still frantically trying to warm his hands up. “You were rushing me!” He protested. 

“I literally said take your time, you’re the one who wanted to get here so quickly.”

“My god, it’s so fucking cold.” He whined, his nose and cheeks turning a crimson red. “And do you really wanna be stuck in the ferris wheel line when it gets busy later? No? That’s what I thought.”

“Fucking hell.” Oscar mumbled under his breath and suddenly he pulled his hands out of his pockets, removing the gloves and tossing them over to Lando. “Don’t say I never do anything for you.”

The older boy hesitated. “Won’t your hands get cold?” He questioned. 

“I got some very warm pockets unlike you, plus I’ll do anything to stop your whining.” Oscar responded in his usual stoic voice. 

Lando looked at the gloves and suddenly felt a pool of guilt building in his stomach. Nonetheless, he pulled the gloves over his hands and sighed in relief when the bitter cold slowly started to convert into a nice warmth. Without a second thought, his hand reached out to grab Oscar’s before it could retreat to the coat pocket, intertwining their fingers. He faced forward, even when he could see Oscar’s head turning to look at him from the corner of his eye. Nothing was said, but he felt like a thousand words had been spoken. 

When they first met, Oscar was never big on physical contact with people and he still wasn’t, unless it was Lando. Holding hands, hugging and cuddling in bed together were off the table at first, but the younger boy had started to reach out himself. It had meant the world to Lando and he took every chance he had to initiate some form of contact without overdoing it. 

They finally reached the entrance of the park and Lando was quick to drag them along to the concession stands, all he could think about since they left their place was a steamy cup of hot chocolate. He sniffed at the air as they joined the queue, moaning in delight when the rich smell of the drink filled his nose. Oscar squeezed his hand 3 times, pulling his attention towards him. 

“I can’t believe a hot chocolate has you making those noises.” The Aussie snorted. 

“Not just any hot chocolate, Oscar.” He said as he overpronounced his friend’s name. “Winter Wonderland hot chocolate.”

“You wouldn’t get like this over a Costa one?” Oscar raised his eyebrow.

Lando tapped his chin with his free hand, pretending to ponder on the question and lightly humming to himself. “Not quite, but if you bought it, it would probably taste a lot better.”

“Everything tastes better with you when I buy it.”

“It’s the sweet taste of someone else’s money.”

“You’re a scab, buy your own hot chocolate.” Oscar refused and moved forward in the queue. 

Lando frowned and looked up through his eyelashes at the younger, it was proven time and time again that Oscar was a sucker for his puppy eyes. “Osc, please?” He pleaded. “What will it take, do I need to beg? Do you want me to get on my knees and beg?” 

He made a bending movement with his knee, playing along with the narrative and laughing to himself when he saw the panicked expression on Oscar’s face. He took some pride in it once he saw his cheeps turning a deep red. “Do not get on your knees, oh my god.” Lando moved his knee again in a teasing manner, laughing maniacally when Oscar turned away from him and dragged him through the queue. “Fine, you can have your hot chocolate, you win.”

“I always do.” He responded smugly. It was their turn now with Oscar ordering their drinks and Lando standing there looking pretty. The younger dropped his hand to take the two drinks and Lando had to physically stop himself from reaching for the hand again. He handed Lando his one, smiling in delight as took a cautious sip and the burning liquid traveled down his throat. 

Oscar paid for their drinks and they trailed off towards the ferris wheel, both agreeing to head there first as the line was getting longer as the minutes passed. Holding the drinks in either one of their hands, Oscar was quick to slot their hands back together and give it three squeezes. 

He never understood the three squeezes, it sort of became a thing they did after the first couple of times. Oscar would squeeze his hand three times, like a hidden message he couldn’t decode. Instead of questioning it or ignoring it, Lando ended up copying him and returning the gesture now and again. 

He gave three squeezes back and hummed as he sipped the drink, the rich chocolate taste coating his tongue with a slight twinge of caramel, Oscar ordered it just the way he liked it. 

“Odds on that we end up seeing Max and Charles here?” Lando asked before taking a long gulp of his drink. 

Oscar tilted his head in thought. “I’d say probably not, they were at the cinema when I checked Charles’ story.”

“Thank god, the last thing I want is to run into them right now. We’d never hear the end of it for coming here without them.” Lando sighed in relief. 

“Charles would finally post the video of you at last year's party for revenge if he knew.”

“Let’s not talk about that!” Lando abruptly shouted, a few people turning to glare at them. A scowl took over his face as Oscar held an amused grin, his lips slightly pursing as he stopped a laugh from escaping. “It’s my job to embarrass you, not the other way around.” He protested

“But you look so cute when you’re worked up.” Oscar blurted out and Lando could imagine how they must look right now. The older boy there with a furious blush spreading throughout his face and Oscar who seemed surprised at his own words. The younger of the two moved his lips to speak, but was interrupted by a worker informing them it was their turn to get onto the ferris wheel. 

They rushed up the walkway, their footsteps echoing as they pounded against the metal planks and threw themselves into the ferris wheel carriage, promptly avoiding eye contact with each other. Their hands detached and Lando was quick to shove the spare one in the hoodie pocket. The ride continued moving, the carriage gently swaying back and forth as it started ascending towards the sky. For Lando, the slow moving journey on the ferris wheel was something he looked forward to every time he went to the park. It felt like a haven for himself, the carriage could usually fit four people and it was a time away from the loudness of the park and enjoying the great allure of the sky, even if the height kind of terrified him. 

Lando’s gaze shifted to the vast void in the sky, a few stars twinkling and winking at him as they created a beautiful canvas in the sky. It’ll never reveal the true glory and beauty it truly holds, not to the city. Either way, he was thankful to see a few more stars glistening than the night on the rooftop with Spiderman. 

As they sat opposite each other, their knees grazed each other and Lando had to stop himself from squealing each time he felt the light graze or bump. He coughed, clearing his throat before he spoke. “I always forget how beautiful the view is from up here.”

“It really is.” Oscar agreed, his voice slightly raising towards the end. 

“I mean the view below is just incredible, the lights just get better every year. And the sky, I wish I could see the swirls of the galaxy or something, mate. One day we’ll have to go somewhere that we can really see space, like a desert or something?” Lando pondered and turned his head to finally look at Oscar, the comment from earlier being forgotten about. 

Oscar was already looking at him.

Oscar was already looking at him and all Lando could do was stare right back.

The younger's lips were slightly parted, a small breath making an escape through the crevice and his chest rose and fell in sync with the intense moment, a thick air of tension rising between them. Lando’s gaze fixated on Oscar, entranced by the boy in front of him and time felt like it had come to a standstill. His eyes traced over the moles littered across his face and the part of his neck that wasn’t covered by the fabric of his coat. Lando felt the overwhelming urge to reach forward and move it so he could continue counting the beauty marks littered over his body. Within the depths of Oscar’s dilated pupils waged a whirlpool of emotions, the flick of the bright lights from the ferris wheel illuminating them. With their eyes locked and time unmoving, he felt like their souls were dancing between them. 

Unconsciously, they had both inched closer and closer, their knees passing each other and their legs intertwined, allowing them to move. For a split second, he thought he may have seen Oscar’s eyes flick to his lips and Lando felt his eyes slowly fluttering shut. They were close now but far enough away for Lando to want to reach forward and crash their lips together, the hot breath of Oscar brushing against his tanned skin wavered an unbearable feeling within. Lando dropped now empty hot chocolate cup to the floor and went to rush forward until-

Ding!

Lando threw himself back in his seat, the carriage swaying harsher than before. His eyes were still locked on Oscar who was blinking himself back to reality. Realising what the sound had been, Oscar’s hand disappeared in his coat pocket and pulled out his phone causing Lando to roll his eyes. Of course it was the goddamn phone ruining their moment. 

While Oscar was looking at his phone, Lando kept replaying the moment over and over again in his mind. What the fuck had just happened? One moment, Lando was babbling about the view and the next he was leaning forward to kiss him. He really needed to to get a hold of himself, they still had the rest of the evening to go. 

The Aussie frowned at his phone and Lando could see his nostrils flare as an exhale passed them. He wanted to bang his head against the carriage wall, he knew what that look on his face meant. “Oscar?” Lando’s voice cut through the silence. “Do you have to go?” His voice wavered, a sickeningly sad tone coating his words like poison. 

Oscar hesitated, his eyes flickering between his lit up screen and Lando’s disappointed face. A determined look came over him and he shoved the phone back in his pocket, not sparing it another glance. “No, it’s just my boss asking if I could go in to do some lab work tonight. I don’t have to go.” Oscar said. 

Lando felt relief wash over him and almost let out a content sigh, now slouching down in the rickety seat with a relaxed stance. His knees grazed Oscar’s again, but this time he didn’t let himself get lost in the feeling. “Thank you.”

“For what?” Oscar asked, baffled and taken aback. 

Lando shrugged his shoulders nonchalantly. “You know- for staying? I appreciate you not rushing off to help them out. Although, it’s unfair for them to ask you to come back after you’ve not long left there.”

“You know how it is- they’re working on a big project right now and they really need me.” Oscar defended them. 

Lando raised his eyebrow suspiciously. “They really need an intern? No offence since you know I love you and all but they have some of the best scientists working there. Surely they can cope without you and do it themselves?”

“Let’s just say I’m really good at my job.” Oscar responded and he shuffled his body to the edge of the carriage, overlooking Hyde Park again. “But don’t worry, you’ve got my undivided attention tonight.” His stoic voice said. 

The rest of the ferris wheel ride was spent talking about how their lives were currently faring, feeling like they had barely spoken about how they were both getting on recently. They talked about their studies, with Oscar obviously excelling like he usually does and Lando having five assessments due that he hadn’t even made a start on. They talked about how their friends were, the weird tension between a few of them (cough Alex, Lily, George and Carmen cough) and how Logan very obviously had a crush on Arthur who had come to visit, the youngest of the Leclerc brothers. Speaking of Logan- Lando had messaged him a real apology for his behaviour and now they got along amazingly well, much to the delight of Oscar.

They had a mini get together with everyone at Charles’ place which had been nice to catch up with everyone. It’s where they saw Arthur on his yearly visit to see Charles and had to witness the weird tension between their friends. The Christmas party had now been fully planned, playlist ready and it was all in motion. 

The carriage descended to the ground and the two made their way out and towards the ice rink. Oscar had pre-booked an ice skating session for them right before they showed up, they were lucky to even get a space. Although, Lando was sure that Oscar wouldn’t have minded skipping this part of their evening. The elder boy could skate, nothing more, nothing less, but Oscar? He was like Bambi on ice, limbs flailing everywhere and falling over every chance he had. He once told Lando during their first year of knowing each other that he had fallen over and broken his wrist when he was thirteen during a school trip to an ice rink. 

Lando had booked ice skating for that same year and decided to help him overcome his fear and teach him how to skate.

The good news was the fear was gone (for the most part) and the bad news was he still couldn’t skate. 

It was a work in progress. 

Lando took a seat on one of the benches while Oscar went up to show their booking confirmation and grab their skates. The younger boy returned with a grimace on his face and gently passed Lando his pair. “I feel sick thinking of the kind of people that have worn these skates.”

“You say this every year, mate. Buy your own pair next year!” Lando said. 

“And I highly doubt they’ve been cleaned since the first year we came here.” Oscar groaned and removed his shoes with a huff. Lando followed his lead, put the skates on, fastening them tightly and stood up to give them a feel. He walked up and down, a distant memory of him trying shoes on as a child and his parents telling him to test the shoes came to shore. Lando smiled briefly at the memory before looking back at Oscar, who was now ready too. “No recordings of me falling over this year, got it?”

“Don’t fall over and then we won’t have a problem.” Lando grinned widely. 

Oscar pulled a face and raised his hands in defeat. “Maybe you should be a better teacher then, how can you keep letting your student fail?”

“Oh I’m sorry my free lessons mean jack-shit to you, Osc. I’m so sorry, would you like me to teach you an axel? Maybe even a lutz? Salchow?” He raised his hands above his head like a ballerina and entered the ice rink, twirling around on the ice to emphasise his point.

“I’m a firm believer that you have no clue what any of those words actually mean.” Oscar retorted and held onto the edge of the ice rink with a firm grip, his teeth gritting together as he placed the first foot onto the ice. His first quickly gave way and slid to the side, the tight grip barely holding him up. Lando watched, bemused as he watched the younger of the two struggle to hold himself up. 

After watching him for thirty seconds and seeing his face turn bright red as children zoomed past him without any trouble, Lando felt some sympathy and glided over to him. His arm came out, allowing Oscar to pull himself up on it and hold him. “And I’m apparently the damsel in distress.”

“You find one thing I’m bad at and you make it your whole personality.” 

Lando snickered deviously and shuffled slightly across the ice, careful for Oscar who was still leeching onto his arm in fear of falling on his butt. As they started to move, the younger boy’s grip tightened as he tentatively placed one foot in front of the other. A nervous laugh from him filled the air and a loving smile blossomed on Lando’s face, feeling like a knight in shining armour. Oscar’s face flushed with embarrassment as children skated past them like borderline professionals. 

“Don’t pay attention to them. Pay attention to us, focus on where you’re placing your feet and copy me.” Lando reassured and gestured his free hand towards their feet. Oscar looked down sheepishly, slowly copying the movements of Lando. Slowly but surely, they were working their way up the length of the ice rink with steady movements. “You’re doing better than you think, Osc. Keep focusing on us.” 

Oscar’s gaze drifted from the floor and to Lando’s face, their eyes meeting amidst the twirling couples and the playful laughter of those around them. The faint scent of hot chocolate and cinnamon drifted from the concession stand nearby, adding to the cozy atmosphere between them both. His grip lessened on Lando, allowing the elder to move his arm and wrapped it around the back of Oscar, the light touch of his hand resting on him. Even with the lays between them, Lando felt like his hand was burning at the touch. He guided Oscar through the icy terrain, the younger gaining bits of confidence the further they went. 

With his nerves quickly fading, Oscar was feeling a new found confidence and picked up the pace, sometimes slipping a little but Lando was right there to prevent him from falling. Lando watched in glee as the younger found his footing, the sound of Christmas music filling their ears as they reached the other end of the rink. 

They continued like this, the hand on Oscar’s back gently guiding them and keeping a hold on the inexperienced boy. After making a few laps around the rink, they were getting by with little to no incidents. However, a few young children came barrelling past them and barged past Oscar. His balance faltered and it sent him stumbling, the sound of ice scraping underneath his feet and a yelp escaped him. Seems his quick reflexes did nothing good for him right now. 

Lando was quick to react, his hand on his back quickly moved to his side, holding him with a tight grip. The hold forced Oscar to be twirled around and brought into Lando’s protective embrace. Oscar’s back was now facing the floor of the ice rink, halfway bent backwards as Lando held him with a tight arm. The two looked at each other with wide eyes before a little laugh filled the air, which then erupted into a loud cackling from Lando and the younger shook his head with a smile. 

“Well, when I envisioned you in some compromising positions, I never saw this being one of them.” Lando admitted out loud and by the time he realised what he said, it was too late to take back his words. He muttered ‘fuck’ to himself and gently lifted Oscar to his feet. 

“What other compromising positions have you thought about me in?” Oscar questioned with an eyebrow raised suspiciously. 

“Not like that! Get your mind out of the gutter!” It was definitely like that. 

“Whatever you say, Lando.” Oscar responded, the tone clearly indicating he didn’t believe the Brit one bit. “Come on, I’m done skating after that.” He grumbled. Lando guided them off the rink, returning their skates and popping their regular shoes back on. 

The two boys strolled around the rest of Hyde Park, trying the rides before deciding to play at the game stalls. They had spent a solid five minutes arguing over who was going to play for the multiple games before Oscar sighed in defeat. Lando looked over at the game stalls, lined up next to each other. He rushed towards a duck game stall, Oscar following behind him excitedly. 

Oscar paid for their goes and they took their places in front of the plastic toy water guns. Lando raised the toy gun up, closing one of his eyes like he was looking through a sniper rifle. His tongue poked out of the side of his mouth and he focused on the floating plastic ducks in the bowl of water in front of him. The rules of the game were simple, they each had to shoot as many ducks as possible, the higher the number, the better the prize. 

The plastic ducks bobbed in the water and the two young boys focused intently on the targets. “Let’s see if your shooting skills are as good as your reflexes then.” Lando quipped. 

The younger boy laughed. “I’ve always got a few tricks up my sleeve, I’ve gotta leave you guessing sometimes.” He retorted. 

Winter Wonderland was bustling with life around them as they took their aimed positions, Lando still looking like a professional shooter while Oscar lazily held the plastic gun in his hands. The stall attendant rang the bell, signaling for them to start. 

Now, Lando wouldn’t say he was the competitive type, unless he was up against Oscar. Which is exactly why he was pretending to be an army soldier shooting down as many ducks as he could, pretending to feel the recoil of the toy gun every time he shot. Oscar had raised an eyebrow judgingly, barely looking as he successfully hit every duck he shot at, which the same couldn’t be said about Lando. 

After the final shot, the stall attendant rang the bell again to signal that the game was over and Lando wiped away a drop of sweat that had formed by his eyebrow. The younger nearly doubled over in laughter at the fact Lando had worked up a sweat over a duck stall game. 

Their scores were tallied up and unsurprisingly, Oscar won the game. Lando pouted as he was informed that he didn’t hit enough ducks to even win a prize. The Aussie had enough to choose a small prize (the stall was totally a scam if his points only accounted for a small prize) and he ended up choosing a plushie of three ducks together. 

“What are you calling them?” Lando prompted as the two walked away from the stall, heading towards a basketball game which was a few stands over. The elder’s hands were clasped together behind his back, leaving over to look at the three ducks in Oscar’s slim, pale hands.

The younger boy shrugged his shoulders, his hands holding the plushies loosely. “I don’t really think they need names.”

“Sure they do! You’re their mother duckling, you need to name your babies. You chose them, this is your fate.” Lando insisted as they walked in sync. 

A deep cold breeze blew past them at that point and Oscar’s shoulders shuddered in response. He took a look at the ducks again. “Cold, As and Fuck.”

“You’re actually so boring.” The British boy snorted and Oscar smiled lightly, shoving the three-duck plushie into his coat pocket. Lando briefly noted the gesture, he wanted to make sure those ducks ended up on Oscar’s bed that night. 

The duo stepped up to the basketball stall game, Oscar once again paying for Lando’s turn and opted out of playing this time around. “Are you sure you don’t want to play?”

“Eh, I’ve already gotten myself a prize, I don’t want to end up being a mother of any other plushies tonight either.” Oscar replied, leaning against the side of the stall as he watched the stall attendant let the basketballs fall from their hold, signaling the game was ready to start. 

He had ninety seconds to score as much as possible and he was determined this time to win a prize. The stall attendant blew her whistle and Lando frantically started throwing the basketballs. He managed to score a few, but most shots had been unsuccessful. Listen, Lando was good at golf, not shooting hoops. 

Oscar watched in a stunned silence at the poor attempt of basketball. “This one’s for you.” Lando winked at Oscar with a seductive grin and turned back towards the hoop. If he had stared at the younger for just another second, he would’ve seen how the Aussie’s cheeks turned a bright red and eyes turned into hearts. Lando pushed his might into the throw and tragically, the ball missed the hoop. In fact, the ball had ended up nowhere near his hoop and actually ended up more towards the couple playing next to him. 

He sheepishly grinned at Oscar with a nervous laugh. “Wow, I’m so impressed, Lando. No seriously, where did you get all that talent?” Oscar said with a deadpan tone. His arms crossed over his chest as Lando sulked silently to himself, only scoring a measly four shots and thus, could not win a prize. 

Lando started to walk away and turned his head to the side to see if the younger was following him, frowning when he noticed the boy was still at the stall. He stalked back over and situated himself next to Oscar, noticing he was handing some cash over to the stall attendant. “I thought you didn’t need any more prizes?” He raised his eyebrow.

“I don’t.” He responded simply. 

The basketballs came rolling down and Oscar quickly grabbed the first one, shooting it and Lando watched stunned as the ball fell into the hoop. The Aussie continued throwing them with perfect precision, not missing a single shot as he rapidly threw them. The stall attendant’s jaw dropped, probably not expecting someone with such a perfect ability to show up today. 

The ninety seconds were over and Oscar dusted his hands off. “Which one do you want, Lando?” Oscar pointed to the huge plushies tied up to the top of the stall. 

Lando whipped his head to look at Oscar with admiration in his eyes, meeting the younger’s gaze and they were locked in. He managed to tear his eyes away, biting his bottom lip to stop a wide grin from taking over his face. Lando scanned the plushies in front of him before looking at a light brown teddy bear, it was huge and it looked so soft. Oscar followed the direction he was looking at, smiling knowingly. “Can we have the big brown bear, please?”

The stall attendant wordlessly used a pole to pull down the big brown teddy bear, handing it over like she was caught in a trance. Oscar gracefully handed it to Lando, who immediately snatched it from the younger’s hands and squeezed the teddy bear tightly. “Oh my god, he’s so big, I love him!” He exclaimed with a laugh, skipping away from the stall with Oscar following quickly on his heels. “We have got to use your weird abilities more often. We could’ve won so many of those games by now.”

“Then our flat would be overrun by a bunch of humongous teddies, it would literally look like a children’s shop.” Oscar retorted in a stoic voice. 

“Well, I personally think that Cold, As, Fuck and Freddy need more friends at our place now.”

“Freddy?” Oscar’s eyebrow raised curiously.

“Freddy Fazbear? You know, the animatronic bear?”

“Actually, I don’t want to know.”

As the two were walking around and looking for their next destination, a loud murmur overtook the crowd and people were pushing left and right. “Why the fuck can’t people say excuse me? Oscar, I’m about to start throwing hands.” Lando grunted as he elbowed someone pushing into the side of him. He glanced at Oscar and the younger one stood there with his eyes narrowed looking into the distance. “Oscar?” He called out. 

A loud crash was heard and that’s when the screaming started. The cries of families echoed throughout Hyde Park and then the running started. If Lando thought he was being pushed before then he severely underestimated it, because now he was close to being toppled over and trampled on as everyone frantically ran to the park’s exit. Lando’s shoulders were thrown around and he could barely keep himself up, he looked all around him and Oscar was gone. 

Another loud crash was heard and he could see sparks coming from the ferris wheel, a loud mechanical sound groaned from the machinery. Panic rippled through the crowd with ear-shattering screams and suddenly, Lando could see the mechanical tentacles of the formidable Doctor Octopus wrapping around the ferris wheel’s frame. His body and controlled arms moved along the ride, overlooking the crowd with a sinister smile. The metal of the machine wrapped under the grip of his metal arms, breaking apart the ride. 

Lando felt a sickness wave over him and his eyes searched the crowd around him for Oscar. They needed to get out of there, now. He pushed as many people back as he could, his legs carrying him unconsciously as he screamed out for the Australian. The big teddy bear had long been abandoned as he zoomed past the growing crowd. “Oscar? Oscar!” Fear rushed through his body as he worked himself further into the depths of Hyde Park. It was an odd sight, everyone rushing towards the exit in a flurry of mayhem while Lando had to work his way further into the crowd. 

Doctor Octopus worked his way down the ferris wheel, damaging it even further as he went. Once he reached the ground, his long tentacle arms crushed the abandoned concession stands. One of his arms picked up a sharp piece of broken wood and launched it towards the crowd, his laugh raining over them. Lando managed to move out of the way, others not so lucky as the wood sent them falling to the ground. 

His breathing became rapid and his chest was rising up and down like it was a struggle to breathe. Lando managed to wrangle his phone out of his pocket and called Oscar, still shouting his name. The crowd was dispersing the further he got into the park, the smell of sweat and fear disappearing. Meanwhile, Doctor Octopus launched a hard blow to the bright and big Christmas tree, causing it to fall backwards and catch on fire as the electrics were fried. 

Lando finally made it out of the crowd, huffing in relief while his phone was still ringing. “Come on, Oscar. Please pick up.” He pleaded to himself. The phone hung up and he was quick to redial it, he needed to find him now and get them out of there, before Doctor Octopus could notice he was the only person still lingering. 

The sound of Oscar’s ringtone could be heard and Lando’s ears perked up, rushing towards the direction of the sound. His heart sank however when he saw that Oscar’s phone was abandoned on the floor near the ferris wheel, a photo of them both cuddling covering the screen as it rang. Landio slowly bent down and picked it up, dread washing over him. Oscar was god knows where, he lost his phone and he lost Lando. 

While Lando stood lost in thought, the moment was shattered by an ominous creaking sound cutting through the air, sending a shiver down his spine. The ground beneath his feet rumbled and soon enough, the sound of metal on metal reverberated throughout the park. Lando’s heart raced as his head turned to the source of the noise, his head rising slowly as he looked at the ferris wheel tilting dangerously forward. The supports were breaking and the air filled with the scent of rust, the lights cutting out and the smell of fried electrics filled the air. 

As the demeaning sound of the metals’ protests intensified, Lando felt a surge of panic grip at his chest, freezing him like a deer caught in headlights. Time seemed to crawl, freezing everything around him. Every fiber of his being was screaming at him to run for safety, but his limbs felt heavy and stuck to the ground. 

Just as the shadow of the descending ferris wheel threatened to swallow him whole, a streak of red and blue movement caught his eye. With a sudden burst of movement, Spiderman appeared swinging effortlessly from the structures ahead and appeared in Hyde Park. The web-slinger’s hand flicked with perfect precision, his webs latching onto the ferris wheel and attaching to the floor behind it, slowing its descent. Lando’s survival instincts finally kicked in, forcing his body to sprint out of the way as the ferris wheel eventually made its way to the ground with a loud bang. 

His body fell to the floor, rolling along the ground and he groaned as the harsh ground thudded against his back. Lando turned on his side to face the ferris wheel on the floor, the concrete broken around it and dust settling in the air. Through the dust, he could see Spiderman swinging over and the hero landed in front of him, perched on his toes with his knees out. 

The beady eyes glared at him and Lando could sense the irritation coming from him. However, the eyes softened and his stance did too, looking at Lando now with concern. “What are you doing? You were supposed to leave with everyone else. You need to get out of here, now!” Spiderman insisted and his hand reached out, pulling them both up from the ground. “Why didn’t you leave?”

“O-Oscar.” 

Spiderman tensed up and his eyes widened at the mention of the name. “What?”

“It’s Oscar, I turned around and he was gone. I don’t know where he is. Please, you have to find him!” Lando begged and tears started to well in his eyes. He wasn’t often a crier, but the thought of something happening to Oscar made him feel wild. The masked vigilante relaxed under his gaze, almost like his concern was lifted from his shoulders. 

“Lando, I’m sure he left with everyone else and just lost you in the crowd. Please, you need to leave now. Let me take you somewhere safe before he realises.” Spiderman said and gestured for them to leave. Lando looked over his shoulder and saw Doctor Octopus destroying Hyde Park around him, a path of destruction behind him, his figure seen briefly through the clouds of concrete. 

He shook his head frantically. “No, you don’t understand! His phone was by here, he had it before I lost him! It doesn’t make sense for Oscar’s phone to be here unless he’s still in the park.”

Spiderman hesitated for a second before grabbing Lando by the shoulders and bringing him closer. “We’re leaving, come on.”

“No!” He protested, screaming as Spiderman tried to grab him. Lando thrashed around in the hero’s hold, but he had a very tight grip. “I’m not leaving, not without Oscar.”
