“Lando, I’ll find him, I promise you.” Spiderman said in a reassuring tone. “But right now, you’re in front of me and I need to get you to safety.”

Before Lando could protest, Spiderman’s grip tightened around the British boy, his fingers digging into the fabric of his hoodie as he prepared to launch them skyward, away from the danger. However, the hero’s senses tingled with a sudden chill running down his spine as he realised what was looming closer. His head snapped up to scan the area with a sense of urgency, but before he could react, a glint of a metal arm materialised through the thick dust. Within seconds, one of Doctor Octopus’ arms shot out from the darkness, snaking its way between the two figures. 

Lando’s breath caught in his throat as the cold metal arm wrapped around his torso, squeezing him with a deadly force. Pain seared through his body as the grip tightened more and more, threatening to crush him. A sharp, piercing scream of pain tore through the atmosphere, echoing as he was wrenched from the grip of the web-slinger. Desperation etched itself on Lando’s features, his eyes wide with fear as he was dragged helplessly towards the sinister figure of Doctor Octopus. The villain’s robust laughter sounded throughout Winter Wonderland. 

As Lando struggled against the grasp of his arm, the villain watched in satisfaction as he squirmed and hissed in pain. Tears streamed down his face, whether from pain or fear, he wasn’t sure. Spiderman took an attacking stance, waiting for the right moment to move. 

“Hello, Spiderman.” Doctor Octopus sneered, his voice dripping with venom. The sinister smile on his face ignited a burst of anger in the hero. “Just the person I wanted to see. Your interference has really started to bug me, I thought it was worth finding you myself.”

“Otto, whatever you’re doing, you need to stop!” Spiderman tried to reason, but his grip around Lando tightened and he let out a yelp of pain. “Stop!”

“You thought you could get away with trying to ruin my plans? You’ve been spying on me, sending your little playthings after me. All of this could’ve been avoided if you just stayed out of my way. My men have been watching you for weeks. Watching you and this little thing-” His arm viciously shook Lando who felt so close to blacking out. “-flirt , you haven’t been very careful have you?”

“Let him go, this is between me and you, Otto. I can’t let you hurt Norman. Those arms are messing with your mind! I’m sorry you lost your wife, I truly am, but I can’t let you do this. You have to understand that.” Spiderman said, his hands up in a surrender motion and he took tentative steps forward. Lando’s head flopped to the side as he tried to keep his eyes trained on the hero, his vision had black dots and a slight ringing tone buzzed around his head. 

A moment of clarity seemed to reach Doctor Octopus, the mechanical arms grip loosened around his torso but his body felt too heavy to move. His posture seemed to relax like the words had reached him. As quickly as it came, it disappeared and the tight death-like grip came back and Lando cried out. Spiderman had gotten closer again. “No, his goddamn Devil’s Breath killed my wife. I can put a stop to him, I can save the city from his so-called cure, but you keep sticking your nose where it doesn’t belong. Tell me, Spiderman, do you want to watch the people of this city die?”

“Doctor Octavius, please!” Spiderman shouted in desperation. “We can stop him from releasing it, but not like this! You are better than this, those arms are controlling you and you’re sick.” 

“Norman will stop at nothing to help his own greedy needs, Spiderman.” Doctor Octopus angrily spat and as he stood on his mechanical arms, they pounded against the floor stomping over towards the hero. Spiderman fell back and sprung his body into the air from his hands, launching himself away. “No, no, Spiderman is wrong! We are doing the right thing.” His head turned to the side, seeming to have a conversation with himself, low mutterings could be heard. “Yes, I know, the plan is already in motion.”

“This isn’t you, this isn’t the Otto that everyone knows. Please, put him down and come with me, we can fix it.” Spiderman pleaded, but his body had contracted into a pouncing position, ready for anything. 

“You haven’t had to watch Norman do all of this for years, sacrificing everyone and everything to get what he wants, willing to destroy it all just to favour his plans. He will stop at nothing, and I will be the one to end that.” 

“But isn’t that what you’re doing now?” Spiderman pointed out almost fearfully, the reaction of the villain to be awaited. 

Lando tried his best to pay attention, but he feared it was too late for him. He was trapped within the grips of a madman who was clearly being controlled by his own invention. Lando had heard the stories from Oscar many times, the whispers about him, the talking to himself. While Lando was busy trying to keep himself awake, he didn’t notice that the supervillain had turned to fully look at him, a look of recognition plastering his face. His black goggles stared at him and Lando felt true fear grip at his heart. “Oh… well, I recognise you , alright. This- this is a very interesting turn of events.”  

Fear bubbled underneath his skin and part of Lando wished he could be put out of his misery already. A snarky retort was caught on his tongue, the reality of the situation dawning on him and realising it probably wasn't the best idea to mock the guy that had your body wrapped in a giant tentacle. 

Regret clawed at his skin as his mind drifted to Oscar. Lando knew he made stupid decisions, but he let love cloud him on this one. He ignored Spiderman trying to pull him to safety, he was stubborn and this was his own fault. Blame ate at his mind and consumed his every thought, wishing that he could’ve seen Oscar one last time. 

“Let him go, Octavius.” Spiderman warned. “He has nothing to do with this.” He urged, the plea filling the tense air. 

“No.” Doctor Octopus declared with a sinister snicker. The cold refusal echoed through the air, no emotion lacing his words. “I don’t think I will.” 

The free mechanical hands raised and Spiderman tried to shoot his webs at them, but they had little to no effect. His arms swiftly moved with an unnatural agility, dashing out from Hyde Park with a half-conscious Lando with him. The mechanical appendages carried him with grace as they destroyed the city around him. 

Spiderman sprung into action, webs shooting in a desperate attempt to catch them. The cold wind whistled past him as he propelled his body in the air to pursue the madman and Lando, the night sky taunting him as he tried to catch up. The figures of them were getting far away, and quick. 

He should’ve known they were being watched, that Doctor Octopus had been keeping tabs on them. Spiderman can only imagine that the villain showed up at Winter Wonderland to seek out Lando himself. It was a clear calculated move, he figured out Spiderman’s weakness and used it as the perfect advantage over him. He let the British boy get too close, and now he was going to pay the price for it. 

“Karen, call Logan!” Spiderman shouted in an act of desperation to his suit’s interface, his body gliding through the air as his eyes trained on the two in front of him. The programme had responded with ‘certainly’ before calling the American. It rang two times before he answered. “Logan!” He exclaimed in relief. 

“Good evening to you too, bro. How’s the date night going?” Logan asked with a suggestive tone and Spiderman was sure that the other was raising his eyebrows with a smirk. “I wasn’t expecting to hear from you soon if I’m honest, is it not going well?”

“Would you class Doc Ock attacking Hyde Park, taking Lando with him and me having to chase them down as ‘going well’?” Spiderman responded through gritted teeth. He retrained his eyes to the target and saw a car flying towards him with debris falling off it. The hero quickly dodged out of the way with ease, this could get ugly quickly, he needed to hurry. 

He heard shuffling over the phone and the loud clicking of keys to a keyboard. “What street are they heading down?”

“We’re at Curzon Street, he’s got to be heading towards Oscorp Tower. Have you got access to the cameras there?” Spiderman asked quickly, scrambling out of the way of a chunk of concrete that came hurtling towards him. “I need you to find out if Norman is still there.”

A soft hum and more rapid fire clicks of the keyboard filled the sound over the phone. “I’ve still got them from the Green Goblin days, dude. Let’s see… oh god…” 

“Logan? What is it?”

“They must be working overtime or something, man. The building is packed full of people, including Norman.” Logan responded with a dreadful tone, his static voice booming in the hero’s ear. 

“Shit, I need you to cause an evacuation in the building now. Get a hold of Commissioner Button and tell him to get the emergency services to Oscorp Tower, I’m going to stop Doctor Octopus once and for all.” Spiderman said through gritted teeth, a clear determination in his voice. 

“Don’t let anything happen to him.” Logan’s voice came through as a clear warning. Nothing further needed to be said, the name needn’t be uttered. 

“I won’t.” The silent promise hung in the air between them. 

Suddenly, the voice of his suit’s interface rang clearly in his ears. “Incoming call from Mark Webber.”

“Shit- Logan, do what you can, I’ll try and buy you some time, I’ve got to go.” Spiderman quickly said before addressing the suit’s interface. “Karen- accept the call.” Logan didn’t have the chance to respond, the call hanging up as the incoming one was accepted. 

“Kid, care to tell me why Seb has angrily texted me saying his cafe was just nearly trampled on?”  

“Mark- hey!” Spiderman responded with fake excitement. He watched as Doctor Octopus bashed through a building and rubble started to fall around the people inside. Spiderman quickly shot two webs inside the building, pulling himself inside. As his body soared through the room and his body faced the ceiling, he shot a flurry of webs, preventing anything further from falling. He soared out of the other side of the building and continued on with his chase. “We may have a bad situation on our hands! How’s Vettel?”

“He’s fine, you know how he is. He said his garden bee houses were nearly destroyed though. What’s going on?” Mark questioned and Spiderman could hear the faint sound of people talking in the background, he could only imagine that Mark had been interrupted during an Avengers team meeting. “Do you need back-up?”

“Let’s just say, Otto finally decided to enact his revenge tonight.” Spiderman responded, feeling out of breath. “No- Seb’s retired for a reason, I can handle it myself.”

“Are you sure about that? I’m watching it on the news now, wait… who is that he’s got?” He asked with concern laced in his voice, there were brief gasps from the other people in the room with him. “Is that-”

“It’s Lando, Mark.” Spiderman exclaimed exasperatedly. “He’s got Lando, I need to stop him now. He’s heading to Oscorp now with him.”

“You know what you need to do, kid.” Mark’s voice came through, a stern tone to his voice. The topic had been brought up a lot, Spiderman was positive that the old Otto was still in there, that he could still be saved from his own creation. Mark on the other hand was a firm believer that there was no coming back from whatever he did to himself. “ There’s no fixing him now, I know you want to help him, but the goal is stopping him. We’ll figure the rest out later.”

Spiderman knew he was right, even if there was still any of the true Otto left inside him, his own creation was taking over more and more. Many innocent lives had been lost due to the villain’s revenge tactics, to his random attacks and now he had Lando. Sweet, innocent, kind and clumsy Lando whose only crime was wanting to help Spiderman. Mark was right, he knew what he needed to do. 

“I wish you were here, Mark. I- I wish we had done this together. You always knew what to do.” Spiderman uttered, his voice tinged with sorrow. 

“I can’t be there right now. But you- you’re more than capable of this, you have it in you to do what is necessary.” Mark reassured the worried boy. “I can get Seb, just say the word.” 

“No- you’re right. I can do this. I’ll stop him, I’ll save Norman, I’ll save Lando.”

“We’re all rooting for you here, kid. You may even leave a good impression on the rest of them.”

Spiderman gasped. “Will I finally get to meet the Avengers?”

“We’ll be on the first flight over, but I will say, they’re not as impressive as me.” Mark responded smugly and it managed to tug a smile on Spiderman’s face, until he saw that Doctor Octopus had now reached his destination. 

“I need to go-”

“I know, call me if you need Seb. Good luck, kid.” Mark interrupted with a knowing tone. “Give him hell, go save your man.” He hung up and silence filled his ears, ringing around his head like a dull bell. 

He built up enough momentum to launch himself high up into the air, steering himself in the direction of the tall building until he landed on one of the tinned wide windows. Spiderman looked down at the ground far beneath him, relieved to see the police cards and ambulance vans arriving the same time as him. He directed his gaze to the window in front of him, the beady white eyes of the suit squinting as they focused on it. Inside were at least six people working on their projects. Spiderman tapped the window frantically, grabbing their attention and pointing downwards with his finger to motion leaving the building. They seemed to understand to some extent as they started piling towards one of the back doors. 

While Spiderman started his journey running up the building and shooting his webs to gradually pull himself up it, Doctor Octopus’ mechanical arms smashed through the building’s structure to pull them up the side of the building. Lando felt woozy and his limbs were flopping around everywhere. He felt a bit of anxiety growing in his stomach and he was sure he was very close to throwing up. The whole way to the tower, the British boy had been in and out of consciousness, his neck stiff from being thrown around everywhere. 

He couldn’t believe the mess he got himself into. One moment he was enjoying the quality time he was spending with Oscar and the next he had a metal arm wrapped around him, soaring through the sky with a madman as he made his journey to kill Norman and potentially Spiderman. He wasn’t too sure how he could explain this one to his friends, although he imagined they were already on every news outlet. 

They finally reached the top of the tower, Doctor Octopus’ arm went straight through one of the windows and they found themselves in an empty office. While Lando’s head lulled to the side and rested on his shoulder, his eyes briefly caught a family portrait photo on the desk. There was Norman Osborn, with a woman and a young boy, presumably his family. One of the arms stomped on the desk, splitting it in half and the picture landed on the floor. A big crack had gone through Norman’s face.

How ironic.

“Norman~” The villain’s voice called out, stalking through the building as he barged down the door to the office. The laboratory outside it was completely empty, Lando could see the remains of projects that were abandoned, most people likely abandoning the building once the news of Doctor Octopus arriving came. 

Doctor Octopus destroyed the work stations, sparks flying as the arms came barrowling down on them. “Where are you, Norman? You can’t hide forever…”

Lando would love to give him a snarky remark, buy Spiderman some time to catch up with them, but nothing in his body would allow him to. Perhaps it was the crippling fear he felt, maybe it was the tight hold on his body, or maybe it was how close he felt to fully passing out. No matter how hard he tried to push some words out, his body protested with a survival instinct. 

Just as an arm came down to hit the last workstation, a shout came out and a body began crawling out from under the space. Doctor Octopus cackled loudly as Norman Osborn tried crawling away from the hero, his breaths becoming rapid as he frantically tried to escape. The villain had a toothy smirk growing on his face, his metal tentacle shooting out and wrapping itself around Norman’s ankles, yanking him into the air and hanging him upside down. 

“Otto! Great to see you, old friend!” Norman chuckled nervously, his face turning red as his body was brought closer to Doctor Octopus’ face. 

The villain used his normal hands to pull the black goggles off his eyes, looking at Norman with nothing but contempt. “Old friend? Old friend? Do you really think that’s what you should be calling me after what you did to my wife?” Doctor Octopus angrily spat, the fear evident on Norman’s face. The grip on both people trapped in the mechanical arms tightened, Lando didn’t have it in him to even react anymore.

“Otto- it was a freak accident, surely you don’t blame me for that still?” Norman asked in disbelief, his hands coming up in defense of himself. “I’m so sorry for your loss, but that wasn’t my fault.”

“She died creating your little death cure, creating something that you knew would kill people.”

“I never knew it would kill people, Otto. I never would’ve created it if I knew what it would really do!” Norman shouted in an act of desperation, thrashing around in the metal arms hold. 

“Liar!” Doctor Octopus sneered and the arm thrust upwards, smashing Norman into the ceiling above them. He let out a yell and Lando audibly gasped. The arm lowered again, still wielding a now injured Norman. “You will suffer the same fate.”

“Four arms, two holding hostages, two keeping you up and zero to protect against a little spider.” A voice came from behind them. 

“What?” Doctor Octopus exclaimed and his body was hit by a fierce force, the arms letting the two bodies go with the sudden shock of the hit. Lando hit the ground with a loud thud, groaning from the pain in his back. He rolled to his side, squeezing his eyes shut before opening them and seeing Norman was already limping towards the exit, trying to make his escape. 

However, Doctor Octopus was quick to recover and one of his arms reached out, smacking Norman into the wall and his head banged against the concrete. The man collapsed to the floor, his head rolling to the side and Lando watched in horror as he laid there still. Whether or not Norman was dead, he wasn’t sure. What he did know was that he wasn’t about to make his escape while the villain was still up and moving. 

Spiderman stood next to Lando, his chest rising steadily as his eyes stayed on the villain who had quickly recovered from the attack. His gaze shifted to Lando, who nodded slightly to signal he was okay. Spiderman stared for a few seconds longer before addressing the threat. “Sorry to crash the party, Doc. I’m quite offended you didn’t book me for your entertainment tonight.”

“You-” A metal arm sprung out which Spiderman was quick to dodge with ease. “You have been nothing but a thorn in my side. How can you say you stand for justice but allow this pathetic excuse of a man to walk around? Norman Osborn is a killer!” The man was relentless, his arms shooting out in an endless flurry of attacks. Spiderman’s agility allowed him to dodge the assaults. 

Spiderman managed to find an opening and swung a hit to Doctor Octopus, sending them both away from Lando. The British boy felt like he needed to do something, anything to help. He found himself crawling on his stomach towards one of the lesser destroyed workstations, his arms moving in front of him to pull him forward. Lando hissed in pain as debris moved under his stomach, his torso had already been in pain from the tight grip the arm had held on him. 

The hero and villain fought around him, Spiderman doing his best to keep the fight away from Lando. When his eyes flickered over to where the British boy had collapsed and saw he was gone, panic settled in and Doctor Octopus managed to grab him, throwing his body across the room. Spiderman crashed into the glass walls of the laboratory, glass shards falling all around them. He was quick on his feet and sprung back into action. 

Lando looked behind him when he heard the crash, his heart sinking when he saw Spiderman go through the glass wall. He carried on moving quickly, surely there was something he could use to help out. Perhaps there was a weapon of some kind or just anything he could use to hit Doctor Octopus. Before he could move any further however, he felt the cold metal of the arm wrapped his torso again and he muttered a quick curse to himself as he was lifted viciously into the air. 

“Enough!” Doctor Octopus yelled and Spiderman stopped in his tracks as he saw Lando was caught in the deadly grip once again. They were standing dangerously close to the tall tinted windows and if Spiderman attacked now, god knows what could happen. “How about I present you with a choice, Spiderman?” Venom laced his voice as he spoke.

“Ooo, a party game? What are my choices?” Spiderman taunted and the villain growled with a grimace. If looks could kill, Spiderman would be a dead man. 

“Oh, well it’s not an easy one, I will admit. You see, I know you’re all about saving the city and its people no matter what. You’re all about putting the bad guys away and saving the good people who truly deserve it. But I also know that you’ll also do anything to save the bad guys, no matter what they do. So, here’s your choices. Do you save the innocent boy who unfortunately got caught up in this? The boy who holds a special place in your heart? Poor little Lando Norris?” The British boy in the metal arms grip sucked in a deep breath, goosebumps rising over his skin as the villain muttered his name like poison. 

He should’ve known that Doctor Octopus would recognise him, Lando had spoken to him many times when he’d be waiting outside Oscar’s lecture for the Australian, back when he was just Otto Octavius. Otto had been kind, a man willing to teach everything he had to offer and even spoke to Lando as his equal. That was until his grief consumed him and that’s where they found themselves now. His life being threatened by the former professor. 

“Or do you save Norman, a killer, the creator of devil’s breath and the Green Goblin himself? The one to kill so many and will kill even more for his own selfish gain?” 

Spiderman stood there in shock, presented with what felt like an impossible choice to make. He needed to be quick on feet and decide how he could save the both of them before anything could happen. His eyes flickered over to where Norman was lying broken on the floor, his next moves were absolutely crucial. 

“Will you save Norman and defeat me to fulfill your hero role, or will you save Lando for your own heart?” Doctor Octopus urged, smiling in delight as he watched Spiderman looking back and forth the two boys with conflict stuck in his movements. “What will it be, Spiderman? Will you be selfless or selfish?” 

The villain gave Spiderman no more time to think about his decision because in the next second, his arm busted through the big window and threw Lando’s body out of the tall tower. One of the other arms reached out to grab Norman simultaneously and he crawled out of the building himself behind Lando’s falling body and made towards the roof. 

Spiderman didn’t even think twice before he ran towards the open space and went after Lando, his body free falling through the air as it pushed back against it with its natural force. There was a lot of space between them and Spiderman was determined to reach him, leaning his body further down to try and reach him quicker. His heart ached as he saw Lando’s panicked eyes staring at him.

Lando was pretty sick of rooftops and tall buildings, if he was honest. It felt like some sick joke that a deity was playing on him, feeling no shame for putting him in so many situations where he’s either falling off roofs or being thrown out of a building, descending through the air. He couldn’t help but feel everything was leading up to this, like it was preparing him for this moment, but nothing could’ve prepared him for the moment he accepted this was it.

Even though he knew Spiderman was closing in, looking right at him with those focused beady white eyes, he felt no hope for surviving this. The city was hushed around him, the bright lights dulled and the traffic and shouting were gone. All he could hear was the pounding of his own heart. 

He knew how the stories went, people like him weren’t meant to be saved. All this time, he’d joked that he was the damsel in distress, when really he was just the catalyst in Spiderman’s story. He was a character in a story where survival wasn’t an option, he’d been shown it many times. Lando was supposed to start an anguish in the hero, to cause a great conflict in him between good and bad and cause a greater movement in the world's story. As Lando fell through the sky, his hair and clothes flapping from the harsh winds, he had finally learnt the consequences of being too close to the city’s hero. 

His pulse quickened as he noted how long he’d been falling, realising that they must be close to hitting the ground and Spiderman still wasn’t close enough to reach him. 

With a heavy heart, he closed his eyes and smiled as he thought about Oscar for the final time. The image of Oscar’s smile rested on his eyelids, engraved in his mind for his last moments. His sweet little laugh echoed through his mind, their fleeting touches, their hands intertwining, holding each other with such care, all their special moments floated to his mind. As he got closer and closer to the floor, he regretted never confessing his love for the boy who held his heart like it was made of glass. 

‘I love you, Oscar Piastri. Forever and always, you own my heart.’ 

An arm wrapped around his waist and pulled him flat against a chest, Lando’s eyes flew open and he was face to face with Spiderman. They barely just missed the floor as Spidrman’s wrist flick, a web quickly shooting out and attaching to the building behind them and their bodies glided through the air before safely landing on the ground. Lando’s legs collapsed underneath him, Spiderman’s grip on him still there and bringing them both tumbling to the ground. 

His arms wrapped around Spiderman’s neck and he embraced the hero tightly. A few tears fell down his cheeks before he realised what was happening and Spiderman returned the hug tightly. “You will never know how thankful I am for you.” Lando cried, burying his face into the crook of the hero’s neck. “You have saved me too many times to count and I can never repay you for it.” He spoke with relief, short rapid breaths escaping him as his heart began to calm down. 

“Lando, you will never need to repay me for this. I- I don’t know what I’d do if I lost you, I will always be there to catch you.” Spiderman reassured and his tight hold loosened, the British boy took this as his signal to depart from the hug. His hands were still clasped behind Spiderman’s neck, still leaving them close together. 

“But why? I’m just another person, I’m just another person in the city to save, the same as Norman. He’s a world famous entrepreneur and I’m just Lando.” Lando responded in disbelief, he couldn’t understand why he was so important to the masked hero. “You could’ve saved Norman, why me?”

Spiderman’s hand reached up from its place on Lando’s torso and rested on Lando’s face, causing him to lean into the reassuring touch. His thumb caressed Lando’s cheek with a soft touch. “You are so much more important to me than you will ever realise, Lando.” Spiderman whispered to him, the kind words for their ear only. Lando’s lips parted at the words and he felt a weird feeling in his stomach, just who was Spiderman really?

Lando heard a shout in the distance and it pulled the two out of their little bubble, he looked over Spiderman’s shoulder and the two saw paramedics making their way towards them. Spiderman turned back towards Lando, the white eyes of the mask had softened. He knew he had to go. “Go save the city, Spidey.” Lando smiled softly.

Spiderman took Lando’s hand in his own and gave it three squeezes, detaching himself from the boy on the floor and quickly ran back towards the building and worked his way up. As the paramedics approached him and started prodding him, looking for any signs of injuries, he watched Spiderman like the graceful thing he was with admiration seeping through him.

Perhaps Lando was wrong, maybe he was there to teach Spiderman another lesson in life. Maybe he was the damsel in distress after all. 

Spiderman quickly worked his way up the building, reaching the roof where Doctor Octopus awaited his return with the still unconscious Norman. The man was sporting a few cuts over his body, his suit was torn in multiple places and it looks like the villain had his fun while Spiderman was away. Doctor Octopus turned to him with a sly smirk, tossing the frail body of Norman to the side. His body rolled closely to the edge of the rooftop, Spiderman was quick to shoot his webs to keep him from rolling anymore. 

“Oh, you have a little look in your eyes. Tell me, Spiderman, did you manage to save him?” Doctor Octopus shouting over the noise of the harsh winds. Spiderman could hear an odd noise, he looked up at the sky and saw a helicopter flying over the scene and down the side of the building, likely air-lifting some people to hospitals. While he was distracted, Doctor Octopus took this as his chance to attack, his metal arms slashing through the air with a malicious intent. 

His spidey-senses were quick to alert him of the incoming danger, throwing himself out of the way and shooting a web to the villains face. He stumbled slightly as Spiderman swung around him, working his way back around to swing-kick him in the chest before he could recover. “He’s safe from you. Enough of these games, we end this now, Otto.” Spiderman said in a tone laced with fury, his white eyes narrowed as they engaged in a series of attacks. 

The two went back and forth for what felt like hours, throwing their might into the attacks. Spiderman’s soft agility against the hard and aggressive stings of Doctor Octopus. The villain was like a formidable force, but Spiderman weaved his way through the attacks with little to no problem as anger soared through his bloodstream like a drug.

One of the mechanical arms managed to get too close, snatching the mask that concealed him. As the mask tore away from his face, his brown hair flopped back down over his face, framing his face near perfectly. A bright red smear rested on his lips that were parted and his eyes simmered with anger as they stared down the villain. As Doctor Octopus looked at the young boy in front of him, he instantly recognised the figure. 

“I should’ve known it was you… always so self-righteous during your lessons and always talking about the greater good. My brightest student… at one point I felt so protective over you and now look at you.” Doctor Octopus tutted and the metal arms dangling the torn mask in front of Spiderman, taunting him with his own reveal. Like a light bulb lit up over his head, his smirk only grew. “No wonder you had such fond affections for that boy, I thought you were smarter than letting him get involved though.”

“Stop it.” Spiderman warned, his eyes only growing with anger. The villain had a triumphant glint in his eyes, thinking he’d already won. He thought he already knew the hero’s weakness, but this? This was far better than what he had ever imagined, this could destroy Spiderman once and for all. 

“You were under my nose this whole time, you and that goddamn boy .” The man spat. “I took you under my wing, I taught you everything you knew for the past few years, and this is how you repay me? You use that very knowledge against me?”

“You did this to yourself, Otto. You have no one to blame but yourself. This is your last chance to step down before it’s too late. I’m already offering you more than I should.” Spiderman offered the last stroke of sympathy in his body left for the madman. 

Doctor Octopus let out a rumbustious laugh. “Do you really think you can stop me? You can’t defeat me, you won’t let yourself. I know how strong you really are, how much you hold yourself back. While I was observing you, I was so curious as to why you were holding back your strength…but I suppose I know why you’re holding yourself back now, it’s for him, isn’t it? You don’t want him to know the true monster you can really be.”

“Enough!” Spiderman shouted, his voice echoing in the air. His firsts were balled together and he was biting his bottom lip enough to bleed.

“You’re holding yourself back for some boy who doesn’t love you.” Spiderman flinched and Doctor Octopus knew he had hit the right spot. “You will forever be choosing between Lando and the city, all because you don’t want him to see bad qualities inside you, you will never tell him the truth of who you are.”

Before he could say another word, the unmasked hero shot his webs with perfect precision at the villain, backing him up against one of the electric boxes and pinning his mechanical arms to it, rendering him powerless. Doctor Octopus’ eyes widened in surprise and shock and before he could completely register that his arms were pinned, Spiderman was now looming over him with a vengeance in his eyes. The glow from the outdoor lights illuminated his face partially, revealing the stained blood and determined look on his face. 

Spiderman grabbed him by the front of his outfit with a great surge of strength, his first yanking him as much as the webs allowed him to, and his fist collided with Doctor Octopus’ face. The hum of the electricity box hummed in the atmosphere as Spiderman let loose the control he had over himself. Something cracked under the hit and the villain already felt blood pooling down his face. Another hit. Again. Again.

Again.

Again. 

Again.

He thought Spiderman had been holding back before and he was right, because the pain and velocity he was feeling from the continuous attacks were nothing short of death-blowing hits. His barrage of strikes landed reverberated in the silence, only the sounds of Doctor Octopus’ groans filling the air. 

In the pulsating heat of the moment, Spiderman’s world narrowed down to a tunnel of red as his fist propelled with a relentless fury. The beats of his heart and the rhythmic movements of his hits were in-sync, like they were intertwined and meant to be. His heart with an endless bound of anger.  His voice, although strained, cut through the atmosphere like a hot blade. “Never utter his name again, you will never touch him again.” Spiderman declared through heavy breaths, the words carrying so much more weight than the blows ever would. 

The face of the madman bore the visible marks of the onslaught from Spiderman, broken teeth, bruised eyes and blood pooling from crevices and cuts. His fist stopped moving, his breathing calmed as he looked at Doctor Octopus with rage. The man looked close to death, his chest barely moving as he sucked in weak breaths. 

He dropped his grip on the villain’s shirt, the man falling to the ground with a huff, Spiderman stayed towering over him with a willpower to speak. “Otto, you were once a kind man. The last few years I knew you as the man who was willing to help those around him, the man who was grieving his poor wife, the man who wanted to help everyone around him. Look at what you’ve let yourself become, you’re a shell of the person you used to be, your own creation controlling you. For so long, I believed you could be redeemed, that I could save you from this fate, but the only person who can help you is yourself.”

“You… you can stand there pretending to be all high and mighty, but deep down you’re just like the rest of us, Spiderman. You have an ugly evil resting in your heart, you just proved it so nearly letting me die at your own hands.” Doctor Octopus heaved, a raspy tone coating his words as he struggled to let the words loose. 

“I’m not like you, I won’t let myself become like you. I won’t let you hurt anyone else, you’re going away.” Spiderman muttered, barely above a whisper as his body relaxed and the rage he once felt started to fade away. 

The villain weakly laughed. “I should’ve known my brightest student would turn on me, you’re just like the rest of this city, you were always out to get me.”

“Turn?” Spiderman responded exasperatedly. “I worshiped you, I came to London to be taught by the great Doctor Otto Octavius, the one with the brilliant mind, the one always looking to help others, the one who never gave up!” Spiderman shouted and tears started to fall down his cheeks silently. A loud roar of thunder was heard from the sky above, unleashing a rainfall upon them, mixing his tears in with the world’s own tears. “You were everything I wanted to be, and yet you gave it all up and for what? To dedicate your life to hunting a man down? For creating your arms that control you?”

“Y-yes, you’re right! The interface was affecting me, we can fix it together! Please, let me go and we can work on this… together.” His normal hand reached out in a surrender, the rest of his body unmoving. 

“I’ll make sure you get the best help they can offer.” Spiderman said with a deep sorrow and turned to walk away. 

“No!” Doctor Octopus protested, the hero stopped in his tracks. “If they put me away, they’ll take my arms! I’ll be trapped within this useless body, I’m losing more function every day, you need to understand. Please- don’t let them take me away!” The villain pleaded as the rain ran down his face, the blood moving with it. 

“It’s for your own good, Doc.” Spiderman looked down and saw the remains of his mask on the floor. He bent down and delicately picked up his torn discarded mask, his fingers tracing the holes with weariness. He clutched it tightly before pulling it over his face to at least cover some of him, he raised himself from the ground again with his shoulders back, the weight from the battle pulled away from him. “I hope you can heal there.”

Spiderman reached to where Norman was, removing the silk webs he was ensnared in. He picked the man up, cradling his limp body in his arms and ignored the yelling of Doctor Octopus as he began his descent down the building. The cries and pleads sounded through the air, the callings of Spiderman’s true name carried away by the wind. 

Lando looked up at the building and smiled to himself when he saw Spiderman diving through the air with Norman in his arms, no villain following after him. The sick feeling that grew in his stomach from the moment Spiderman left finally subsided, his body relaxing. He turned to the side of him, the paramedics were still checking him over, it turns out Lando had a few minor cuts on his stomach from crawling around, but other than that, he came out unscathed. The police had also questioned him a little, asking why he was there and caught up in the mess. He answered the questions as best as he could before they finally let him be. 

He was currently sitting on the curb, the road long closed off due to the attack from Doctor Octopus. Lando had his hood up and his gloved hands stuffed in the hoodie pocket. The rain was pouring down on him, but he didn’t have it in him to move. 

Lando wasn’t too sure what to do, Spiderman was off dealing with other things now and Oscar was nowhere to be found, plus he had the younger boy’s phone with him. His heart ached as he thought about Oscar, perhaps lost and scared with no clue what was happening, no way to contact anyone. Lando tried asking around if they had seen him, but there were a lot of people with wavy brown hair and weird accents around apparently. 

The police offered to take him back to his flat which he was seriously tempted by now because for all he knew, Oscar was waiting at home for him. He waited for around five minutes longer as he contemplated what to do. As he begrudgingly lifted himself off the floor and ignored the ache in his bones, he heard someone call his name from the distance. 

Lando lifted his head and looked around him, he couldn’t see anyone he recognised. He clocked it down as imagining it until the voice called him again, this time he looked behind him and saw the familiar figure of Oscar running towards him, his wind-swept hair flying everywhere and his body moving like his life depended on it. Lando’s legs were moving before he could even realise, his mouth opening wide with a great smile. “Oscar!”

The two boys met in the middle, Oscar’s arms instinctively reaching for Lando’s torso and his big hands resting on his back. Lando’s arms wrapped around the younger boy’s neck as they collided, swaying together under the pale glistening moonlight and the little droplets hit them. Lando, overwhelmed with the events and emotions of the day, felt his legs turn to jelly and he collapsed to his knees. Oscar followed after him, not caring if their clothes would be dirty from the ground. 

Lando let out a cry, the tears falling down his face relentlessly as Oscar let go of Lando’s waist and his hands came up to rest on the elder’s face. He cupped the boy’s head with such tenderness and care, wiping the tears away with the pad of his thumb. Oscar licked his lips slightly before leaning forward, leaving a kiss between Lando’s eyebrows and resting their foreheads together. 

“ O-Oscar…” Lando hiccuped and one of the soothing hands on his cheek moved back down to his waist, cradling him like a delicate creature. He looked through his lashes at Oscar, seeing the boy was still looking at him, Lando found a weak smile grew on his face. “I thought I fucking lost you, I turned around and you were gone! Your phone was on the floor and then Doctor Octopus was there, Spiderman showed up, Doctor Octopus grabbed me and took me all the way up that fucking gigantic building! He threw me off the top floor and I just realised that I-”

‘I realised you would’ve never known how much I truly loved you.’

“-that I… I never got to see you one last time.” Lando finished his ramblings, his love could wait for another day. 

“Lando, I turned around and you were just gone, my phone was gone and I had no way to find you. I went back to the park and no one was there. I managed to run to an electronics store to try and contact you, I saw you on the news with Doctor Octopus and I ran here as fast as I could. Just- just come here.” Oscar exclaimed and pulled the boy flat against him, their knees either side of each other as they held each other tightly. “I never want to let you go again.”

They embraced for what felt like hours, the rain slowing down and eventually reaching a stop. Lando moved his hand to pull the hood of his jumper down, his hair moving and revealing a little cut on his forehead. Oscar traced the cut with his thumb, frowning as he looked at Lando. “Are you hurt anywhere else?” He asked with a gentle tone, taking Lando’s hands into his own and sliding the gloves off them to check for any injuries. 

Lando shook his head lightly. “Not really, there’s a few grazes on my stomach but nothing more, I’m okay, Oscar.” He sniffled and looked at the younger boy in front of him, loving soaking through his chest and his heart yearned for more. While he was looking at him, he noticed a slight cut on Oscar’s lip, still fresh with a little bit of blood falling from it. Lando tore his hand from the Aussie’s grip and wiped the blood away. “What happened here?” Lando questioned with a worried tone. 

“Nothing- when I tried to get back into the park, a lot of people were pushing me back and I ended up falling over, nothing serious.” Oscar brushed it off lightly, going back to handle Lando’s face, tilting it to the side to look for anything else on him. “Can you show me your stomach? I just want to check if it's all okay.”

“Whatever you say, Doctor McSteamy.” Lando snorted, attempting to ease the mood between the two. It worked to some extent, as Oscar rolled his eyes and tried to fight off the smile forming on his face while Lando pulled up the front of his top and hoodie. 

The older boy flinched at the cold touch from Oscar, butterflies forming in his stomach as the hand carefully traced the wounds there. If this were anyone else, Lando would feel exposed, but he felt nothing but safe in Oscar’s arms. He watched Oscar’s face distort in anger, biting his own injured lip. Once the hand retracted, Lando pulled the clothes back down with a shiver. “Lando?” A quiet whisper filled the space.

“What is it?”

“I thought I lost you, it felt like the world had crumpled around me and all that was left was the ghost of you. Y-you looked so broken when I saw you on the news and honestly? If you died, I would literally go out of my mind, I can’t do it. I promise you now, I won’t let anything or anyone ever hurt you again.” Oscar promised with a set in stone tone, taking his hand and giving it three squeezes. “I’m tired of nearly losing you, I need to keep you safe.”

“Oscar…” Lando returned the three squeezes. “I’m right here, you didn’t lose me. I’m okay.”

“But what if something worse had happened to you?” Oscar counteracted, concern laced within his words as he addressed the elder. “You keep getting caught up in all this shit and I’m left to worry about whether or not you’re going to survive it this time. You will never understand the grief I felt thinking you were possibly already dead, that it was too late…” His voice cracked with a heavy heart.

Lando gave him a sad smile, his ungloved hands coming up to rest on Oscar’s cheeks and he had to resist every primal urge in his body to lean forward and collide their lips together. Everything in his body screamed at him to do it, to give it a chance and he might see that perhaps the feelings were returned. Moments ago when he thought he was about to die, his only regret in life was never telling Oscar his feelings for him and living out a true life together. 

But it wasn’t the right time. 

“I’ll stay away from Spiderman, no more sticking my nose in places where it doesn’t belong. It’s me and you, forever and always.”

Oscar nodded in relief and closed his eyes for a few seconds. He pulled himself up from his knees, Lando doing his best to follow with his wobbly legs. Oscar wrapped his arm around Lando’s torso, the boy slightly hissing in pain when he touched an open wound through the fabric. “Can we go home now?” Lando asked in a soft voice, breaking the tranquility of the moment, a vulnerability evident in his voice. 

“Of course we can, can you walk okay?” Oscar looked down at Lando’s legs, seeing them shake as they tried their best to keep the poor boy up. “Actually- don’t answer. Remember when I had to carry you home from the club on my back?”

A mischievous glint filled Lando’s eye and he let out a loud laugh, remembering the time he had gotten so drunk that Oscar had to carry him on his back all the way home, doing so without any complaints. “Are you offering it again?” He asked hopefully. 

Oscar removed his arm from Lando’s torso and bent his knees a little, presenting his back. “Jump up before I change my mind.” The Aussie groaned. 

Lando giggled as he geared himself up to jump, his hands pushing down on Oscar’s shoulders and he launched himself up and wrapped his legs around the other boy’s waist. Oscar’s hands immediately grabbed Lando’s thighs tightly, adjusting the position and Lando felt his thighs tuning sensitive in the boy's grip. He wrapped his arms around his neck, resting his chin on Oscar’s shoulder and whispered in his ear teasingly. “Nobody’s taking them away from you.” One of his hands came down to rest on Oscar’s own, which was gripping his thigh like he was holding on for dear life. 

Oscar, who had stuttered out a few intelligible words with embarrassment, loosened his grip on his legs, causing Lando to slide down his back a little and let out a loud yelp. Once he was satisfied Lando learnt his lesson, he pulled him back up by his thighs and gripped them tightly again. “All good?”

“All good.” Lando responded and closed his eyes as they began their journey home.

“You know, Cold, As and Fuck are in my pocket if you wanna see them again.” 

“Oh my god, our sons!” Lando exclaimed and shuffled so he could free his hand and reach into the coat pocket, pulling out the plushie with the three ducks. “We lost a poor soldier along the way.”

“Freddy’s gone? Awh, I guess I can get another one for us.” Oscar concluded, already thinking of the array of plushies he could fill their flat with. 

“No son left behind!”

The two walked away, the occasional laughter of them could be heard as they went further and further down the street. The unspoken confessions lingering between their souls could wait for now. 

Just a little longer.

Lando and Oscar go to the Christmas party, things get a little messy.

A white shirt was thrown across the room, crumbled up and smacking against the wall with the sound of heavy footsteps pounding against the wooden floor of the flat. “I look stupid. That’s it, I’m not going!” 

“Lando, you’re going, stop being dramatic!”

“No! This whole outfit just doesn’t go, do you really want me showing up looking like an idiot?” 

“I mean… looking like one will make no difference to what our friends already think of you.” Oscar’s teasing voice came, his body on the sofa shifting from the direction of the fallen shirt and instead faced the boy stomping around the place. 

Lando made an angry noise to himself as he trudged back into his bedroom, throwing the wardrobe doors open as he frantically searched for another outfit to wear. His hands pushed through the array of shirts, huffing in frustration when he saw nothing that satisfied him. 

It was the 22nd December, the long-awaited night of the annual Christmas party had finally arrived and the weather outside was brutal enough now to finally match the season. Small glistening snowflakes fell from the twilight sky, melting as they hit the already wet floor outside. The sound of the traffic and bustling city life could be briefly heard, the music inside the flat doing its job to barely drown it out. 

Lando had been counting down the days for the party, especially since the incident at Winter Wonderland with the madman called Doctor Octopus taking him captive and then proceeding to throw him off the top floor of the Oscorp Tower. And before that, he had nearly got caught up in being caught by Doctor Octopus' goonies. And he had been meeting with Spiderman on multiple occasions. 

His life had been hectic, to say the least.

He needed a bit of normalcy in his life right now after his near death experience(s), and this party? This was exactly what he needed, it needed to be perfect . Which is why Lando felt like he was going insane looking for the best outfit while Oscar had his outfit all ready. 

The British boy had been so preoccupied with his life that he failed to notice that even though he had found Oscar the perfect outfit, he hadn’t to sort his own one. 

“Come on, you’ve got plenty of clothes! I’ll help you find something to wear.” Oscar called out and followed the elder into his room, the discarded white shirt in his hand. His eyes landed on a wild-looking Lando who peeked out from behind the wardrobe doors. The younger boy’s eyes drifted to his shirtless body, his tongue poking out as it wet his lips, he quickly shifted his gaze back to Lando. “Whatever you wear, you’ll be the best dressed there.”

“You’ll just make me wear a hoodie or something!” Lando whined, turning back to his wardrobe and in a comical fashion, began to launch the clothes out of his wardrobe in a flurry. 

Oscar leant against the door frame with his arms folded over his chest, his eyebrow raising as he watched Lando attack his wardrobe. “Have some more faith in me, will you? You wore this shirt when we went clubbing like last year and you haven’t touched it since, I’ve got a vision. Let me look.” 

He pushed himself off the door frame and approached the wardrobe, an exasperated Lando very easily giving in, walking away and throwing himself on the bed. Oscar glanced over at him before averting his eyes to the heap of clothing in front of him. 

While Oscar ravaged through the wardrobe, Lando took his phone out of his jogger pockets and opened their shared group chat with his friends.

“Stop spamming the group chat and come try this outfit on!” Oscar interrupted his frantic messaging of their shared group chat, his head snapping up to look at the younger boy. He stood there holding a black button up shirt and white trousers. 

“Really?” Lando raised his eyebrow, placing his phone down on the bed next to him. He pulled himself up from the bed, stretching his arms over his head and walked towards Oscar. He took the clothes from the Aussie’s hands, eyeing them carefully before lowering his arms in exasperation. “I can’t believe I’ve had to trust you to pick my clothes out.” 

“Well, it’s either me or I can get Charles up here, your call.” Oscar offered, the slight teasing twinge to his voice showing he knew full well what the answer would be.

Lando rolled his eyes and twirled his finger at Oscar. “Turn around then! Don’t let me catch you peeping, I know I’m hot but geez.” 

“Wouldn’t dream of it.” The Aussie grumbled and turned to face the wardrobe. “But you’re acting like I haven’t seen it all before!”

Instead of answering, Lando blushed deeply and tore his sweatpants off quickly, pulling the white trousers up his toned legs. He unbuttoned the black silk shirt and swiftly pulled it over his body, buttoning it until the top three were undone. The shirt was a little loose and the undone buttons showed off his collarbones and chest, but it looked pretty good from what he could see in the mirror- considering Oscar picked it out. 

Lando told Oscar he could turn around, gauging the younger’s reaction. The Brit felt confidence surge through him as he watched Oscar’s eyes traveling from the bottom half of his body to his revealed chest. When his tongue poked out, slightly wetting his parted bottom lip, every muscle in Lando’s body worked overtime to stop him from rushing forward and colliding their lips together. 

God, why was he looking at him like that?

After a few beats of silence and Oscar still staring, Lando cleared his throat. He watched as the younger’s cheeks turned a dusted pink and his eyes averted away from his chest. “See- I told you to trust me.” 

“Where did your sudden fashion sense come from? You usually look a little homeless.” Lando snorted, taking a look at himself in the mirror again and running a hand through his curls, messing and fluffing them up a little. 

“Bastard!” Oscar gasped and threw one of the fallen shirts off the floor at Lando.

“Ow! Fuck you- okay- okay you can stop!” He shouted as even more shirts were thrown his way, backing up until his knees hit the bed and he fell backwards giggling. 

Lando lifted his head from the bed to see Oscar rummaging around on his chest of drawers, looking at his jewelry stand. He titled his head while watching as the Aussie picked out a silver chain. It was one that he had worn quite a lot the previous year when he suddenly found himself wanting to wear a lot more jewelry, he completely forgot he had it. “Nice, found it- now you’ll feel even more Lando-y.”

“Lando-y?” He laughed loudly and sat up properly on the bed. 

“Yeah, I regretted it as soon as I said it.” Oscar winced and approached the boy on the bed, silver chain in hand. “Come on, if you’re going to be showing off your tits all night then you may as well have something pretty there to decorate it.”

Lando scrunched his nose in laughter, moving across the bed so Oscar could kneel down next to him to reach his neck. He heard the chain being unclipped and moved the collar of the silk shirt out of the way. Oscar’s hands with the chain clasped in his two hands came over Lando’s head, hitting the furious mop of curls. Lando blew a breath up to one of the fallen curls, frowning when it just flopped back down onto his forehead, he’d sort it in a moment. 

Oscar’s hands brushed against Lando’s exposed collarbones as they moved to wrap the chain around his neck, his body stilted at the sudden touch and he let out a quiet gasp. Goosebumps gathered on his collarbone, spreading across his skin as he felt a warmth grow in his stomach, butterflies aching to break free. Whether Oscar realised the effect the small touch had on Lando was unknown, but the older boy could’ve sworn he felt the other one hesitate for a second before clasping the chain together, his big hands leaving Lando’s neck. 

He let out a breath he didn’t realise he was holding and scrambled up from the bed, towards the mirror. Lando refused to look Oscar in the eye in case he was sporting a bright blush on his face. He ruffled his hair again and fixed the shirt to rest loosely on his collarbones. 

Shuffling could be heard behind him and Oscar appeared behind him in the mirror, a sort of dazed look clouding his eye as he looked over the boy's shoulder, staring at them both standing there. “How do I look, Piastri?” Lando asked with a feigned smirk, but the voice crack that came through exposed the nerves he was feeling. He may as well have just shouted from the rooftops that Oscar looking at him like this made him want to run around screaming like a crazed man. 

Oscar didn’t answer straight away, instead he reached up to fix the collar of the silk shirt and Lando bit his lip to stop a sound escaping him as the hand grazed his neck again. Their eyes locked together through the mirror, time slowing down and his hands gently rested against the crevice between Lando’s neck and shoulders. Lando watched as Oscar’s Adam’s apple bobbled in his throat before speaking. “You look really good.”

“Just really good?” Lando fake pouted and nearly whined as the hands left his neck, missing the warm and soft touch. 

“I- you… you look stunning- like you always do.” Oscar broke eye contact and scurried away and out of Lando’s room with his head down. Lando whipped his head around as he watched him leave with his brows furrowed, wondering to himself ‘what the fuck is going on?’ 

“Osc?” Lando called out, grabbing one of his thicker coats before leaving the room and gently shut the door behind him. As he was leaving his room, he briefly glanced at the door in front of him which led to Oscar’s room, hanging on by the hinges after the younger was too rough with it. 

Right, they still needed to fix that before their landlord did a check. 

“Oscar?” He called out again, hearing a quiet ‘in here’ coming from the living room.

Lando moved towards the sound and saw Oscar jumping around the living room, trying to force one of his shoes on. Whatever had just happened was already long forgotten as Lando burst out laughing, taking his phone out and videoed the ridiculous scene in front of him. Oscar’s foot underneath him twisted and as he was about to fall to the floor, his hand shot out with his wrist flicking towards the ceiling. 

However, he still ended up falling flat on his face, despite whatever the attempt he just tried was. 

Oscar let out a loud groan and pushed himself up from the floor, a grimace taking over his face as he pushed his waves out his face. “Delete that.”

“Why would I when I’ve already sent-”

Crash. 

Lando practically jumped in the air at the loud noise coming from the street outside their building. Before he could react by dropping to the floor to make himself seem small and disappear, Oscar had appeared by his side and taken his hand into his own, giving him three hard squeezes. Lando returned them after a few seconds of deafening silence. 

To say he was coping well was a complete and utter lie, he could pretend he was okay when around the others, but he can never pretend with Oscar.

It had been a few days since the event at the time, Oscar had been treating him like he was fragile, and maybe he actually was, at least for the first few days. He never expected that it would affect him so deeply, he’d only now been able to turn the news on to see what happened to Doctor Octopus and freely talk about it without feeling like he was falling through the sky again. 

Oscar had invited everyone over the days after it all, all of them wanting to check on Lando and try to distract him from what happened. They were all talking casually until George had finally popped the question. 

‘What was it like to be there when it all happened?’

Lando was polite, he answered calmly and detailed to them all exactly what happened, he should’ve known that Oscar would see straight through the facade he was holding. He eventually excused himself and went to his room, the younger one rushing after him and his body let loose on every emotion he was feeling, all the pent up fear he had felt. They stayed there for god knows how long, Oscar holding him and rubbing his back while Lando blubbered on about how scared he felt and how he was still scared.

They had discovered last time that contact was good for him, Lando liked to be held, like a tight reassurance. It grounded him, in an odd way. It reminded him that it wasn’t the maniacal laughing madman holding him, it was Oscar. He wasn’t in Oscorp Tower crawling on glass and debris, he was on the floor of his flat. He wasn’t falling through the sky from the top floor, he was safe with Oscar. 

They had gotten pretty good already at sensing when Lando was feeling like he was back there, like his emotions were about to let loose, back in the rain and back in that goddamn building, back in the hold of the madman. 

Loud sounds reminded him of it too easily, which wasn’t ideal considering they lived in a busy city and on a very busy street. But Oscar was there- Oscar was quick, he’d been staying home a lot more and every night since then, they had shared the bed together, limbs intertwined as their hearts molded together. 

“You’re here in our flat, you’re with me, we’re about to leave for the party and we’re going to have a lot of fun.” Oscar gently said, a reminder, a grounding phrase for him. Lando nodded frantically, his grip on Oscar’s hand tightening. 

“Can- can we see what that noise was?” Lando mumbled, taking a tentative step towards the window as a shaky breath escaped him. 

“Of course, come on.” Oscar moved forward, gently pulling the older boy towards the window in the living room. Their eyes drifted downwards once they reached the window, the faint sound of people yelling could be heard even through the thick glass. 

On the street below them were two banged up cars, with traffic piling from both directions and two people shouting at each other over whose fault the crash really was. Lando breathed a sigh of relief, the tension in his body slowly evaporating. 

He opened his mouth ready to apologise for his reaction but was quickly interrupted. “Don’t even- you know I’d hate for you to deal with it by yourself.”

“I know, but it’s not fair for you to be worrying about it.” Lando frowned as he watched the two men on the street escalating their voices even further. “I’ll be okay eventually, I just need a bit of time.”

A silence fell over them, the only noise filling the air were the horns of frustrated drivers and the yelling of the pedestrians. Lando’s hand slipped out of Oscar’s, falling to his side. “You’ll always have me here, Lando.” 

“I know.” Lando said quietly. His eyes drifted towards the building opposite him, to the rooftop, where the sunset used to meet the streaks of red and blue. The longing that once bubbled in his chest was there no more, no longer feeling the pull to rush to the rooftop and throw himself into whatever danger was coming next. 

“We should leave now, we’ll probably be about half an hour late. Fashionably late, just the way you like it.” Oscar suggested with a hopeful tone, his shoulder nudging Lando’s own. 

“Yeah… we should.” Lando slowly nodded, his eyes moving from the rooftop, letting go of the fond memories he had. He looked up to Oscar next to him, their eyes locking as a fond smile grew on the Brit’s face. He melted under the Aussie’s gaze, the urge to capture the smile on his lips between his own intensified the longer he stared. “Daniel said there’s a bottle of vodka calling my name.”

“Congrats on making the front page again! Even if the story was about- uh, you know. Seriously, Lando, they aren’t paying you enough for those photos, man.” 

Lando and Oscar had made it to the party- only half hour late, as predicted- while it was in full swing. The pulsating music echoed from inside the venue and the occasional bright light shone through the windows of the building, illuminating the dark street. The venue was filled with at least seventy people from what Lando could tell, thankfully big enough to host them all and not be as overcrowded as a club. 

Charles wasn’t kidding when he said they needed a bigger place, he guessed. 

The two boys had made their way inside, navigating their way through the crowds of people they hadn’t spoken to since last year’s Christmas party, both wanting to avoid any awkward small talk. Lando didn’t have the time to just stop and chat to people he didn’t know, he needed to get a jagerbomb down him and listen to whatever party playlist Charles created this year. 

While discreetly avoiding everyone, the two had bumped into Alex and George seated on a round table surrounded by half full glasses. A brief glance to the dance floor showed their girlfriends and the rest of their friends were already letting loose- courtesy of Daniel Ricciardo. Lando laughed to himself when he saw stiff limbs being thrown around the dance floor and their poor attempts at dancing to American Boy by Kanye West and Estelle. 

“Alex is right, you’re really letting your photography skills go to waste at the Daily Bugle.” George agreed and Lando watched with careful precision as the older Brit’s arm snaked around the seat of Alex, resting on the wooden back. 

“I’ve been telling him this for ages.” Oscar declared, pulling out one of the seats and gesturing for the older boy to sit down. Lando looked at the chair for a second while George and Alex eyed them with curiosity. He bit back the question on the tip of his tongue and graciously took the seat, the Aussie joining next to him. “We’ve had some very lengthy conversations the past few days, haven’t we? Think he’s finally figured out what he wants to do.”

And they had. After his photo of Spiderman was published along with another picture of him in Spiderman’s arms after falling through the sky and an article about the whole ordeal- Lando and Oscar had discussed what he wants to be doing with his life next. It had been a topic over the years with Lando unsure if he wanted to pursue anything with his degree at the moment as well as his hobby with photography and content spiking. 

A lot of brainstorming went on, discussing what he was actually interested in whether he saw a future in taking pictures for blogs or journalists. Eventually, they had landed on something that he was pretty happy with, something he was already looking quite heavily into. 

Alex titled his head and asked the question on both his and George’s mind. “What’ve you decided to do then?”

“Well, I’m no longer sending my pictures to the Bugle.” Lando responded. “I’m finally free of Jameson!”

George took a sip of his orange cocktail with a raised eyebrow before speaking. “About time then, are you looking for another journalist company? I know a few places, I could pull a few strings?” 

“What he really means is, he could ask his dad about you working for them.” Alex quickly intercepted. George shot him a quick glance before turning back to Lando, a slight shift at the corner of his mouth. 

“What he said.” George titled his head to Alex, taking another long sip of his fruity cocktail. 

“Actually- I was thinking of starting my own company?” Lando said in an unsure voice, waiting for the reactions of the two boy’s in front of him. Oscar’s hand reached out to his own, gently brushing it, a soft reassurance.

George’s face had a surprised expression while Alex was beaming in excitement. “Lovely, it’s nice to see you being ambitious! What’s the business for?” Alex questioned, his shoulders coming up to rest on the table as he listened intently. 

“I wanna create content, whether that’s through making videos or something, I’m not sure, but it’s something I’ve been looking into a lot. Do you guys remember my online friends?” The two boys nodded at the brief mention of some of his friends such as Max and Maria. “Well- we’ve been talking a lot about it and I might be starting it with them. It’s not going to immediately lift off, but I could see it going places.”
