Chapter 7: Chapter 7

Chapter Summary

Lando wakes up in Charles place, wondering where things had gone wrong.

Chapter Notes

I’m very sorry for breaking all your hearts in chapter 6 :( although it was very entertaining watching everyone freak out over it and messaging in tears! Hope you all enjoy this next chapter <3

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Lando’s eyes fluttered open as he was pulled out of his deep sleep, the sound of Max and Charles rushing around the house booming through the spare bedroom as they frantically tidied the house to get ready for Christmas Eve. 

With a reluctant groan, Lando begrudgingly lifted his head from the pillow, his headache subsiding after spending the previous day curled up in Charles’ spare bedroom, wallowing in self-pity. His hand emerged from beneath the warm blanket, darting out to grab his phone, a shiver coursing through him as the cold air met his skin. He retreated back within the blanket, checked his phone, and frowned when he saw no new messages from Oscar.

After Lando abandoned the party —after Oscar had abandoned him— he had woken up the next morning to messages sent from the Aussie at 4am, begging him to return home. The messages continued all the way into the next day, pleading with him and making promises to tell him what happened. Lando ignored them all, swiping them off his phone after staring at them each for a few minutes. 

It gave him some sick satisfaction, knowing Oscar was grovelling and Lando hadn’t given in to the attempts, no matter how tempting it was. Except, the grovelling already stopped and now he found himself feeling miserable all over again. 

It was now Christmas Eve and Lando still hadn’t returned home, spending the last two nights at Charles’ place. The Monegasque voiced that he was more than happy to accommodate Lando the past two nights while he figured out what to do with himself. He felt guilty for interrupting his time with Arthur and Max, but how was he supposed to return home after that?

He heard the sound of footsteps coming closer and he shifted until his body was fully submerged beneath the blanket, entrapped in the heat. The door swung upon with a slight crack and Lando wrapped himself up tightly in the blanket. 

“Come on, get out of bed before I drag you by your ankles.” Charles' accented voice burst through the silence Lando had come to love. He mentally cursed to himself, refusing to move a muscle. “I know you’re awake, you need to get up today.”

“I don’t need to get up, I can stay in bed all day again.” Lando protested with a whine. 

“No you can’t, because I won’t let you. Get up and have some breakfast. Then, we’re talking about what you’re going to do from here.” 

“I don’t wanna talk about it, Charles.” Lando responded with a miserable voice, his grip on the blanket around him loosening as he thought about the night of the party. 

Yesterday, they left him alone to drown in his sorrow, only entering the room to check on him and bring him food. Lando had tried desperately to eat, but he could only stomach a few bites of each meal they brought, even only having a couple of sips of water despite the mind-curdling hangover he had. 

He couldn’t find it in himself to do anything, the motivation to move or continue with anything left his body the moment Oscar chose to leave him there. Everything inside him slumped and came to a halt, feeling trapped in his own body and mind. 

Charles' hands entered the blanket at the bottom of the bed and gripped onto Lando’s ankles, yanking him down the bed as he let out a loud yelp. The blanket was suddenly flung off his body and on the floor, the ceiling light blinding him with a hiss. Charles stood over him with his hands on his hands, tapping his foot. “You will eat your breakfast, and then we’ll talk about what you’re doing. Got it?”

Lando cursed to himself and sat upright, his muscles aching as he dragged himself up. “Got it.”

“Good.” Charles smiled and went to walk out the room, hesitating as he reached the doorway. “Go shower, you stink, breakfast can wait.”

“I’ve just had my heart broken, fuck you! I’m allowed to stink!” Lando shouted after Charles, who strutted away without a care. As annoyed as he was, he knew it was Charles’ way of caring for him and making sure he looked after himself. 

Lando stood up from the bed, grimacing as his bones clicked with each movement. He grabbed one of the outfits Arthur had left in his room the day before and made his way to the shower. Lando had scoffed when he woke up yesterday and saw the spare bedroom had its own en-suite- talk about being rich! The Monegasque always lived it large. 

Lando stripped down to nothing after locking the door, his body yelling at him with agony to climb back into bed and sleep away the day again. Turning on the shower, he cautiously tested the water’s temperature with his hand. Once he was satisfied with the scalding hot temperature burning his hand, he stepped into the shower and let the water envelope his body. 

Under the steady stream, Lando let the heavy water cascade over his body, his curls fading away and sticking to his head. He welcomed the water with open arms, attempting to wash away all emotions and memories from that night, the argument and Oscar.

But as the water washed over him, so too did his emotions. Despite his best efforts to evade all memories of that night, they came surging through like a tornado warping his mind. The shower before him turned into the rain-soaked street, he closed his eyes in acceptance- he could hear himself pleading for Oscar to stay as the younger boy’s lingering presence started to fade. 

Since then, Lando had avoided letting his emotions get the best of him. He had his phone, the mind-numbing hangover and endless amounts of sleep to distract him, but here in the shower- he was vulnerable to it all. Blinking back his tears proved to be futile- the salted tears falling and blending with the freshwater, conjuring a tsunami. 

“It’s not that simple, Lando.”

“It never is with you.”

“You promised you’d stay…”

“And that was a mistake…”

His hand instinctively wound through the soaked curls, frustration rushing through his body as he clasped his mouth, stifling a scream. The sound of falling water muffled the yell, trapping the screams from the world. Lando’s chest heaved, a choked sob escaping him. His back collided with the cold, wet wall, flinching away slightly before embracing the baltic feeling. 

As the water continued on its relentless descent, Lando found himself grappling onto any memory of Oscar, anything at all that would make him forget that he was ever left alone in the street after their argument. Each drop from the showerhead thumped against the tiles, the noises wailing with him. 

With trembling hands, he wiped away the remaining tears as his body calmed itself down. His breath steadied and his sobs were silenced. Lando pulled himself away from the wall, shivering at the loss of the cold tiles. The room fell near silent, the deafening sound of the shower the only thing left. 

His shower passed by in a flurry after his little moment and soon he found himself dried and dressed, hesitantly heading downstairs to meet the others. Exiting the spare room, his gaze met the mirror on the wall in the hallway, the reflection staring back at him caused a shocked gasp to leave his body. 

To put things lightly, Lando looked like shit. 

And if he looked like this after a shower, then he dreaded to think of what Charles had seen of him before. His eyes were bloodshot with dark circles accompanying it, the colour in his pupils had faded to a dull grey. The curls on his head were a damp flat mess and his shoulders were hunched together, making himself look small. 

Lando’s fingers delicately ruffled his hair, attempting to fix it. He threw his shoulders back and raised his head. The Brit gave himself a small smile, his face brightening a little. Fake it till you make it, even if he still felt like a ton of bricks were weighing him down both mentally and physically. 

Once he was satisfied with the little improvements he made, Lando made his way down the staircase. He peered around the corner as he heard the sound of the television and people quietly chatting, only to see Charles and Arthur seated in the living room and the moving images of Cars 2 on the television. 

“Are you back in the land of the living?” Charles raised an eyebrow as he caught Lando peeping his head around the corner. The Brit glared back at him and entered the living room, his hands clasped behind his back. He took a seat on the far end of the sofa, cuddling into the side. He pretended not to notice the sympathetic gaze Arthur gave him. Charles pursed his lips. “Max is just cooking up some breakfast now. Do you want me to put something else on?”

Arthur grabbed the television remote, ready to hand it to Charles, but Lando quickly raised and shook his head at the two. “No, I like Cars and especially this one… it’s a good film. Keep it on, please.” His voice trailed off, becoming quiet and small. 

“Okay… we’ll leave it on.” Lando stared at the screen and heard the sound of the remote being gently placed on the coffee table. He watched the movie moving with dull eyes, not even finding it in himself to laugh at how scarily similar Charles and Lightning McQueen look. 

In the corner of his eye, Lando could see the watchful gaze of Charles- eyeing him for any hints of emotions breaking through his fine facade. He turned to meet his gaze, expecting Charles to look away- but they locked eyes and Lando felt the vulnerability crawling back. Charles gave him a look, as if to ask if he were okay. Lando turned back to the movie, pretending it never happened.

As he watched the movie with his mind half there, Max came trailing in with stacks of pancakes. He gently placed them down on the coffee table before running back into the kitchen, returning with four plates, cutlery and toppings. Lando’s eyes flickered over to the plates and he internally groaned, of course it was pancakes. He truly couldn’t escape the reminders of him.

“Thanks, Max.” Arthur chipped and graciously took one of the empty plates, immediately stacking pancakes on it. The younger Monegasque took a huge bite out of one of them, moaning as he smacked his lips. “Goodness, I will thank God everyday for bestowing these pancakes upon me.” He muttered like a prayer. 

“I can cook better pancakes than this.” Charles rolled his eyes and took two pancakes for himself, smiling at Max like an idiot in love as he too took a bite of the food. “Although- they are delicious. Thank you, mon amour.” Max perked up at the pet name, his eyes crinkling as he loaded himself up a plate. 

Arthur scoffed and kicked Charles in the shin, causing the older boy to let out a loud yelp as he nearly dropped the plate on the carpeted floor. Charles glared at him, but Arthur paid it no attention. “You cannot even cook pasta, your pancakes would give anyone salmonella.”

“He cooked some chicken a few weeks ago and gave himself salmonella, actually.” Max replied. 

Charles huffed angrily and turned towards Lando, expecting him to comment on his atrocious cooking skills. He frowned to himself when he saw Lando hadn’t given himself any food. While Arthur and Max were distracted and discussing all the times Charles’ somehow ruined anything he cooked, he grabbed the last empty plate and put two pancakes on it, drowning them in strawberry sauce and nudged the younger boy with it. Lando blinked himself out of a trance and looked down to the plate hitting his leg. 

He looked up at Charles, mouthing him a small thank you and took the plate, grateful to see they were made just how he liked them and that the others hadn’t noticed his lack of enthusiasm for the food. Taking the knife and fork, Lando started to cut the pancakes into small bite-size pieces.

Max grabbed the remote from the coffee table and flicked the channel, much to the protest of the three boys who were happily watching Cars 2. With a mouthful of pancakes, the Dutchman shrugged his shoulders and spoke. “I’m just catching up on the news! I’ll put it back on in five minutes. Calm yourselves!” 

“Why can’t you go watch the news in our bedroom?” Charles hissed and quickly tried to grab the remote, noticeably failing to reach for it. Lando decided to ignore the fact that Charles called it ‘our’ bedroom, he wasn’t in the mood to tease him when it could easily be turned back onto himself. 

“I just want to see the weather, it’ll literally be five minutes, Charles! Plus- I’m eating down here.” Max finally found the news channel and hummed with delight once he saw the segment just started. Charles didn’t bother arguing anymore, accepting their fate to be forced to watch a boring news outlet. 

Arthur grumbled under his breath, “We have the weather app for a reason.” But Max didn’t seem to hear it, intently focusing on the television instead. 

Lando- having left his phone upstairs and having nothing better to do- listened to the news too, occasionally chewing into a small piece of the pancakes. He struggled to swallow them as his stomach still felt uneasy from the shower. Lando felt like he was eating gravel as he forced it down his throat, despite how delicious they really were (but nothing would compare to his).

“-and now onto the robbery of the Bank of England which occurred on the 22nd December at approximately 11pm. Reports say that a group of six armed robbers had broken into the bank, leaving two of the six security guards with life-threatening injuries and the others with minor injuries. Spiderman soon attended the scene and apprehended the criminals before they could escape, saving the hostages and the life of the guard with life-threatening injuries. Once again, Spiderman has saved the lives of the city. Rob, do you-”

Lando breathed a sigh of relief upon hearing that Spiderman was there to save the rest of the security guards, feeling a wave of gratitude wash through him as he remembered all the times the hero had saved him. 

He tried not to admit it to himself, but Lando really missed Spiderman. Lando felt an ache within him, not only had he lost Oscar, but he also lost Spiderman. He missed sitting on the rooftop with him and talking, even if it were only a few times. The hero felt so familiar to him and offered him comfort when he needed it, saving him from danger countless times and overall made him feel alive with purpose. No matter how many times he checked the rooftop since that night however, he was nowhere to be seen. 

Perhaps he took Doctor Octopus’ words to heart, the fact that they were being watched and the villain knew exactly who to target as his weak spot probably raised some alarm bells for Spiderman. Lando couldn’t blame him for staying away, all he did was cause trouble for him anyway. 

No wonder he stopped returning to the rooftop where he knew Lando could find him, he probably wanted nothing to do with him anymore. Lando supposed he’d never return to the rooftop again, he’d never admire the sunset there again. 

Besides, he did make a promise to Oscar to stay away… at least he would keep his promises, unlike a certain someone. 

“I actually drove past your place yesterday, Lando, Spiderman was on the roof there. When you said he hangs out next to your place, I didn’t realise it was that close!”

Or maybe he wouldn’t?

“What?” Lando snapped his head up at Max who had just spoken. Charles was glaring at Max already, causing the Dutchman to look sheepish and shrink in his seat. “You saw Spiderman there?” He asked with a hopeful tone. 

“I mean-” Max began to say, looking back at Charles for any signal on if he should even continue talking. “-he was there, but he left as soon as I saw him there.”

Lando felt himself visibly brighten as a smile grew on his face, his chest seeming to burst with emotions. “But he was still there, yes?” The question escaped his lips, the words wrapped with anticipation. 

“Uh, yeah?”

Lando let out a squeal as he kicked his legs in excitement, forgetting to ask why Max was even by his flat with Oscar in the first place. If Spiderman was still going to the rooftop, then surely it was a sign that he was still okay to meet with Lando. Why else would he go somewhere that he knew Lando could see him from his flat? That he knew he could show up any moment like he had the previous times? It was a sign, a sign they were okay, an open invitation for Lando to take. He was so distracted with trying to figure out a time he could go to the rooftop that he hadn’t noticed what else the news channel were currently reporting on. 

“-that’s right, Rob. After the event in which Doctor Octopus destroyed Hyde Park and took a hostage with him to Oscorp Tower, the supervillain will now be placed within the new maximum security facility called ‘The Raft’ this afternoon. It’s currently holding some of the United Kingdom’s notorious villains and recently, Doctor Octopus. Spiderman is due to accompany officers in making sure Doctor Octopus safely enters the facility, tune in later this afternoon at 5:00pm for any updates from us.”

Lando watched as the news played a clip of Doctor Octopus practically soaring through the city using his mechanical arms, feeling a wave of sickness wash over him as the camera zoomed in on Lando’s near unconscious body. He watched as his head flopped side to side, remembering the fear and dread he felt all throughout the event, how his body was petrified and frozen in fear. Lando tossed the plate to the side, landing on the coffee table with a clatter as he felt his stomach performing twists and turns. 

Suddenly, the television turned black and all Lando could see was the faded reflection of himself, boring his eyes into the screen. The silence was deafening, uncomfortable, he knew that none of them even knew what to say to him. He couldn’t tear his eyes away from himself, looking into his own sad eyes. 

Right- this is why Spiderman stayed away. This is why Lando promised Oscar he’d stayed away in the first place. It wasn’t safe, and these were the consequences of getting too close. 

“I’ll stay away from Spiderman, no more sticking my nose in places where it doesn’t belong. It’s me and you, forever and always.”

“Lando?” Charles' soft voice called out, a hand touching his knee lightly. Lando flinched away, tearing his eyes away from the television to look at Charles, his hand hovering in the air. 

Lando took a look around the room at the other two boys, the tension leaving his body seeing that he was safe, he was in Charles’ living room. He was with his friends. He was sitting on the sofa. He was next to the coffee table with a plate of pancakes. And Charles’ hand was on his knee. 

Charles looked at him with a face covered in concern. Right- none of them had to deal with it yet. For a brief moment, Lando found himself wishing Oscar could just burst through the room and wrapped him up in his arms tightly. 

“Lando, are you okay?”

“I’m fine, I’m all good. Just- sometimes I need a few moments, it’s not nice to see it and have to relive it.” Lando blubbered and fidgeted with his hands as he spoke. He sunk into the chair, wishing he could disappear. “I’m okay.” He repeated. 

“Okay… listen, are you up for talking now? If you’re not ready then it’s fine, but we will need to talk eventually.” Charles said with a gentle tone, his voice sounding like it was laced with honey. He spoke with understanding, and it caused a feeling of guilt to build up inside Lando. 

He knew that the Monegasque just wanted to help him, wanting to help resolve whatever happened between them. Lando sighed, finally giving in. “I’m up for talking now, let’s just get it out of the way.” 

Max took this as his queue to leave, picking up the now empty plates and dirty cutlery, returning to the kitchen to wash the dishes. Arthur however, stayed put, unmoving. Lando didn’t mind really and he knew that Max could probably still hear him from the kitchen, just moving out of the room to give off some politeness. 

“I’ve had Oscar texting me non-stop, he’s been worried about you and he had no idea where you were. I let him know you were staying here and he asked to come over, I told him no unless you explicitly invited him. He was fine with that, but he asked me to let you know that he wants to talk about everything as soon as you’re ready to hear it. I don’t know what was said between you two, but I know he promised you he’d stay and he left. I know how you feel right now, I know-”

Lando was quick to cut Charles off before he could continue. “Charles, I’m sorry but you have no idea how I’m feeling right now. I know he’s been lying to us all, but it hurts so much more when he’s my best friend, when he’s been lying directly to me, promising me he’d actually stay for once and telling me we’ll talk about everything, only then to up and leave me. I appreciate your concern and I know you’re just trying to help, but I don’t want to see him nor do I want to speak to him.”

Charles bit his lip and glanced over at Arthur for help, who in return shrugged his shoulders with a bewildered look on his face. The older Monegasque turned back to Lando with a sympathetic gaze. “I’m not asking you to speak to him, I’m just letting you know he’s been in touch.”

“Then what’s this about?” Lando asked, the question coming across as rude. He winced to himself when he saw a flicker of hurt on Charles face, quickly washed over by the concern evident in his eyes. 

“I’m just asking you to talk to me about this and figure out what we’re doing from here. It’s clear you don’t want to go back to your place.” Lando quickly shook his head. “I’m more than happy to have you here, you know that- but we’ve at least gotta go to your place and pick up some of your things. You can’t live out of Arthur’s clothes forever.” Charles lightly joked and Lando couldn’t help the small smile playing on his face. 

“I know, I just- I don’t want to see him.” Lando admitted, his fingers messing with the drawstrings of the joggers he was wearing. “I’m not ready, not yet.” 

Some part of him felt sick to admit it, because no matter how badly he didn’t want to face Oscar, he felt horrible knowing that the Aussie would be spending Christmas alone. His family were in Australia and would be asleep most of Christmas day, all their friends were either with their families for the holidays or with their partners. Lando and Oscar were supposed to spend the day together, exchanging their gifts and cooking their own makeshift Christmas dinner. And now they were separated, left to mourn whatever was there between them. What could’ve been something amazing.

But who was to blame for that?

“That’s okay!” Charles reassured him and took the Brit’s hand into his own, gently squeezing it. Lando stared down at their intertwined hands, the ghost of another two squeezes lingering like a soft touch. “I can ask him to leave for a bit so you can grab some things, I’m sure he wouldn’t mind.”

Lando chewed his bottom lip anxiously before nodding slowly. “Okay… as long as he’s not there, I’ll go grab some things.”

“I’ll drive you there whenever you’re ready. Let me know when you wanna head over, I’ll text Oscar and ask if he can leave for a bit.”

“I’m ready whenever, let’s just get it over with.” Lando grumbled. Charles nodded and grabbed his phone, frantically typing away. “Charles?” The older boy hummed in response. “Thank you- for everything, I know I can be a handful. Thank you for looking after me and letting me stay.”

“Oh, come here you little sap.” Charles cooed at him, pulling Lando towards him and hugging him. The older boy rubbed his hand on the small of Lando’s back soothingly, like a mother comforting her son. “I will always help you, no matter what! Even if you have been acting like a moody teenager.” Charles teased. 

“Charles!” Lando gasped in offence. 

“Kidding! I love you really, I’ll always be here to help you and pick up the pieces. We’ll fix it all soon, I promise you. We’ll work it out.” Charles' phone buzzed as it rested against Lando’s shoulder. The two broke apart and Charles checked the notification, smiling to himself. “Well, Oscar said he’s actually leaving the house in a few hours, we can head over when he’s left. Is that alright?”

“Yeah, that’s fine with me.” He responded weakly, his words trailing off as he got lost inside his mind. 

Just where was Oscar disappearing to now on Christmas Eve?

*

“Okay, you wait here, I’m just gonna grab some of my clothes quickly.” Lando instructed as he made his way through his flat, gesturing for Charles to take a seat in the living room. He glanced at the living room, seeing that the window was slightly open again. “If Oscar comes back home, give me a text and I’ll go through the fire escape.” Lando schemed.

“Or,” Charles began and shrugged his coat off before making himself comfortable on the sofa, sprawling out. “I can just ask Oscar to wait in his room so you can come out? Besides, he’s respecting your wishes and not coming home until I say he can.”

Lando didn’t bother responding and instead made his way to his bedroom, sparing a brief glance to the door to Oscar’s room, to the broken hinges. He waited for a moment, expecting Oscar to walk out of his room. It reminded him of whenever they would exit their rooms at the same time, fighting and pushing each other into the walls to reach the bathroom first. Lando wiped the smile that appeared off his face, turning towards his room. 

He entered his room, surprised to see that the clothes that were once scattered around his room from preparing for the party were now neatly folded at the bottom of his bed. Even the room itself seemed a whole lot cleaner, the speckles of dust having disappeared. 

Lando felt a feeling of frustration wash over him at the thought of Oscar coming into his room, feeling guilty to his core and folding his clothes to pass the day away. He imagined him sneaking glances at his phone every few minutes, either sending Lando a text or praying to receive one. He imagined his eyes flicking towards the door at any slight noise, hoping that it was Lando returning to his open arms with forgiveness. 

He looked at the folded clothes with a sneer before throwing them off his bed, allowing them to fall without a care. If he couldn’t face Oscar to shout at him, he’d take it out on the clothes instead. 

He stepped over the now mess of clothing and reached his wardrobe, grabbing one of his biggest duffel bags and began to grab as many things as he needed. Lando grabbed all the comfy and warm clothes he could, shoving them in his bag without a care, he could just iron the creases out if needed. Besides, where else was he planning to go? They were there just to mope in bed with style and comfort. 

Lando continued stuffing the duffel bag before it was filled to the brim with clothing. He decided that for now, he could always wash these or come back for more if he needed to. Another duffel bag was opened and he filled it with colognes, his camera, hygiene supplies and more. As he reached under his bed to see if there was anything else he needed, his eyes landed on a folded up orange blanket, the one that the duo always shared. 

Hesitantly, Lando pulled the orange blanket out, staring at the neatly folded bundle. Oscar must’ve stored it under his bed, unable to stare at it in the living room for any longer than he needed to. He knew how the Aussie operated and how his emotions worked, he definitely hid it so he didn’t have to look at it.

The orange stuck out against their flat like a sore thumb, the rest of the flat was mostly white with beige or greys. They picked it up in their first year of university, back when they lived in dormitories and they’d stay in each others’ rooms. They bought the biggest blanket they could find, Oscar vouched for the fact it was orange and told Lando it reminded him of the sunset. Lando at the time shrugged and bought it, now they use it all the time. 

Anywhere you go in the flat, the blanket just seems to follow. 

Lando stuffed the blanket in his bag, no way was he letting Oscar have it right now if he was just going to stuff it under his bed with all the dust. Besides- he’ll get a lot more use out of it right now. 

He zipped the bag up, ready to take the two duffel bags and leave. As he was exiting his room, his eyes landed on the broken door of Oscar’s room again. Lando sighed to himself, giving in to temptation and swinging the broken door open as he entered the room. 

A loud gasp escaped him once he saw the chaotic mess Oscar’s room was in. Now- Oscar was known for his mess, he was pretty good at containing it to his bedroom. But this? It was as though Oscar had let loose his fury on the room and ripped it all apart. His drawings were scattered on the floor, his clothes strewn everywhere with plates and cups piling up on his bedside table. If he had let himself get to this level, then Lando was surprised his door was even still attached to the hinges. He was beyond baffled. 

His own room was the look of order, while Oscar’s room was always like a robber had torn the room apart looking for something. 

Lando looked over his shoulder, glancing into the living room where he could see Charles was still on his phone, paying no attention to the gasp that left him. He turned back to the room and closed the door slowly behind him, careful of the broken hinges. 

He may as well steal some hoodies while he was there, Oscar would understand. In fact, he imagined the Aussie would encourage him. 

Lando gravitated towards Oscar’s wardrobe, the oversized clothes chanting his name like a prayer and swung the wardrobe doors open. His eyes scanned the clothes hung up, his finger tapping his chin in contemplation as he viewed his options. A tinge of disappointment crept in when he saw the lack of actual hoodies in there, Oscar must’ve decided to finally do a big round of washing for them. 

His eyes flickered down to the floor of the wardrobe, by the pairs of shoes he kept in there. Lando furrowed his brows in confusion as he squatted down to take a closer look at what was stuffed into the corner, almost as if it was hidden away. His hand reached out to the black bundled material, pulling it out from the corner and placing it in his lap. 

His knees hit the ground, allowing himself to get comfortable on the floor. Lando looked at the black material, it was just Oscar’s black hoodie. Come to think of it, Lando hadn’t seen Oscar wearing it for a few weeks now, he just assumed the boy had lost it, maybe it fell from the hangers and he didn’t see it by his shoes?

No- that couldn’t be it, because the hoodie was neatly folded and if it fell from the hanger, it wouldn’t look like that. The hoodie was deliberately placed there, perhaps he was trying to hide it so Lando didn’t take it. But that didn’t make any sense- he always took Oscar’s hoodies with no issues. Besides, this wasn’t anything special, it was just a plain black hoodie that he probably picked up from Primark or something. 

Why was it hidden?

His fingers trailed over the soft fabric, reminiscing on memories with Oscar, remembering the times he’d worn it. As Lando trailed over the hoodie, his fingers brushed against something odd. Lando titled his head in confusion and took a closer look at the fabric, tracing his fingers over it again. There was a big hole in the black fabric, his fingers playing with the broken seams and he couldn’t help but feel like he’d already seen it. That must’ve been why it was at the bottom of Oscar’s wardrobe, it was broken. 

Wait. 

Suddenly, a memory emerged and flooded his mind, the memory from a time where he’d seen a black hoodie with a big hole in it before. Dread began to fill his body in an ugly pit as he recalled the moment, down to the minute details. 

He looked down towards the direction of his arm and noticed there was a big hole in the black jumper. His hand wandered to the hole, playing with it a little and examining it. 

“No.” Lando uttered to himself in disbelief, his hand smacking over his mouth as his eyes widened and eyebrows nearly flew off his head. There was no fucking way. Back to the day that Lando met ‘Professor Diabolical’, when he found out it was really Logan and nearly being caught with him. Where Spiderman saved them both from the criminals. 

Where he hugged Spiderman, who was wearing a black hoodie and Lando felt the big hole under his fingers. 

A gasp left him at the revelation and suddenly, all the pieces of the puzzle he’d been trying to solve for years finally slotted together perfectly.. All the little clues across the last few years that were so painfully obvious and so in his face, Lando wasn’t sure how he missed them in the first place. 

Spiderman dropped him outside his building, ready to rush off before Lando rushed forward and gave him a hug, not even thinking about it before his body told him to do it.

How could he be so naive? When Spiderman saved Lando the first time, all the way back to Christmas time during his first year of university, he never even bothered questioning how the hero knew exactly where he lived or where to send the new phone he bought him. It was right in front of him. 

“My best friend, the one whose hoodie I’m wearing, he actually works at Webber Industries. He said he’d get me an autograph from Mark next time he sees him. He’s doing an internship there, you may have seen him around! His name is Oscar Piastri, have you heard of him?”

Of course- Spiderman knew Iron Man, he had all his suits and gadgets made for him by Webber Industries. All those late night shifts, all those times he was suddenly called hin. The internship in general at a prestigious company like Webber Industries should’ve been enough to call it into question. 

“It’s not mine… I’d never buy such a plain hoodie! No logo, no design, nothing! It’s my best friend's hoodie.”

“Well, tell your roommate it’s a really nice hoodie.”

How the fuck did all of this never occur to him that there was something going on? Lando hadn’t even told Spiderman that his best friend was also his roommate. 

“I had no clue what was happening, you didn’t answer my texts and I got myself so worked up. I-I thought it was already too late. Guns and knives, Lando, I can’t have you near it. What if anything happened to you?”

The guns and knives. Oscar knew about the fucking guns and knives. 

Oscar would go on runs multiple times a day.

Oscar’s multiple drawings with weird designs.

Oscar lying about leaving his job.

Oscar broke his bedroom door with barely a touch. 

Oscar’s hand was stuck to Lando’s shirt. 

Oscar would leave their flat windows open all the time. 

Oscar disappeared all the time, including Winter Wonderland. 

Spiderman knew who Logan was.

Spiderman gave his hand the three squeezes. 

Spiderman had an odd accent and wasn’t from here. 

Spiderman felt so familiar to him and treated him like he always knew him. 

Spiderman was close by enough to save him the very first time. 

Spiderman openly flirted with him. 

Spiderman saved him when he should’ve been saving Norman. 

Lando looked back down to the hoodie and raised it in the air, hanging it there to examine the hole once more. Until, something fell out from the previous folded creases and landed on the floor next to his knees. He slowly placed the hoodie down and his eyes nearly bulged out of his head as he saw what had fallen. 

Lando’s hands trembled as they picked up the fallen item, his body shaking with adrenaline coursing through his veins and his breathing sharpened. Slowly, he lifted the item up and let out a shaky breath, finally accepting the reality of the situation. God, no wonder he hid everything for so long, it made so much sense now. All the lies, all the disappearing, all the shady behaviour that Lando once criticised Oscar for was all excused in his head now.

In his hands, was the torn and faded mask of Spiderman. 

Oscar was Spiderman. 

“What the fuck?” Lando screamed, his throat hurting after the puzzle was finally completed in his mind. How the fuck was Oscar Piastri Spiderman? Sweet Oscar, who could never hurt a fly, was out there battling with supervillains and criminals during his day-to-day life. He was out there saving the city while Lando, oblivious as ever, would be sitting in their shared flat complaining that Oscar never spoke to him about anything. 

God, he felt like an asshole.

Lando was always so harsh on him, if only he had known from the beginning. 

As the revelation sank in, a whirlwind of emotions took hold of him, storming through his mind relentlessly. Lando felt so much admiration for both Spiderman and Oscar, knowing how much the Aussie gave himself to that life, how much time he’d dedicated to it and sacrificed leading a normal life all to save the city time and time again. 

Oscar- sweet, stoic, Oscar, was Spiderman. The boy he lived with for years, the boy he thought he knew so well, was the very hero he admired. Oscar was the boy he’d gran Mcdonald’s with, the boy he’d sit there watching reruns of his favourite shows. Lando would complain about his normal day, about his shitty job and about how his professor set him too much work again. Lando complained about all of this to a fucking superhero, one that saved multiple people every day, risking his life for the good of the city. 

Suddenly, all of Lando’s problems seemed very juvenile to everything Oscar had to experience over the last few years.

God, Lando was hurt. Sure- he understood why Oscar acted the way he did for so long, but why couldn’t he have known since the beginning. He felt a sense of betrayal building a nasty pit in his stomach, realising that Logan most likely knew the truth before him. Lando shook the feeling away, he couldn’t let himself be upset over that. Oscar must’ve been dealing with so much, carrying the weight of the secret with him for so long. 

Suddenly, Lando heard loud footsteps hurtling towards the door to Oscar’s room. Before he could shout to be careful with the door, it was too late. In a split second, the door swung open with such a force that the top hinges of it finally broke completely. It was left suspended at a certain angle, broken beyond a simple repair. 

“Great, now we’re never getting our deposit back.”

Lando looked over at Charles’ bewildered face from his place on the floor. Charles didn’t seem to be paying any attention to the half hanging door and instead at the mask Lando was holding. He stared at the Monegasque, a spare puzzle piece found its place in the puzzle in his mind. 

Charles was saved by Spiderman and was acting very strangely with Oscar that same day. 

“You knew, didn’t you?” Lando spoke up, his voice tinged with disappointment and accusation as he addressed Charles. His fingers tightly clutched the broken mask, as if letting it go would make him forget the truth, make him forget what he had learnt. 

“Knew what?” Charles asked with a perplexed expression, having the audacity to sound dumbfounded at the question.

“Don’t play stupid. You knew- you knew that Oscar is Spiderman.”

“Lando-” Charles began to plead. 

“Charles.” Lando interrupted quickly. “Did you know?” He demanded with a stern voice. 

Charles pursed his lips before slowly nodding, he cautiously made his way over the mess of clothing and kneeled down next to Lando. He took a look at the mask again, letting out an exhausted sigh. “Yes, I knew. I only found out when he saved me though, no longer than that.”

“And you kept it from me?” Lando whispered, his voice barely audible as his fingers brushed over the textured fabric of the mask, hurt evident in his voice. 

“Lando,” Charles began. “It wasn’t my story to tell.” He explained with an apologetic tone. 

“Well, he was never going to tell me himself, was he?” Lando spat bitterly. “Why would Oscar tell you and not me?”

Charles erupted into a loud laugh, the sound reverberating throughout the room as he clutched his side in amusement. Lando eyed the Monegasque in genuine concern, shaking his head lightly. “What? Do you really think Oscar of all people opened up to me?” Charles quipped.

“How else would you know?”

Charles gave him a gentle smile before remarking, “Let’s just say we’re not all exactly as naive as you, Lando.”

*

“Right, our agenda for today is breakfast at Vettel’s Cafe, then padel, view the venue for the party, FaceTime maman and then date night!”

Charles had a skip in his step as he weaved through the people of London, hand intertwined with his-kinda-boyfriend, Max. As time drew closer to Christmas, the streets of London busied with people rushing to get their Christmas shopping done. The both of them had lived there for a few years now, being used to the chaotic nature of Christmas time in London and had mastered the art of lightly grazing past everyone to reach their destinations. 

“Slow down, Charles! We have all day to do this, there is no rush, schat.” Max loudly reassured, hoping his voice could be heard over the loud chatter and rolling engines of the city. Charles felt three light squeezes on his hand and a soft smile began to form. 

Contrary to popular belief, Max was an absolute sweetheart, and a sap to those who actually knew him. Having known him since their youth, Charles had been able to see every side of him, the good, the bad and the ugly. It had started in their karting days, from snatching the win from each other to pushing each other in puddles, they had quickly grown as rivals and were always looking to one up each other, both power hungry teenagers. Many people commented over the years about them being polar opposites, that how they ended up being friends was beyond them. Sure, they had their differences but they were eerily similar in the sense of their passion and the way they both do whatever it takes to get what they want. They had fought dirty in their karting days and even now as they were dating, they were still looking to one up each other (in a much more healthy and romantic way, of course). 

Maybe it’s why they had been so drawn to each other, why Charles couldn’t get enough of Max and his mind was always preoccupied with the Dutchman. Looking back, the two now laugh at their petty rivalry as they had now realised they were both purely obsessed with each other and couldn’t get enough of frustrating the other. 

It was by complete chance that they ended up both attending the same university in another country, his brother Arthur liked to joke that perhaps they were predestined. Charles wouldn’t complain about that behind closed doors. 

Charles had gone there to study architecture, landing a placement with one of the biggest architecture firms in London and working there ever since. He had worked his way up the chain fairly quickly and in the next two years was looking to create his own business. Max had gone to study mathematics and eventually started working towards becoming an accountant after leaving education. They were both successful in their own right, clawing their way through university with the top grades and now having their careers to show for it. 

Perhaps their power hungry, hormonal instincts hadn’t quite left them yet. 

“You know I like to sit down and talk to Seb for a while! We have to hurry!” 

“Okay, okay! How come you’re booking a venue for the party anyway?” Max had managed to slot himself back by Charles’ side after the crowds started to disperse and they were in the clear. He used his one hand to adjust the scarf around his neck as the cool chilling breeze attacked the exposed skin. “Last year you had a house party and now you’re booking a venue . If no one knew you were well off before, they will now.” 

The Monegasque rolled his eyes playfully, strutting ahead as Max trailed a slight bit behind him trying to keep up with his pace. “I have worked hard for it, non?” 

“Of course you have! It’s just very odd to go from the comfort of your home to now drinking in a fancy venue.” 

“It won’t be fancy when we’re all done with it, that’s for sure.” 

“Are you sure you want to book a venue? Lando broke your piano last year, you know what he’s like.” 

Charles pursed his lips before saying, “That’s true, but he’ll contain himself in a place he doesn’t know.” 

“He once fell off a roof, Charles. He also once ate paint when he was drunk and was rushed to hospital.” He deadpanned.

“Minor casualty, plus he was younger then!” Charles defended, although he didn’t trust the young British boy too much himself. “Anyway, I’m inviting work friends too so it’s important to me to invest in this! I have people to impress.” 

Max frowned. “You don’t need to impress them at all, you impress them daily with the work you do. I can’t wait until you leave that place and start your own company. They undervalue you so much and don’t appreciate any of the extra work you do for them. You’re so much better than them.” 

“Well we have a while to go before that happens, mon cheri.” Charles stopped in his tracks and reached up to brush his thumb against the Dutchman’s cheek. “But you are very sweet and I appreciate it. I'll get there in the end and you will be my personal accountant when the time comes.” 

Max faked a gasp. “I knew you only wanted me for my career.” 

“Nonsense, your body was a big factor too.” Charles grinned, leaning forward to give him a soft kiss, Max’s hands making their way through the younger’s coat and resting on his hips. Their noses bumped, still working on the perfect rhythm between them. Charles could be lost in a kiss with him forever with Max’s slightly chapped lips moving in tune with his own, it makes him wonder why they hadn’t been doing this from the moment they met.  The Monegasque grinned into the kiss before pulling away, not wanting to get too excited so early in their day. “Now, can we continue with the schedule before I miss my breakfast?” 

Max slightly pushed him on his chest, a grip on Charles’ wrist so he didn’t end up moving too far away. “All you can think about is food while you’re getting me worked up!” 

A snicker left his body and he gave Max’s cheek a peck. The Dutch dropped the grip on his worst and Charles skipped ahead again and left Max rushing after him. He truly adored him. Charles really wished that he could turn back time and just admit his feelings so much sooner, to turn back time and tell his 13 year old self that it wasn’t hatred he felt but instead a big fat crush. But he was grateful for how it turned out now, both older to understand their feelings and old enough to properly pursue them with comfort. His family knew he was seeing Max, the same as Max’s mother and sister knew too, having remembered the other back from their karting days. 

He’d been thinking of officially asking Max to be his boyfriend soon. They pretty much were already, just without the label. Charles practically lived at Max’s house, keeping clothes and toiletries there since Max’s cats couldn’t be left alone too long. They went on dates regularly, their friends recognised them as a couple and he heard Max refer to him as his boyfriend on the phone to a friend from work. 

… Okay yes, they were practically together. But it didn’t hurt to ask!

Charles was busy brainstorming all the different ways he could ask Max to be his boyfriend. His most ideal plan was at the Christmas party, he’d purposely chosen the venue they were viewing that day as it had a balcony overlooking a gorgeous flower garden. Charles thought it would be perfect, while everyone else is inside the venue downing shot after shot, he could ask Max to officially be his boyfriend under the dark of the night, overlooking a beautiful creation. 

While Charles was stuck in his head, he hadn’t heard Max shouting his name as he started walking across the street while the traffic lights were still green. If he had been paying any attention, he would’ve heard his lover and noticed how the older boy tried to grab him before he could cross the road. Suddenly, he heard a loud beep of a car. His head turned to the side in time to see a car come speeding towards him. His body stopped, frozen in the middle of the road as the cars in the other lane flew past him. Eyes locked onto the car, he accepted his fate at that moment. 

Charles hadn’t believed Lando when he said your life flashed before your eyes when faced with death, that the universe was kind enough to let you relive your best memories before you were taken away. He brushed it off as the young boy being dramatic, but here he was replaying the happiest moments of his life before the car could come barrelling towards him.

His mind reeled back to fond moments with his family, his childhood memories with his father and Jules. Growing up with his brothers and seeing them flourish in life. Exploring London for the first time as he took in the new life he had. Meeting George, Alex, Oscar and Lando. Graduating with his degree. Becoming the team manager at his job.

His first meeting with Max.

His confession to Max. 

His first kiss with Max. 

His first time with Max. 

No. This wasn’t his time.

Charles’ body was suddenly yanked from the street, his eyes still trained on the car and watching it fly past where he had been standing. The wind swept violently through his hair and his eyes were forced close from the sting of the breeze. Before he could completely register what happened, his feet landed safely on the other side of the road, away from the growing traffic. 

He hadn’t realised there was someone holding him until the hands were removed. Charles’ hands rested on his knees as he bent over, taking in the ground and the safety of the pavement. He knew there was only one person in the city who could’ve saved him from that. 

“Spiderman?” He allowed himself to stand up straight, panting as he tried to stabilise his breath. Charles’ turned to look behind him, a crowd gathering as they looked in awe at the superhero. Blinding red and blue covered his eyesight as he viewed Spiderman before him, it was his second time seeing the hero up close and yet it did nothing to hinder his reaction. 

He could see the array of phones, he scowled to himself, mentally noting to check Twitter later and see if anyone had a bad angle of him. He felt like throwing himself at the hero or collapsing to the floor and worshipping the very ground he walked on. “Thank you, from the bottom of my heart. I could’ve died if you hadn’t shown up then. You once saved my friend and over the years I’ve brushed off why he spoke so highly of you, but you have honestly done something no one else here could ever do. You are truly a hero to us all here.”  

Spiderman chuckled before addressing the Monegasque, coughing deeply. “It’s what I’m here for. As long as I’m around, I’ll be here to save the people of London from walking into oncoming traffic.” He teased lightly. “It’s okay though, you’re alright. Just make sure you look both ways next time before crossing, mate.” 

He truly did have a charm to him but… there was something familiar to him. No, it wasn’t from the day he saved Lando. Looking back now, he hadn’t really had the chance to notice it while worrying over Lando and the two of them disappearing into the night, but he could feel like he recognised him then. His voice seemed eerily similar and set off alarm bells in Charles’ head. Charles looked over the body, the stance, the voice, the accent, it was all coming together now. 

“Oh my god…” Charles whispered out loud, his eyes widened in disbelief as the gears started turning properly in his head. How did he never see it before? How did he never realise? How did Lando never realise? He was about to confront Spiderman before realising they were still surrounded by a very large crowd. Instead, he walked up to the vigilante and hugged him without any warning.

“Oh, wow. You don’t have to do this! This is just in my job description. You were one of the easier ones anyway.” 

Spiderman lightly patted his back in response to the hug. Charles leaned towards the hero’s ear before whispering barely above a breath. “Thank you, Oscar.” 

The man in his arms froze, the hand patting his back suddenly coming to a stop. Charles imagined his blood ran cold and his brain was going hectic. 

“U-uh, what? Who is Oscar?”

“Don’t take me for a fool, Piastri. Does Lando know?” He already knew the answer, he just needed Spidey-Oscar to confirm it. 

“No… no he doesn’t know.” 

“You fucking idiot! You are a stupid man! I cannot believe you!” Charles shouted, drawing the attention of the crowd again as they listened in. 

“Charles-” Spidey-Oscar whispered again, reminding the Monegasque that they are in fact still in public and that revealing his identity to the huge crowd would be less than ideal. 

His eyes glanced over the crowd, running his tongue over his teeth in frustration. The crowd seemed to be trying to draw closer, the gap between them and the civilians decreasing as the seconds passed. Everyone was on the tips of their toes, eager to try and get as close as possible to the masked vigilante. Whisper-arguing it was then.

“Non! Do not ‘Charles’ me right now! What were you thinking? You stupid Australian nut job, you’re fucking Spiderman? Really?” Charles was angry beyond belief. Oscar was so young when Spiderman was first seen, risking his life everyday, multiple times a day, he’d seen all the videos and photos of the younger fighting supervillains and now all he wanted to do was wrap Oscar up in a blanket and keep him away from it all.

He was also angry on behalf of Lando and the rest of their friends. For all the times Oscar had left them, not shown up or hadn’t spoken to them in days, never giving an explanation as to why.

This was a pretty damn good reason though, if Charles was being completely honest with himself.

But he was also grateful, Oscar had given everything to help the city of London. He knew how busy the boy was and he could only imagine the experiences he had donning the mask. Lando had complained a few times that the Aussie was on his runs all the time, when in reality he was saving the lives of many. God knows how people he saved over the years. Hell, Oscar even saved himself and Lando!

Spidey-Oscar moved so they were now facing each other, still whispering hushed tones between them both. Charles could imagine how Oscar looked under the mask in that moment, with fearful eyes and nervous sweat, even his voice had started to wobble a little. “I can explain-”

“Oscar, I don’t know how you’ve managed to keep this a secret for so long, especially from Lando, but it’s unfair to keep him in the dark. You know how heartbroken he will be to know you’ve lied to him so long. You need to tell him.” Charles had put his foot down, his stern tone coming through. Lando had put up with it for so long, the constant disappearing act of Oscar’s and the cancelling of plans, hiding what he was doing. Charles couldn’t help but feel protective over them both . He understood both sides of it, but he couldn’t see hiding this secret going well. 

“To be fair, I haven’t hid it very well, Lando just thinks that’s how I behave.” 

“Exactly my point! The poor boy is convinced it’s just you and hasn’t even begun to think of you holding a secret like this. You need to tell him…” Charles had a thought. Maybe a bad one, it certainly wouldn’t put him in Oscar’s good books, but it would definitely speed things along. “…or I will.” 

“What?”  

“You heard me.” 

“You can’t tell him, Charles! I’m keeping him safe-”

“Charles!”

The Monegasque quickly let go of Spidey-Oscar, the hero also stepped away as he realised who it was. “Max!” Charles shouted back with glee, relieved to see the face of his lover. Max came running through the crowd, pushing through everyone gathering. The Dutchman nearly crashed into him, wrapping his arms tightly around him, disregarding the fact Spiderman was standing right there. 

“Schatje, I thought I had lost you. There… there was nothing I could do, part of me nearly went to push you out of the way but I just couldn’t do anything when I saw you there.” Max rushed out, Charles could hear the panic still evident in his voice, despite seeing him safe. The Dutchman rested his forehead against his own, his hand resting on Charles’ cheek. He held the boy with such care, as though he would break if Max were to let him go. He held Charles like he couldn’t bear to have him leave his touch. In the calming presence of his kinda-boyfriend, his nerves had calmed and he could feel his body vibrating with energy, a rush soaring through his veins. 

“Max, I'm okay. Feel me, feel my heart, I am here.”  Charles took Max’s hand, lacing them together from the back and placing the palm of the older boy over his heart. He recognised that his heartbeat felt like it was about to break through his chest, but feeling it could maybe help ground Max a little bit. “We’re here, in London.” 

After a few moments of breathing with each other and allowing themselves to calm, Max lifted his forehead to address Spidey-Oscar who was shuffling his feet awkwardly to the side of them. His one hand was still holding Charles’s face and caressing it softly while the other was pressed against his chest. Many of the crowd had dispersed by now, probably bored seeing the couple standing there in silence and Spiderman doing nothing interesting. “You have no idea what you have done for me today saving him. Thank you, mate. What you do is beyond incredible, we’re lucky to have you.” 

“All in a day's work, mate. Just-ah… your friendly neighbourhood Spiderman.” Spidey-Oscar awkwardly giggled, averting his gaze to everywhere but the couple. 

Charles eyed Max for a few seconds, waiting for any sign of recognition to appear on his face. Nothing came, in fact he seemed a little in love with the hero (wait till he one day finds out who it really is. Actually, Max adores Oscar, he might be even more thrilled with that). But there was nothing to show that he knew who the vigilante was. 

“Right well, we have to get going, Spiderman. Thank you so much for saving me and I’m sure you will help many more people today. We have to get going to go see our friend, Lando.” Charles had a smile slowly creeping onto his face. Yeah, this was the plan to help speed things up. 

Max furrowed his eyebrows and pouted. “Lando? I thought you wouldn’t want to do anything after what just happened. I could understand still wanting to go to Vettel’s, but why go to see Lando?” Charles really wanted to kiss the pout away and fix all the worry evident in Max’s face. He knew that the boy was worried for him right now, but he had some very important things to do. 

Spidey-Oscar snapped his head to look at them both quickly, finally acknowledging that they were still standing there. “O-oh, well I gotta get going. I’m sure there’s other people who need help so I’m just gonna-yep I’m just gonna- go?” The hero squeaked out, his voice breaking as he pointed his thumbs to the side. Charles almost chuckled at seeing how nervous and eager he was to get away. “See ya, mates!” Charles watched as a web flew out from a gadget on Spidey-Oscar’s wrist, pulling himself away from the street and down the roads of the never dying city. 

Max watched him leave, disappointed at how quickly he rushed off when he arrived. “Are you sure you want to go see Lando? We could go home, I’ll draw us a bubble bath with some candles, I could even give you a massage.” He offered graciously. 

“While that sounds amazing, I need to tell Lando all about Spiderman. Come on, cheri. If we go now, we’ll still make it in time for breakfast.” Charles gently took their intertwined hand and separated it to rejoin them normally. The Monegasque gave Max a cheeky grin, with a wink that moved his whole face. “The bubble bath will be great for later though.” 

*

“You figured it all out from having one conversation with him?” Lando asked with a baffled tone, filling the silence in the room after Charles finished reciting the encounter he had with Spiderman- Oscar. He sat there for a few moments, absorbing the information bestowed upon him. 

Charles had figured it out near instantly while it took Lando every clue you could throw at him to even realise it. He couldn’t help but feel so stupid. It was there right in front of him all along, the last thing Oscar really needed to do was spell it out for him. He lived with the Aussie for the last few years, observed all his behaviours and he still couldn’t get it. Lando just felt so clueless, he wondered if Oscar became fed up with him just not knowing. 

A revelation sparked in Lando’s mind as he remembered the conversation held with Spiderman, well- Oscar on the rooftop, where he confided in the hero about Oscar lying to him. God, he was cringing just thinking about Oscar listening to Lando complain about himself. But he remembered Spiderman had pleaded for him to ‘just ask’, it being Oscar’s own way of encouraging him to question it and he’d get the answers he wanted. 

Except, he finally questioned it and Oscar left him in the pouring rain, wondering what he did wrong in the world to deserve it. 

But he understood it now, he knew why he left, Spiderman’s duties had to come first above all else, people’s lives were in danger. 

Lando felt guilt eating at his stomach as he thought of the poor victims of the bank robbery, the two security guards with life-threatening injuries. Was that because he stopped Oscar? Because he stood there arguing with him when Spiderman was supposed to go and save the day? He was just thankful no one had died, he couldn’t live with himself knowing he prevented the hero from saving the people of the city. 

God, how many times had he prevented Oscar from being Spiderman?

Charles shrugged his shoulders nonchalantly. “I thought it was pretty obvious, I truly don’t know how you didn’t figure it out the first couple of times you met him.”

Lando buried his head in his hands, the torn mask brushing against his cheek as he shivered from the texture of the material. He didn’t even have any excuses, for christ sake, he lived with him for years and never thought for one second he could be Spiderman. If you asked Lando fifteen minutes ago if he ever thought Oscar could be Spiderman, he would’ve laughed in your face and told you to fuck off. Now, he was left reeling at all the moments he’d ever suspected Oscar of having dodgy behaviour. 

“Don’t forget- he wanted to keep you safe, Oscar said it himself. He thought if he didn’t tell you, then you wouldn’t be involved with Spiderman and you’d be safe.” Charles tried to explain, offering him guidance in what felt like a moment of loss. 

“But he let me hang out with him as Spiderman, he met me and saved me so many times.” Lando exclaimed, feeling exhausted as he tried to wrap his head around everything. 

“And what ended up happening once Oscar let his exterior down and hung out with you as Spiderman? You were captured by a supervillain-'' Lando winced and Charles gave him a sympathetic smile, but carried on nonetheless. “-and Spiderman had to save you after you were thrown out of a building, oui? Oscar believed that having you too close to Spiderman- even if you didn’t know it was him- was too dangerous and he needed to step back. All Oscar has ever wanted was to keep you safe, Lando.”

“Why does he never consider how I feel about it? Maybe I’m willing to risk it. I already have been for years, unknowingly.”

“We both know Oscar doesn’t think like that, he saw the first sign of danger and your life truly at risk and he couldn’t hack it. It’s his own messed up way of keeping you safe.”

“You know…” Lando spoke up softly. “I spoke to him as Spiderman and I even complained about Oscar hiding stuff from me then and you know what he said? He told me to just ask him, ask him to tell me the truth and he will.” Lando sighed and rubbed his face with frustration. He was sure he could feel wrinkles forming on his forehead by now. 

“Don't you see, Lando?” Charles perked up with enthusiasm, his voice raising an octave and his eyes lighting up in delight. “He wants you to know, he wants you to ask. Oscar wants to tell you the truth.”

“And he was on the rooftop yesterday… the one place he knew I’d go to look for Spiderman.” Lando said quickly and grabbed the hoodie torn mask, rushing out of Oscar’s room and past the broken door. 

Charles hastily rose from the floor, scrambling to catch up with Lando. He found him already situated in front of the living room window, his gaze fixated on the rooftop of the building opposite them. The cold air breezed through the living room from the slightly ajar window. “Do you think he’ll be there again?” 

The question hung in the air as the tension grew. If he was honest? Lando wasn’t too sure, his theory could’ve been completely wrong and maybe Oscar really did just go there to enjoy the sunset. But why would he bother returning when he knew Lando could turn up at any moment? 

Lando knew what he needed to do. 

The rooftop was empty, the sunset would be upon them soon, glistening the sky with the orange fury. He gazed down at the broken hoodie and mask in his hand. With determination filling him, Lando grinned to himself as he worked up a plan. “There’s only one way to find out.”

*

Across the street, past reception, up the stairs and through the exit to the rooftop, Lando stood there prepared with his backpack slung over his shoulder and a fierce determination. 

The cold winter air bit at his cheeks, bestowing a rosy red tint upon them and his nose. The breeze sent chills down his spine and his curls swept in the air, leaving him wishing he’d wrapped up just a little bit warmer for the occasion. Each heavy exhale left behind a trail of mist.

Nervousness overwhelmed him for a moment like a weighted blanket thrown over him. The sun dipped below the skyline, casting the warm orange glow over the city as the Christmas lights began to twinkle. His stomach churned with unease, each passing second of anticipation sending waves of sickness through his core, his heart beginning to feel like it was bumping out of his chest. Lando’s mind began to reel as he thought of all the possible outcomes of confronting him. Thinking of everything that could go wrong. 

No, absolutely not. Lando wouldn’t let things turn sour again, they weren’t leaving the rooftop until he said everything he needed to. He allowed an unshakable confidence to roll over him, the sickness evaporating as he stood tall and firmly, the sunset casting an ethereal glow over his tanned skin. 

Perched on the rooftop’s edge, Spiderman let his legs sway casually back and forth, his vigilant gaze surveying the city below. The bustling hum of the city reached Lando’s ears, piercing the silence on the rooftop, the sound of roaring engines and distant conversations ruining the peaceful tranquillity. 

Inhaling deeply, Lando released a loud exhale as he mustered the courage to take a step forward. He watched as the muscles under Spiderman’s suit flexed, tensing as his gaze retracted from the street below, looking out to the city skyline. 

He knew Lando was here. 

Taking it as his queue to speak, Lando drew in a deep breath, his chest rising and falling with the beating of his heart. “I finally took your advice, Spiderman. You told me to just ask Oscar what was going on. At first, I couldn’t do it, a part of me didn’t want to know the truth because I was too scared. I was scared that facing the truth would mean the end of whatever was going on between us… and I think he was scared too.”

He watched as Spiderman glanced over his shoulder before rising from the edge of the rooftop, bathing in the radiant glow of the sunset. Lando’s breath caught as he gazed at Spiderman, reminding himself that he knew what lay beneath the mask.

“I didn’t think I’d see you here again, how did-” Spiderman cut himself off for a moment and Lando shook his head at himself for not recognising the voice earlier on. Sure, the voice was concealed and perhaps a little distorted, but underneath, it was still the boy he knew so well and loved. “-how did it all go?”

“To be honest with you, I asked him and he ran away from me.” The white eyes of Spiderman’s eyes shrunk slightly, looking past Lando as if they couldn’t face looking at him. “And I thought you ran away from me too, I thought both Spiderman and Oscar were done with me, yet here you are, exactly where you knew I’d find you.” Lando accused, taking a few long strides forward, only a few metres between them now. 

Spiderman gulped and stepped down from the rooftop’s edge, taking a tentative step forward, his legs feeling like jelly beneath him. “Lando-”

“I’m not done” Lando quickly interrupted, he needed to say his piece before his confidence left him and he was left to pick up the pieces. “You left and I couldn’t find you, but I accepted it because I knew I should stay away, in fact, I promised to stay away. But Oscar? He ran away from me and left me heartbroken in the pouring rain. I gave him the choice to either tell me the truth or I was gone from his life, and he chose to hide behind his lies again. And I was pissed- I was so upset that I told myself I’d never speak to Oscar again. I even went back to our place and was packing my things to stay at my friend’s place. But then- I found something.” Lando spoke clearly. 

Lando dropped the backpack to the floor, unzipping it carefully as he took out the carefully folded material. He brought it out and threw it to the cold concrete floor of the rooftop. Spiderman’s eyes followed the movement, watching the black hoodie sprawl on the floor with the hole evident. The white eyes of the mask widened in an almost comical fashion. 

The two boys stood there opposite each other with the black hoodie holding them apart, the barrier between them as the world moved on. 

Lando began talking again before Spiderman could get another word in. “I found this- and I remembered it from the day you saved Logan and I from Doctor Octopus’ men, I remembered feeling the broken hole and thinking ‘what a shame’ because it was destroyed. Do you know where I found this, Spiderman?” Lando quizzed him with an all-knowing tone. Spiderman just stared at him like a blank slate, his eyes still stuck to the black material situated between them. “I found this in Oscar’s wardrobe, stuffed right away at the back by his shoes, where most people probably wouldn’t have bothered to look. But I’m not like most people, I suppose.”

“Lando, please just let me explain.” Spiderman pleaded. “I’ll tell you everything.”

Lando delved into the backpack once more, retrieving the torn and frayed mask, tossing it atop the fallen black hoodie. The mask settled, landing head to head with Spiderman’s face. Its white eyes peered into the hero’s eyes, staring into his soul, a reflection of the memories the cuts and tears held. 

“Take off the mask, Oscar.” Lando whispered in the space between them, a plea cutting through the thick tension in the air. His heart ached, desperation clawing at him. “I want to see you.”

Spiderman’s trembling hands raised to his neck, stopping with a hesitant pause as they reached the edge of the mask. Lando’s gaze followed the gloved fingers, watching as the fingertips toyed with the fabric. Spiderman’s fingertips ventured beneath the snug material of the mask, gradually drawing it over his face in what seemed like slow motion. 

Lando held his breath as the mask was removed, watching as the brown waves of Oscar’s hair bounced over his forehead, flat in volume from being compressed under the mask. Oscar’s eyes glistened with moisture, tinted red as they held a look of regret and sorrow. The two remained motionless, stuck there in time as Oscar laid himself bare. 

“Lando, I wanted to tell you everything, I swear. But- you asked at the wrong time, I needed to go and I’m so sorry, but I had to go save them.” Oscar pleaded, his eyes watering more as he desperately flailed his hands. “You deserved to know it from the beginning, I thought I could keep you away from it all and you’d be safe, but you’d never stay away. I should’ve known you’d get a little superhero obsession.” A weak chuckle filled the air. 

“I know.” Lando offered him a tender smile. “I was angry- and rightfully so. And we’ve got a lot more to talk about, but I understand why you hid it and why you couldn’t tell me at the time.” Lando elaborated, his voice carrying an understanding tone. 

“We can talk about it now, I can explain everything to you.” 

Lando briskly shook his head. “I don’t want to talk about it right now. I’ve waited a few years, I can wait a little longer, it won’t hurt. I think there’s a conversation that we need to continue, however, from before you left the party, before we were called up to sing a goddamn karaoke song.” 

“Oscar, despite all the times you’ve kept this from me- and yes, we’ll definitely have that talk eventually- you are genuinely my everything. I can’t imagine my life right now without you and I’m not sure how I even survived through life before meeting you. You’ve made me a better person and everyday you inspire me to be an even better person again. Waking up, knowing you’re in my life or even in my arms just gives me such a purpose in life. Every day I wake up just to see you smile, every joke I tell is just to hear your laugh. Everything I do is for you.
